Domain confirmed available (`itestified.app`, admin controls DNS) but the Slice 1 page itself is not yet deployed to that subdomain (still reachable at the dashboard's own domain, `itestifieddashboard.ifnotgod.com/share/<id>`, which is also what Slice 2's mobile share links and Slice 3's Android App Links intent-filter/`assetlinks.json` point at) -- domain wiring is an infra/DNS step, not code, and can happen independently whenever it's convenient; if/when that migration happens, Slice 2's `testimonyShareBaseUrl` constant and Slice 3's intent-filter host + `assetlinks.json` all need updating together.

### Phase 12: Scripture Of The Day Notifications

Build:
- notify every active, non-admin user (in-app + push, respecting each user's own `allow_push_notifications` preference) the moment a Scripture of the Day actually goes live
- cover both ways a scripture can go live: an admin creates or edits an entry dated today (immediate publish, Phase 7's `perform_create`/`perform_update`), and the daily `publish_due_scriptures` cron catching a scheduled entry up
- reuse the existing Phase 6 push pipeline (`send_push_to_users`) rather than building a second one

Sub-slices:

- **Slice 1 — Notify on immediate publish** — Goal: Every user finds out the moment a new scripture goes live today. an admin creates a scripture dated today, or edits an existing entry's date to today (or earlier); every active, non-admin user gets a notification the moment the save request completes
  - Implemented 2026-07-28. New `NotificationType.SCRIPTURE_PUBLISHED` choice (migration `0010_alter_usernotification_notification_type`) and `apps.notifications.services.notify_all_users_of_scripture_published(*, scripture, actor=None)`, mirroring `notify_new_video_testimony_published`'s recipient scoping: `User.objects.filter(account_status=ACTIVE)`, excluding active admins and (when given) the acting admin themselves, since they already see the change in the dashboard. Wired into `AdminScriptureListCreateView.perform_create` and `AdminScriptureDetailView.perform_update` (`apps/content/api/views.py`) right after the existing `refresh_status_for_today()` status-flip save — only fires when that flip actually lands on `PUBLISHED`, so an edit that doesn't touch publish status (e.g. changing the prayer text on a still-scheduled entry) never re-notifies anyone.
- **Slice 2 — Notify on scheduled (cron) publish** — Goal: Users are notified for a scripture published automatically, exactly as if an admin had just done it. a scripture scheduled for a past date that was never manually touched gets published by the daily `publish_due_scriptures` management command; every active, non-admin user gets notified at that point, exactly as if an admin had just published it
  - Implemented 2026-07-28. `apps.content.services.commands.publish_due_scheduled_scriptures()` now captures the IDs of rows about to flip *before* the bulk `.update()` (bulk updates don't give per-row hooks), then calls `notify_all_users_of_scripture_published(scripture=entry)` once per newly-published entry afterward (`actor=None` — nothing to exclude on the cron path, but active admins are still excluded). Normally this is exactly one entry (today's), but a missed cron run catching up several days at once now correctly sends one distinct notification per entry rather than one generic message.
- **Slice 3 — Recognizable in the notification centre** — Goal: A scripture notification is instantly recognizable by its own icon. a scripture-published notification renders with its own icon in the mobile notification list rather than the generic fallback
  - Implemented 2026-07-28. Added `NotificationType.scripture` to the mobile enum and `'scripture_published' → NotificationType.scripture` in `NotificationItem.typeFromApi`, plus a dedicated purple/book icon case in `_NotificationAvatar` (`notifications_sections.dart`), matching the treatment already given to `app_update_available`. Unmapped/future types still fall back to the existing generic icon, so this was additive only.

Test:
- backend: `NotifyAllUsersOfScripturePublishedTests` (excludes admins including the acting admin, excludes inactive users, notifies eligible members, pushes) plus a push-wiring assertion alongside the other `notify_*` functions in `apps/notifications/tests/test_push_notifications.py`
- backend: extended the existing Phase 7 immediate-publish regression tests (`apps/content/tests/test_api.py`) to assert the notification is actually created, added a same-status-edit no-op test and a dedicated `publish_due_scheduled_scriptures` command test
- mobile: `typeFromApi` mapping test for `scripture_published` and the unrecognized-type fallback
- full backend suite re-run clean: 254 tests, same pre-existing 21 `apps.authn` failures/errors (live Brevo calls, unrelated), zero new regressions; `manage.py check` and `makemigrations --check` clean

Status: Completed. All 3 slices implemented 2026-07-28 — see each slice's notes above. Not yet covered: a notification when a scripture is scheduled (not published) or when a picture/testimony curation change goes live — out of scope for this phase, which was scoped specifically to the "no notification on scripture upload" gap reported live.

### Phase 13: Profile Support & Community Content

Background: auditing the mobile Profile screen (triggered by the Help-screen contact-info bug in Phase 7's post-completion review) found two more items that looked real but weren't: "Follow @iTestified" opens a stub screen whose body literally reads "Follow and community touchpoints are surfaced here" (no real links, ever), and "Rate iTestified" is a fully local star-rating + review form — tapping Submit only flips local widget state to show a fake "Rating Submitted!" message; nothing is saved or sent anywhere. About Us/Terms/Privacy and the Help FAQ were also audited at the time and found to be real, intentionally-static content (not broken) — but the admin's standing preference is to control profile-facing content from the dashboard generally, not just fix what's outright broken, so Slices 4-7 extend this phase to make that static content dashboard-editable too rather than hardcoded in the app.

Build:
- admin-configurable social/community links (platform, URL, active flag, display order), managed from the dashboard rather than hardcoded in the app, per the admin's explicit preference to control profile features from the dashboard
- a public, unauthenticated read endpoint so the mobile Follow screen renders whatever the admin has actually turned on
- replace the fake local "Rate iTestified" submission with a real deep link to the platform's actual store listing (reusing Phase 10's `androidStoreUrl`/`iosStoreUrl`), so a rating a user leaves actually counts publicly — deliberately not building a second, competing "review" concept here since Phase 8 already scopes a real submitted-review domain (not started) as a separate, bigger effort
- make About Us, Terms of Use, Privacy Policy, the Help FAQ list, and the Help screen's support email/phone all dashboard-editable, so any of this copy can be corrected or updated without shipping a new app build (this is exactly the class of bug the Help-screen contact-info fix was — text baked into the Dart source that can only be fixed by a store release)

Sub-slices:

- **Slice 1 — Admin manages social/follow links from the dashboard** — Goal: Admin controls which social platforms the app promotes, without a code change. admin opens a settings screen, sets a URL per platform (Instagram, Facebook, X, TikTok, YouTube, WhatsApp), and toggles each on/off; only platforms that are both on and have a real URL appear to users
  - Implemented 2026-07-28. New `apps.social_links` app, mirroring Phase 10's `apps.app_versions` structure: `SocialLink` (`platform` unique per a fixed `SocialPlatform` enum, `url`, `is_active` default `True`, `display_order`, `updated_by`). `GET /api/v1/social-links/admin/` always returns a row for every known platform (blank/inactive if never configured) so the dashboard can render the full list from the first load; `PUT /api/v1/social-links/admin/<platform>/` upserts one platform, rejecting an unknown platform (400). `IsAuthenticated, IsActiveAdmin` — lower stakes than app-version gating, so no `IsSuperAdmin` requirement. Public `GET /api/v1/social-links/` (no auth, matching the `mobile_*` convention) returns only rows that are `is_active=True` and have a non-blank `url`, ordered by `display_order`. Dashboard: new "Follow links" settings screen (`/social-links`, linked from the settings nav group alongside "App version") mirroring `app-version-page.tsx`'s layout — one card per platform with a URL input and a "Show in app" checkbox; `display_order` is set implicitly from the fixed platform list order on save rather than exposed as its own input, keeping the form simple since reordering platforms isn't a real need yet.
- **Slice 2 — See real follow links on mobile** — Goal: A user only ever sees social links that are actually configured and live. user opens "Follow @iTestified" from Profile and sees the platforms the admin has actually configured, each opening the real link externally; the screen shows nothing if the admin hasn't configured any yet, rather than a fake filler message
  - Implemented 2026-07-28. Replaced the `ProfileRouteKeys.follow` case's `ProfileSimpleInfoScreen` placeholder with a real `FollowScreen` that fetches `GET /social-links/`, renders one row per platform with an icon + external-link affordance, and opens the URL via `url_launcher` (`canLaunchUrl`/`launchUrl(mode: externalApplication)`, same pattern already established in `update_required_screen.dart`). An empty result renders a plain "No social links yet" state instead of any placeholder copy; a genuine fetch failure surfaces as a real error state with retry, not a silent blank screen.
- **Slice 3 — Rate iTestified opens the real store listing** — Goal: A user can actually leave a public app store rating, not a dead-end form. user opens "Rate iTestified" and is sent straight to the app's actual Play Store/App Store listing to leave a public rating, instead of submitting into a form that goes nowhere
  - Implemented 2026-07-28. Rebuilt `RatingsScreen`: dropped the fake in-app star/review widget and fake "Rating Submitted!" success state entirely, replaced with a single explanation + "Rate us on the Store" button that opens `androidStoreUrl`/`iosStoreUrl` (from Phase 10's `store_links.dart`) via the same `url_launcher` pattern as Slice 2 and `update_required_screen.dart`. iOS still points at the App Store search-results fallback until the app has a live listing (same pre-existing TODO from Phase 10, not new to this slice).
- **Slice 4 — Admin edits About Us / Terms of Use / Privacy Policy from the dashboard** — Goal: Admin can update legal and about-us copy without shipping a new app version. admin opens a settings screen with one text area per document, edits the copy, and saves; mobile then shows the updated text without an app release
  - Implemented 2026-07-28 (backend + dashboard; mobile consumption is Slice 7). New `apps.profile_content` app: `ProfileContentBlock` (`key` unique per a fixed `ProfileContentKey` enum — `about_us`, `terms_of_use`, `privacy_policy` — plus `body` text, `updated_by`), mirroring the `apps.social_links`/`apps.app_versions` fixed-key-set pattern rather than an arbitrary admin-creatable-document model. A data migration (`0002_seed_initial_content`) seeds all three keys with the *exact* copy that was previously hardcoded in `about_policy_screen.dart`, via `get_or_create` so it never overwrites an admin edit on a future re-run. `GET /api/v1/profile-content/admin/blocks/` always returns all three keys; `PUT /api/v1/profile-content/admin/blocks/<key>/` upserts one, rejecting an unknown key (400). `IsAuthenticated, IsActiveAdmin`. Public `GET /api/v1/profile-content/blocks/` (no auth) always returns all three keys (blank string, never missing, for a key somehow cleared). Dashboard: new "About & policies" settings screen (`/profile-content`, settings nav alongside "Follow links") — one text area per document, same save-flow shape as "Follow links." 8 backend tests, 3 dashboard tests.
- **Slice 5 — Admin sets the support email and phone number from the dashboard** — Goal: Admin can correct support contact details without a store release. admin edits the two fields shown in Help's "Contact our support" section; today these are real values but still hardcoded in the Flutter source (fixed there after the Phase 7 contact-info bug), so correcting them again would require another store release
  - Implemented 2026-07-28 (backend + dashboard; mobile consumption is still Slice 7). Reused Slice 4's `ProfileContentBlock` model rather than a new table for two fields: two more `ProfileContentKey` values, `support_email` and `support_phone` (migration `0003_alter_profilecontentblock_key`), seeded via `0004_seed_support_contact` with the real values already live in the app (`ifnotgodtech@gmail.com`, `+2348061464092`) so nothing regresses. Because the admin list/update/public views already iterate generically over `ProfileContentKey.choices`, no view code changed to support the new keys — only the serializer gained key-aware validation: `validate_body` reads the target key from serializer `context` (passed in by the view, since `key` itself is read-only and never in the request body) and applies `django.core.validators.validate_email` for `support_email` or a permissive digits/`+`/spaces/dashes/parens pattern for `support_phone`, while leaving About/Terms/Privacy's freeform text unvalidated. Dashboard: same "About & policies" page, split into a "Documents" section (text areas, unchanged) and a new "Help screen support contact" section (single-line `type="email"`/`type="tel"` inputs).
- **Slice 6 — Admin manages the Help FAQ list from the dashboard** — Goal: Admin can keep the in-app FAQ current without a code change. admin adds, edits, reorders, and removes FAQ question/answer pairs; only active ones show in the app
  - Implemented 2026-07-28 (backend + dashboard; mobile consumption is still Slice 7). New `HelpFaqEntry` model (`question`, `answer`, `display_order`, `is_active`, `updated_by`) — a dynamic list, not a fixed key set, so this got full CRUD (`generics.ListCreateAPIView` + `RetrieveUpdateDestroyAPIView`) rather than Slice 4/5's per-key upsert, closer in shape to the existing inspirational-picture-category admin management than to "Follow links." Seeded via `0006_seed_help_faqs` with the 4 existing hardcoded FAQ items from `help_screen.dart`, in their original order (guarded by `if HelpFaqEntry.objects.exists(): return` rather than `get_or_create` per row, since question text has no unique constraint to key off). A new entry is appended at `HelpFaqEntry.objects.count()` rather than requiring the admin to submit a position; reordering is a separate `PUT /admin/faqs/reorder/` taking `ordered_ids` — validated to be a permutation of every existing entry's ID (rejects a partial/incomplete list, matching the "replace-all" discipline used elsewhere in this codebase, e.g. Phase 7's home curation). Public `GET /profile-content/faqs/` returns only `is_active=True` entries (question/answer only). Dashboard: new "Help FAQ" screen (`/help-faqs`) — unlike Slices 1/4/5's simple form-POST-then-redirect pages, this is a `"use client"` component (mirroring the existing inspirational-picture-category management modal's fetch-and-local-state pattern) since add/edit/remove/reorder all need to feel immediate: add form, per-row Edit/Hide-Show/Remove, and ↑/↓ reorder buttons that optimistically swap two rows locally and PUT the full new order, reverting on failure.
- **Slice 7 — Mobile renders About/Terms/Privacy, Help FAQ, and support contact from the backend** — Goal: Every legal/help/contact screen on mobile reflects the admin's latest edits automatically. `AboutPolicyScreen` and `HelpScreen` (both the FAQ list and the contact section) fetch this content instead of using the hardcoded strings/list currently baked into the Dart source
  - Implemented 2026-07-28. Two shared providers (`lib/features/profile/presentation/state/profile_content_controller.dart`): `profileContentBlocksProvider` (`GET /profile-content/blocks/`, one fetch backs all three About/Terms/Privacy screens plus Help's contact section — all five keys come back from the same backend endpoint) and `helpFaqsProvider` (`GET /profile-content/faqs/`). `AboutPolicyScreen` converted to `ConsumerWidget`: renders the fetched body split on `\n\n` into paragraphs (same visual treatment Terms/Privacy already used before this slice — About Us's old heading/bullet-list structure is intentionally gone now that it's a single admin-edited text field, a tradeoff accepted back in Slice 4). `HelpScreen` converted to `ConsumerStatefulWidget` (keeps its local FAQ-expansion state, now sourced from the fetched list instead of a hardcoded const list); its two backend fetches (FAQ list, contact blocks) are independent `.when()` blocks so one failing doesn't block the other from rendering. Both screens show a real loading spinner and an error state with retry (`ref.invalidate`) rather than silently falling back to stale hardcoded copy.

Test:
- backend (`apps.social_links`, 11 tests): admin permission matrix (auth required, non-admin denied), unknown-platform rejection, upsert create/overwrite/deactivate-without-clearing-url, malformed-URL rejection, public endpoint excludes inactive/blank rows and respects `display_order`, returns empty list when nothing configured
- dashboard (3 tests): renders every platform with empty defaults, pre-fills a configured platform's URL/active state, renders success/error/validation states
- mobile (6 tests): `socialLinksProvider` maps the result array, returns empty list, surfaces a real fetch failure as `AsyncError`; `FollowScreen` renders configured links and the empty state (asserting the old fake filler text is gone); `RatingsScreen` exposes the real store-review button and asserts none of the old fake star/review/"Rating Submitted!" UI survived
- backend (`apps.profile_content`, 25 tests total): admin permission matrix, unknown-key rejection, upsert create/overwrite, public endpoint always returns every key (blank rather than missing for an unset one), email/phone format accepted/rejected per key, a regression guard that key-specific validation doesn't leak onto About Us/Terms/Privacy, FAQ CRUD (create appends at the end, blank question/answer rejected, edit, deactivate-without-deleting, real delete), reorder (accepts a full permutation, rejects an incomplete list), and the public FAQ endpoint returning only active entries in order
- dashboard (9 tests total): renders every key with empty defaults, pre-fills a configured key's body, renders success/error states, support email renders as a real `type="email"` input rather than a text area, Help FAQ page renders/adds/hides/removes entries and submits the correctly-swapped order on a reorder click
- mobile (8 new tests): `profileContentBlocksProvider`/`helpFaqsProvider` map results and surface real fetch failures as `AsyncError`; `AboutPolicyScreen` renders the fetched body split into paragraphs under the correct section key; `HelpScreen` renders the fetched support email/phone and FAQ entries, with regression assertions that none of the old hardcoded copy survives

Status: All 7 slices completed 2026-07-28 — see each slice's notes above. Backend and dashboard suites re-run clean at Slice 6 (backend 290 tests, dashboard 184 tests, same pre-existing 21 `apps.authn` failures/errors, `manage.py check`/`makemigrations --check`/`tsc --noEmit` all clean); mobile's new Slice 7 test files and `flutter analyze` (whole project) pass. Phase 13 is now feature-complete end to end: every admin-editable screen built in Slices 1-6 actually reaches the app.

Post-completion refinement (2026-07-28): `mobile/AGENTS.md` requires Hive-backed caching of API responses for frequently-accessed data; Slice 7's two providers had shipped as plain `FutureProvider`s (in-memory only, re-fetching from empty on every cold start) — consistent with every other content-fetching provider already in the app (`socialLinksProvider`, `inspirationalPicturesProvider`, `homeContentProvider` are all the same shape), but not what AGENTS.md actually calls for, and About Us/Terms/Privacy/FAQ is about the best-fit candidate for it given how static this content is. Converted both to `AsyncNotifier`s with a cache-then-network shape (mirrors `ProfileAccountController`'s existing cached-session-then-refresh pattern): a cached value, if present, is returned immediately via `CacheService`/Hive so the screen paints without waiting on the network, then a background refresh (scheduled via `Future.microtask`, matching `ProfileAccountController`'s exact technique for avoiding a "modified provider during build" error) updates both the cache and the UI once the real response lands; a background refresh failure silently keeps the stale cached value rather than surfacing an error over content the user can already see. A true cold start with no cache yet still awaits the network directly, so the existing loading-spinner/error-with-retry UI in both screens is unchanged. Deliberately scoped to just these two providers rather than retrofitting the other content providers in the same pass — they're a separate, pre-existing gap not introduced by this phase, better addressed as its own deliberate effort using this as the reference pattern. 5 new mobile tests using a real Hive box (`hive_flutter`, matching `cache_service_test.dart`'s setup) prove the cache-first read, the background-refresh-updates-state behavior, and the write-through-on-first-fetch behavior — not just the network path the earlier tests already covered. `flutter analyze` (whole project) and the targeted test files pass, and a full `test/features/profile/` directory re-run (33 passing) confirmed the only 2 failures are the same pre-existing live-network-dependent `profile_flow_test.dart` cases documented elsewhere in this plan, not a regression.

Post-completion fix, 2026-07-29 (mobile-only, found live: an admin-added Instagram link rendered correctly on the Follow screen, but tapping it did nothing): root cause was `android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml`'s `<queries>` block only declaring visibility for `PROCESS_TEXT` intents, not for viewing `http(s)` links — on Android 11+ (API 30+), package-visibility restrictions make `canLaunchUrl()` silently return `false` for any external URL without an explicit `<queries>` declaration, even with a browser installed. This affected every `url_launcher` call site in the app, not just Follow links: the App/Play Store update-required screen, the update-available banner, and Slice 3's "Rate iTestified" store button were all equally broken, all failing silently with zero user-facing feedback. Added `VIEW` intent queries for both `https` and `http` schemes. Also hardened all four call sites (previously identical silent-no-op-on-failure code, copy-pasted from `update_required_screen.dart`'s original pattern): each now shows a SnackBar ("Couldn't open this link" / "Couldn't open the store listing") when `canLaunchUrl`/`launchUrl` genuinely fails, so a future failure (malformed URL, no browser at all) is visible instead of looking like a dead tap. New test in `follow_screen_test.dart` fakes `UrlLauncherPlatform.instance` (via `plugin_platform_interface`/`url_launcher_platform_interface`, added as dev dependencies) to force `canLaunch` to fail and asserts the error message renders — the manifest requirement itself can't be exercised from a widget test since it's a real OS restriction outside Flutter's test sandbox. `flutter analyze` (whole project) clean.

### Phase 14: Self-Service Account Security (Change Password & Delete Account)

Background: auditing the Profile screen found "Change my password" and "Delete Account" are both still the generic `ProfileSimpleInfoScreen` placeholder ("Password change is routed through account/security flows." / "Account deletion requires confirmation and backend action in a later milestone."), confirmed live 2026-07-29. Checked the backend too: `apps.authn` only has the logged-out forgot-password OTP flow (`PasswordResetRequestView`/`Verify`/`Complete`) and the admin-side `AdminChangeTemporaryPasswordView` (forced first-login change) — there is no authenticated "I know my current password, let me set a new one" endpoint. `apps.users` only has `AdminUserDeactivateView`/`AdminUserReactivateView` (an admin acting on someone else) — there is no self-service "delete my own account" endpoint at all. Both are real gaps, not just missing mobile UI.

Build:
- mobile UI for both screens first, as real, polished, but explicitly not-yet-wired mock screens — mirrors the existing "Apple sign-in is coming soon" pattern already used in `create_account_flow.dart` (`AuthSocialButtonsRow.onAppleTap`) for exactly this "UI is real, backend isn't built yet" situation, rather than a generic placeholder screen
- backend: authenticated change-password endpoint (current password verification + new password validation) — not started
- backend: self-service account deletion endpoint — needs a product decision first on soft-delete vs hard-delete and what happens to a deleted user's existing testimonies/donations/comments (cascade-delete vs anonymize-and-keep) — not started
- wire the mock screens to the real endpoints once built, replacing the "coming soon" feedback with the real flow

Sub-slices:

- **Slice 1 — Change Password screen (mock)** — Goal: A user sees a real change-password form, even though it doesn't work yet. user opens "Change my password" from Profile and sees a real form (current password, new password, confirm new password) with client-side validation (fields required, new/confirm must match, a minimum length); submitting shows a "Coming soon" message rather than doing nothing or pretending to succeed
  - Implemented 2026-07-29. New `ChangePasswordScreen`, replacing the generic placeholder. Three obscured-with-visibility-toggle fields (styled inline, matching `AuthTextField`'s look without importing across the `profile`/`auth` feature boundary per `mobile/AGENTS.md`'s "features depend only on core/" rule). Validates in order: all fields required, new password at least 8 characters (matching `AuthValidators.validatePassword`'s existing rule, kept local to this screen for the same feature-independence reason), new/confirm must match. A valid submission shows a "Changing your password is coming soon." SnackBar. 4 new widget tests cover each validation rejection plus the success path.
- **Slice 2 — Delete Account screen (mock)** — Goal: A user sees a real, deliberately-frictioned delete-account flow, even though it doesn't work yet. user opens "Delete Account" from Profile, must pick a reason for leaving (a fixed list plus "Other" with a required detail field) and check an explicit "I understand..." box before the destructive button is reachable; confirming shows a "Coming soon" message rather than doing nothing or pretending to succeed
  - Implemented 2026-07-29, reason requirement added same day per the admin's explicit request ("deleting an account should be with a reason"). New `DeleteAccountScreen`, replacing the generic placeholder. Real consequences copy (profile, testimonies, comments, favorites, giving history), a required reason selection ("I don't use the app anymore" / "I have privacy concerns" / "I'm creating a new account" / "Other", radio-tile styled to match `display_page_screen.dart`'s existing pattern) with a required free-text field when "Other" is picked, plus the "I understand..." checkbox gating the destructive button (disabled until checked). Submitting without a reason, or with "Other" and no detail text, shows an inline error instead of proceeding. A fully valid submission shows an "Account deletion is coming soon." SnackBar. 5 new widget tests cover the disabled-until-confirmed button, the missing-reason rejection, the missing-"Other"-detail rejection, and both success paths (fixed reason, and "Other" with detail text).
- **Slice 3 — Backend: change password while authenticated** — Goal: A logged-in user can actually change their password, securely. user submits their current password and a new one; the backend verifies the current password is correct before accepting the change, and rejects a new password that fails Django's configured password validators
  - Implemented 2026-07-29. `POST /api/v1/auth/mobile/change-password/` (`MobileChangePasswordView`, `apps/authn/api/views.py`), authenticated via the same defaults `CurrentProfileView` already relies on (`IsAuthenticated` + `SessionAuthentication`/`TokenAuthentication`, no override needed). New `change_mobile_user_password` service command (`apps/authn/services/commands.py`) mirrors `change_temporary_admin_password`'s existing shape exactly (`check_password` against the current password, `validate_user_password` — Django's real configured `AUTH_PASSWORD_VALIDATORS` — against the new one), but for a Token-authenticated mobile user instead of a Session-authenticated admin: rather than `update_session_auth_hash` (which only matters for sessions), it rotates the DRF auth token — deletes the old one and issues a fresh one in the same response — matching `complete_password_reset`'s existing token-invalidation intent (a leaked/stolen token from before the change must stop working) without forcing the user through a fresh login, since this path is already authenticated. New `ChangePasswordSerializer` (current/new/confirm, mirrors `ChangeTemporaryPasswordSerializer`). 5 new tests in `apps/authn/tests/test_api.py`: requires authentication, rejects an incorrect current password (password unchanged), rejects a mismatched confirmation, rejects a new password that fails validators (e.g. too short), and the full success path proving the old token stops authenticating anything while the new token (returned in the response) works. Full backend suite: 295 tests, same pre-existing 21 `apps.authn` failures/errors (live Brevo calls, unrelated), `makemigrations --check` clean (no schema change). Mobile `ChangePasswordScreen` is not yet wired to this endpoint — that's Slice 5.
- **Slice 4 — Backend: delete my own account** — Goal: A user can actually delete their own account and have it take effect immediately. user confirms account deletion (current password re-entry required, similar in spirit to the change-password slice, so this can't be triggered by someone who grabbed an unlocked phone) and submits their reason for leaving, captured for retention/product analysis; the backend revokes the session/auth token immediately and applies the data-retention decision below
  - Data-retention decision (2026-07-29, discussed with the admin): soft-delete/anonymize, not hard-delete. Two things specifically can't be cleanly hard-deleted here — donation records likely need to be retained for financial/audit purposes regardless of user preference, and testimonies/comments are shared content other users have favorited, replied to, or built notification threads around, so cascade-deleting them on account deletion would break other people's data, not just the deleting user's. Chosen approach mirrors the Reddit/Twitter "deleted user" pattern: block login permanently and scrub personally-identifying fields, but leave testimonies/comments/donations in place with the author anonymized.
  - Implemented 2026-07-29. `POST /api/v1/auth/mobile/delete-account/` (`MobileDeleteAccountView`), same auth defaults as Change Password. New `delete_own_account` service command (`apps/authn/services/commands.py`), wrapped in `transaction.atomic()`: verifies the current password (reusing the same `check_password` pattern as Slice 3), validates `reason` against a new `AccountDeletionReason` enum (`not_using` / `privacy_concerns` / `new_account` / `other`, stable wire codes distinct from the mobile screen's display copy so the copy can change independently later), requires non-blank `details` when `reason == other`, then: records a new `AccountDeletionFeedback` row (`apps/authn/models.py`, `user` kept via `SET_NULL` FK since the row isn't actually removed by this flow); sets `Profile.full_name = "Deleted User"` and clears `phone_number`/`avatar`; scrubs `User.email`/`username` to a deterministic `deleted-user-<id>@deleted.itestified.local` address (both fields are `unique=True`, so this has to be a real, unique replacement, not a blank); sets `account_status = UserAccountStatus.DELETED` (a status this codebase already had reserved and already blocks login via `ensure_active_user` — no changes needed to the login flow itself) and `set_unusable_password()` as defense-in-depth on top of that; deletes the DRF token and invalidates tracked sessions via the existing `_invalidate_user_sessions` helper. Deliberately did **not** touch `Testimony`/`TestimonyComment`/`Donation` rows at all — every existing serializer already reads an author's display name from `Profile.full_name` (confirmed by reading `TestimonyListSerializer.get_author_name`), so scrubbing that one field is what makes a deleted user's past contributions show as "Deleted User" everywhere, with zero other code changes. 6 new tests in `apps/authn/tests/test_api.py`: requires authentication, rejects an incorrect current password (account untouched), rejects an invalid `reason` value, rejects `other` with blank `details`, full success path (status/email/password/profile all scrubbed correctly, feedback row created, old token immediately stops authenticating), and — the key proof of the design — a test that creates a testimony, deletes the author's account, then fetches that testimony through the public detail endpoint and confirms `author_name` now reads "Deleted User". New migration `authn.0003_accountdeletionfeedback` (`makemigrations --check` clean afterward). Full backend suite: 301 tests, same pre-existing 21 `apps.authn` failures/errors (live Brevo calls, unrelated).
- **Slice 5 — Wire the mock screens to the real endpoints** — Goal: The change-password and delete-account screens actually work end to end. replace both "Coming soon" messages with the real request/response flow now that Slices 3-4 both exist
  - Implemented 2026-07-29. `ChangePasswordScreen` now calls `POST /api/v1/auth/mobile/change-password/` via `serviceLocator<ApiClient>()` (no cross-feature import needed — `profile` stays independent of `auth` per `mobile/AGENTS.md`, aside from the pre-existing precedent of reusing `AuthSession` from `auth/domain/entities` for the cached-session shape, same as `ProfileAccountController` already does). On success, the rotated token from the response is applied via `ApiClient.setAuthToken` and written back into the cached `auth_session` Hive entry (keeping the cached email/full name, replacing only the token) so the existing session survives the rotation without a re-login; on failure, the backend's `message`/`error.message` (surfaced by `ApiClient` as `AuthException`) is shown as the existing inline error text. `DeleteAccountScreen` needed one addition beyond wiring: the mock screen never collected a password, but `MobileDeleteAccountView` requires `current_password` (same "can't be triggered by someone who grabbed an unlocked phone" reasoning as Change Password), so a "Confirm your password" field was added. The screen's four display reason strings are mapped by list index to `AccountDeletionReason`'s stable wire codes (`not_using`/`privacy_concerns`/`new_account`/`other`) before submitting to `POST /api/v1/auth/mobile/delete-account/`. On success the screen calls `AppFlowController.signOut()` (the same routine the existing logout confirmation dialog uses), which clears the token/cache and drops the app back to the guest home stage; on failure the backend message is shown inline, same pattern as Change Password. `DeleteAccountScreen` is now a `ConsumerStatefulWidget` (needs `ref` for `signOut()`); its `ListView` also picked up `cacheExtent: 1000` — a real bug, not just a test fix: without it, `SliverList`'s default cache window unmounts far-enough-offstage children (the new password field, the submit button) once the "Other" detail field pushes the form taller, so on a real device with a short viewport a user could scroll to a spot where those widgets rebuild/dispose churn during scrolling; pinning a larger cache extent for this short, static form avoids that. Widget tests rewritten for both screens to mock `ApiClient` via `http.testing.MockClient` (success path, backend-rejected-password path) instead of asserting "coming soon" copy; `DeleteAccountScreen`'s tests additionally wrap in `ProviderScope`/`UncontrolledProviderScope` and assert `appFlowProvider`'s state flips to guest access after a successful deletion. All 12 widget tests across both files pass; `flutter analyze` clean on every touched file.

Test:
- mobile: both screens submit real requests through a mocked `ApiClient` and assert both the success path (token rotation + cache update for Change Password; sign-out for Delete Account) and a backend-rejected-password path shows the server's error message inline; existing client-side validation tests (required fields, password length, mismatched confirmation, missing reason, missing "Other" detail, missing password) still pass unchanged
- backend: current-password verification (accepts correct, rejects incorrect) for both endpoints; new password validator enforcement; deletion actually revokes the session/token; the deletion reason is actually persisted; a deleted user's existing testimonies remain visible with an anonymized author name; deleted accounts can never log in again

Status: Phase 14 complete 2026-07-29 — all 5 slices implemented, see each slice's notes above.

Post-completion fixes (2026-07-29, both found live-testing on device):
- **Delete Account never returned to the guest home after a successful deletion.** `DeleteAccountScreen` is reached via a pushed route (Profile -> `profileOptionDetail` -> here), but `AppFlowController.signOut()` only flips Riverpod state — it has no idea a route is pushed on top, so the rebuilt guest-mode root stayed hidden underneath the still-visible Delete Account screen. Regular logout never hit this because it's called directly from the Profile tab, which isn't pushed on top of anything. Fixed by popping back to the root route (`Navigator.of(context).popUntil((route) => route.isFirst)`) right after `signOut()`. New widget test pushes the screen onto a real navigation stack (the existing tests used it as `home:`, which couldn't have caught this) and asserts the root becomes visible again post-deletion.
- **Change Password now pops back to the previous screen on success**, instead of leaving the user sitting on the form. Discussed whether success should instead force a full re-login: decided against it — the endpoint already re-verifies the current password before accepting the change, and the old token is already invalidated by the rotation, so forcing a fresh login afterward would add friction without proving anything the flow doesn't already guarantee. Uses `Navigator.of(context).maybePop()` (safe no-op if the screen is ever shown with nothing to pop to, unlike `pop()`).
- **A deleted account's push-notification device token was never cleaned up.** Live device log showed `DELETE /notifications/devices/` returning 401 "Invalid token" right after a successful deletion — the mobile client's best-effort deregister call (part of `signOut()`) fires *after* `delete_own_account` has already deleted the DRF token server-side, so it can never succeed for this specific flow. Not just log noise: `send_push_to_users` (`apps/notifications/services.py`) has no `account_status` filter of its own, so a stale `DeviceToken` row left behind would keep receiving pushes meant for a deleted account. Fixed backend-side instead of relying on client ordering: new `deregister_all_devices_for_user(*, user)` in `apps/notifications/services.py`, called from inside `delete_own_account`'s `transaction.atomic()` block (`apps/authn/services/commands.py`) alongside the token/session invalidation — guaranteed cleanup regardless of what the client does. `test_delete_account_succeeds_anonymizes_and_blocks_future_login` extended to assert the user's device tokens are gone after deletion. Full backend suite: same pre-existing 21 `apps.authn` failures/errors (live Brevo calls, unrelated), `apps.notifications` (40 tests) and the rest of `apps.authn` all pass, `makemigrations --check` clean (no schema change).

### Phase 15: Testimony Reactions (Praying for you / Amen / Gives me hope)

Background: engagement review (2026-07-29) found the Home feed, Profile, and testimony detail screens give users no reason to return daily, no reflection of their own activity, and only one generic heart/favorite reaction on a testimony — which treats a hardship-to-breakthrough story the same as any other social post. Concept mockups covering all of it (habit streak, personalized "For You" feed, Profile activity recap, and richer reactions) were reviewed with the admin as a single artifact before any build work; see that review for the full rationale and competitor research (YouVersion, Duolingo, Facebook/LinkedIn reactions, Strava Kudos, GoFundMe). Of the three build-able concepts, **Reactions is going first** — smallest backend lift (one new model, no scheduled jobs), and the fastest way to see real user response before investing in the personalization or streak work.

Decisions locked in during review (2026-07-29):
- **Build order**: Reactions first. Personalization ("For You" feed, follow-a-category) and the Scripture streak are separate future phases, not part of this one.
- **Streak default** (for whenever that phase happens): default-on for every user, not opt-in — matches YouVersion/Duolingo's habit-formation model; accepted the tradeoff that it may feel slightly forward on first launch.
- **Reactions vs. the existing Favorite**: kept as two separate, coexisting actions. Favorite stays exactly as it is today — a private "save this to find later" bookmark, used to drive the existing My Favorites list/feed. Reactions are a new, separate public sentiment signal. They are NOT merged, so a user's bookmark list is never conflated with how they publicly reacted to a testimony.
- **Reaction counts**: visible to everyone viewing the testimony (public social proof, e.g. "128 praying for you"), not author-only.
- **Reaction set**: exactly three, no negative/critical option — 🙏 Praying for you, 🙌 Amen, 💛 Gives me hope. One reaction per user per testimony (switching pills changes it, tapping the active one again removes it), same single-reaction-slot model as Facebook's original 2016 reactions launch, not a multi-select.

Build:
- backend: new reaction model + choices (stable wire codes distinct from display copy, same pattern as `AccountDeletionReason`), a set/toggle endpoint enforcing one reaction per user per testimony, and per-type counts + the requesting user's own reaction exposed on the testimony detail/list responses
- mobile: testimony detail screen's single heart/favorite icon is replaced by three reaction pills with live counts; the existing Favorite/bookmark action is kept but needs a new icon (a heart no longer makes sense once "Gives me hope" 💛 exists as a separate reaction) — likely a bookmark/ribbon icon instead
- guest-gating: reacting requires an account, same pattern already used for Favorite (prompt to sign in, don't silently no-op)

Sub-slices:

- **Slice 1 — User reacts to a testimony (backend)** — Goal: A user can express how a testimony affected them, and switch their mind later. an authenticated user can react to a testimony with exactly one of the three types, can switch to a different one, or remove their reaction entirely; every testimony response (list and detail) includes the count per reaction type, and the detail response additionally includes which reaction (if any) is the requesting user's own
  - Implemented 2026-07-29. Mirrors the existing Favorite feature's shape closely, per the admin's own precedent in this app: a separate `TestimonyReaction` model (`apps/testimonies/models.py`) with a `UniqueConstraint(user, testimony)` -- one reaction per user per testimony, same single-reaction-slot model as Facebook's original 2016 reactions, not a multi-select. Three denormalized counters on `Testimony` itself (`praying_for_you_count`/`amen_count`/`gives_me_hope_count`, same pattern as the existing `comment_count`) so list/detail responses never need a live aggregate query -- kept in sync via `F()` updates in two new service commands, `set_testimony_reaction`/`remove_testimony_reaction` (`apps/testimonies/services/commands.py`), both wrapped in `transaction.atomic()` with `select_for_update()` on the reaction row. `POST /api/v1/testimonies/<id>/react/` sets or switches the reaction (posting the same type again is an idempotent no-op, it does not toggle off -- removal is a separate `DELETE` on the same URL, matching Favorite's POST/DELETE split exactly); both return the fresh `{"reaction_counts": {...}, "my_reaction": ...}` state so the mobile client doesn't need a second round-trip. `reaction_counts` was added to both `TestimonyListSerializer` and `TestimonyDetailSerializer` (free -- just reads the three int fields); `my_reaction` was added to `TestimonyDetailSerializer` only, deliberately excluded from the list serializer to avoid an N+1 query across a testimony list, since the reaction pills only render on the detail screen anyway. `TestimonyReactionInputSerializer` (a `ChoiceField`) validates the wire code, giving a 400 for free on an invalid type. New migration `testimonies.0009_testimony_reactions`. 8 new tests in `apps/testimonies/tests/test_api.py`: set + counter increment, switching moves the count instead of adding to it, repeating the same type doesn't double-count, invalid type rejected (400), unauthenticated rejected (401, `TokenAuthentication`-only view like `FavoriteToggleView`), remove decrements and clears `my_reaction`, and two serializer-exposure tests (detail includes `my_reaction` for the reacting user and `None` for a guest; list includes `reaction_counts` but omits `my_reaction` entirely). Full backend suite: 309 tests, same pre-existing 21 `apps.authn` failures/errors (live Brevo calls, unrelated), `makemigrations --check` clean.
- **Slice 2 — User sets and sees reactions on a testimony (mobile)** — Goal: A user can react to a testimony with one tap and see real counts update live. the testimony detail screen shows the three reaction pills with real counts pulled from the backend; tapping a pill sets/switches the user's reaction (calling `DELETE` instead of `POST` when the tapped pill is already the user's active reaction, to get the "tap again to remove" feel client-side over Slice 1's POST/DELETE split); a guest tapping a reaction is prompted to sign in rather than the tap doing nothing
  - Implemented 2026-07-29. New `ReactionPillsRow`/`_ReactionPill` widgets (`testimony_detail_primitives.dart`) render the three pills (🙏/🙌/💛 + label + count, count omitted when zero), added below the existing Save/Comment/Share row on both `WrittenTestimonyDetailContent` and `VideoTestimonyDetailContent`, plus the "No negative reactions..." caption from the original concept mockup. `Testimony` (`core/content/entities/testimony.dart`) gained `reactionCounts` (`Map<String, int>`, default `{}`) and `myReaction` (nullable `String`), plus a `TestimonyReactionType` class holding the three wire codes (must stay in sync with the backend's `TestimonyReactionType` enum) -- `copyWith` needed a sentinel-object trick (`_unset`) to distinguish "leave myReaction alone" from "explicitly clear it to null", since a plain `?? this.myReaction` can't tell those apart. `TestimonyFeedController` gained `setReaction`/`removeReaction` (`testimony_feed_controller.dart`), mirroring `toggleFavorite`'s optimistic-update shape but reconciling with the response's authoritative `reaction_counts` on success rather than trusting local arithmetic (these are server-aggregated numbers other users also affect) -- and, unlike `toggleFavorite`, rethrowing on failure rather than swallowing silently, so `TestimonyDetailScreen` can surface a real "Couldn't update your reaction" SnackBar. New `GuestRestrictions.showReactionRestriction` (`guest_restriction_modals.dart`) for the guest-gating case, same pattern as favorite/comment. 8 new/changed tests: 4 widget tests for `ReactionPillsRow` in isolation (counts render, zero-count omits the number, active pill gets the filled purple style, tap reports the right type) and 4 controller tests using a mocked `ApiClient` (optimistic update before the request resolves, reconciliation with the server response, rollback on failure, and set-then-remove using the real POST/DELETE split) -- the controller tests needed to account for `TestimonyFeedController.build()`'s own background feed/favorites refresh hitting the same mock client and racing the assertions, so the mock keeps testimony `t1` present in that generic response rather than wiping it. Full `flutter analyze`: clean. Full `flutter test`: 9 pre-existing failures, all confirmed unrelated by reproducing one (`login flow - validation and success navigation`) against a stashed pre-Slice-2 baseline with identical results -- all 9 are live calls to the real backend (`itestified-backend.onrender.com`) returning 400s or, for two of them, hanging past Flutter's 10-minute test timeout (`ApiClient` has no request timeout configured, a pre-existing gap, not something this slice introduced); none reference any file this slice touched.
- **Slice 3 — Favorite/bookmark stays intact, re-iconed** — Goal: Favoriting still works exactly as before, just without visually colliding with the new reactions. no functional or backend change to the existing Favorite action, but its icon changes from a heart to something that doesn't visually collide with the new reactions (bookmark/ribbon, exact icon TBD at implementation time)
  - Implemented 2026-07-30. Scoped narrowly to where the actual collision exists: the testimony detail screen's Favorite toggle (`Icons.favorite`/`favorite_border` → `Icons.bookmark`/`bookmark_border`, same purple60-when-active color logic, in both `WrittenTestimonyDetailContent`'s top-bar `IconButton` and `VideoTestimonyDetailContent`'s `OverlaidActionButton`) -- the only two spots where the icon now sits directly next to the new reaction pills, one of which ("Gives me hope" 💛) is itself heart-adjacent. Deliberately did **not** touch the heart icon anywhere else in the app (home discover cards, the Favorites tab's own lists, search results, inspirational pictures, onboarding) -- those never render next to reaction pills, so there's no actual collision there, and sweeping the icon change app-wide would have been a much bigger visual-identity change than what was reviewed for this phase. No test changes needed: grepped both `test/` for any assertion on `Icons.favorite`/`favorite_border` scoped to the testimony detail screen specifically and found none (the two hits elsewhere are unrelated features -- inspirational pictures and an unrelated "part6" flow). Full `testimony_detail_verification_test.dart` (27 tests) and the reaction pills test still pass; `flutter analyze` clean.

Test:
- backend: reacting creates the reaction; switching a reaction type moves the count rather than creating a second row or double-counting; removing is a separate action from setting (not a toggle on the same call); counts aggregate correctly per type; a guest (unauthenticated) cannot react; Favorite is unaffected by any of this — all implemented and passing as of Slice 1, see its note above
- mobile: pills render real counts and the current user's active reaction; tapping switches/removes correctly; guest tap shows a sign-in prompt instead of a silent no-op; Favorite/bookmark still works exactly as before, just with its new icon — all implemented and passing as of Slices 2-3, see their notes above

Status: Phase 15 complete 2026-07-30 — all 3 slices implemented, see each slice's notes above.

### Phase 16: Personalized "For You" Feed

Background: second of the three engagement concepts from the 2026-07-29 review (see Phase 15's background). Today's Home feed is one admin-curated payload, identical for every user. This phase makes it start reflecting what someone actually cares about, without losing the admin's editorial Trending section underneath it.

Decisions (accepted by the admin 2026-07-30, "I am okay with phase 16, lets go ahead"):
- **Signal source: both explicit and implicit, blended.** Explicit: a "Follow" action on a category, same shape as the existing Favorite feature. Implicit: categories a user has already favorited or reacted to are auto-included even without an explicit follow, so there's no cold-start problem for existing users on day one. This mirrors YouTube's own model (explicit subscriptions blended with watch-history weighting), the same pattern cited in the original concept review.
- **Follow is its own model**, not reusing Favorite: `UserFollowedCategory` (user, category, created_at, unique together) — same shape as `TestimonyFavorite`, kept as a separate table since "I favorited one testimony in this category" and "I want to follow this category going forward" are different signals with different lifetimes.
- **Empty state**: a user with zero followed categories and zero favorites/reactions sees no "For You" section at all — Home falls back to exactly what it shows today (Trending only). No fabricated personalization for a signal-less account.
- **"Follow more" suggestions**: v1 just lists all active categories the user doesn't already follow, alphabetically. No ranking/popularity logic yet — simplest thing that works, revisit if it's not good enough once real usage data exists.
- **Guests**: no personalization is possible without an account, so guests never see a "For You" section, same as today.

Build:
- backend: `UserFollowedCategory` model + follow/unfollow endpoint (mirrors `FavoriteToggleView`'s POST/DELETE shape exactly); a `for-you` feed endpoint unioning testimonies from followed categories and categories the user has favorited/reacted to, most recent first, same pagination as the existing testimony list
- backend: category list response includes each category's `is_followed` for the requesting user (same `context["request"]` pattern already used for `TestimonyDetailSerializer.my_reaction`)
- mobile: Home gains a "For You" rail (hidden when the user has no signal at all) with follow chips for their current topics plus a "+ Follow more" entry point; Category screen / testimony detail gain a way to follow a category directly

Sub-slices:

- **Slice 1 — User follows or unfollows a testimony category (backend)** — Goal: A user can tell the platform which categories they care about. an authenticated user can follow any active category and unfollow it later; the public category list reflects the requesting user's follow state
  - Implemented 2026-07-30. New `UserFollowedCategory` model (`apps/testimonies/models.py`), `UniqueConstraint(user, category)` -- same shape as `TestimonyFavorite`, kept as its own table per the decision above. `CategoryFollowToggleView` (`apps/testimonies/api/views.py`) mirrors `FavoriteToggleView` exactly: `POST /api/v1/testimonies/categories/<id>/follow/` (`get_or_create`, idempotent, 201; 404 if the category doesn't exist or isn't active), `DELETE` on the same URL to unfollow (200, no-op if not following). `TestimonyCategorySerializer` gained `is_followed`; rather than one `.exists()` query per category row (categories are admin-curated and few today, but no reason to build in an N+1), `PublicCategoryListView.get_serializer_context()` prefetches the requesting user's followed-category IDs once as a set and the serializer just checks membership -- zero extra queries for a guest, one query total for an authenticated user regardless of how many categories exist. New migration `testimonies.0010_userfollowedcategory`. 5 new tests in `apps/testimonies/tests/test_api.py`: guest sees `is_followed: false` for everything; follow then unfollow reflects correctly in the public list; following the same category twice doesn't error or create a duplicate row; unauthenticated follow attempt is rejected (401); following an inactive category is rejected (404). Full backend suite: 314 tests, same pre-existing 21 `apps.authn` failures/errors (live Brevo calls, unrelated), `makemigrations --check` clean.
- **Slice 2 — User's feed reflects what they follow and already engage with (backend)** — Goal: The backend can produce a feed personalized to a specific user's interests. a new `for-you` endpoint returns approved testimonies from the user's followed categories, blended with categories they've favorited/reacted to, most recent first; returns an explicit empty result (not an error) for a user with no signal yet, so the mobile client knows to hide the section rather than show nothing
  - Implemented 2026-07-30. `GET /api/v1/testimonies/for-you/` (`ForYouTestimonyListView`, `apps/testimonies/api/views.py`), same auth/pagination shape as `FavoriteTestimonyListView` (`TokenAuthentication` + `IsAuthenticated`, `TestimonyPagination`). `get_queryset` unions three small ID sets -- categories the user follows (`UserFollowedCategory`), categories of testimonies they've favorited (`TestimonyFavorite`), and categories of testimonies they've reacted to (`TestimonyReaction`) -- then filters approved, active-category testimonies to that set, ordered most-recent-first. A user with no signal in any of the three sources gets `Testimony.objects.none()`, which `ListAPIView`'s normal pagination renders as an ordinary `{"count": 0, "results": []}` response -- no special-casing needed, this is just what an empty queryset already does. 5 new tests in `apps/testimonies/tests/test_api.py`: guest rejected (401), no-signal user gets an explicit empty result, followed-category-only testimonies show up and nothing else does, a favorited-but-not-followed category's testimonies show up (proving the implicit signal works without an explicit follow), same for a reacted-but-not-followed category. Full backend suite: 319 tests, same pre-existing 21 `apps.authn` failures/errors (live Brevo calls, unrelated), `makemigrations --check` clean (no schema change -- this slice is read-only over Slice 1's model plus existing Favorite/Reaction tables).
- **Slice 3 — User follows a category and sees Home change accordingly (mobile)** — Goal: A user's Home screen visibly reflects their own interests, without losing the existing curated sections. "For You" rail wired to Slice 2, "Follow"/"+ Follow more" chips wired to Slice 1, section hidden entirely for a signal-less account; existing admin-curated Trending sections stay exactly as they are today, underneath
  - Implemented 2026-07-30. Found and fixed a real bug along the way: `categoriesProvider` (`lib/features/categories/presentation/state/categories_controller.dart`) was calling `/testimonies/categories/` with `includeAuth: false` hardcoded -- meaning even a logged-in user's request never carried their token, so `is_followed` could never come back true for anyone. Fixed by dropping that override (default is authenticated). `CategoryItem` gained `id` and `isFollowed`; adding a required `id` field broke three files (`categories_local_datasource.dart`, `categories_repository_impl.dart`, `categories_repository.dart`, plus `get_categories.dart`) that turned out to be genuinely dead code -- confirmed via grep that nothing outside that subtree ever referenced them, `categoriesProvider` has always bypassed them and called `ApiClient` directly -- so they were deleted rather than patched to satisfy the compiler. New `toggleCategoryFollow(WidgetRef, CategoryItem)` (same file) POSTs/DELETEs to Slice 1's endpoint and invalidates both `categoriesProvider` and the new `forYouTestimoniesProvider` (`lib/core/content/testimony_feed_controller.dart`, mirrors `remoteFavoriteTestimoniesProvider`'s exact shape against Slice 2's `/testimonies/for-you/`). `CategoryScreen` gained a Follow/Following pill per row (guest tap shows `GuestRestrictions.showFollowRestriction`, a new fourth method alongside the existing favorite/comment/reaction ones). `DiscoverScreen` gained a "For You" section -- deliberately spliced in unconditionally rather than through the backend-driven `sectionOrder` switch (adding a case there would silently no-op until an admin config change includes it), reusing the exact same video-rail-then-text-list pattern already used for Trending, hidden entirely whenever `forYouItems` is empty (covers both "guest" and "registered with no signal yet" in one check). New `AppRouter.forYouFeed` route reuses the existing generic `TestimonyListScreen` for its "See All" destination. 11 new tests: 4 for `categoriesProvider`/`toggleCategoryFollow` (id/is_followed parsing, the includeAuth regression guard, POST-when-unfollowed, DELETE-when-followed) and 7 for `CategoryScreen` (Follow pill renders, Following pill renders, guest tap shows the restriction dialog instead of calling the API). Full `flutter analyze`: clean. Full `flutter test`: verified against a second complete run before this slice's changes -- identical failure set (same 9 pre-existing live-backend-dependent tests, cross-checked test-by-test, not just by count) both before and after.
- **Slice 4 — Admin sees which categories are most followed (admin)** — Goal: Admin can see which categories users actually care about, to guide content strategy. the existing admin category list gains a follower count per category, so content strategy decisions (what to commission more of) aren't guesswork
  - Implemented 2026-07-30. `AdminTestimonyCategorySerializer` gained `follower_count`, a `SerializerMethodField` that reads an annotated `follower_count` off the model instance when present and falls back to a live `obj.followed_by.count()` otherwise -- so it works correctly everywhere `AdminTestimonyCategorySerializer` is used (including `AdminCategoryActivationView`, which builds a single unannotated instance) without forcing every call site to remember to annotate. `AdminCategoryListCreateView`/`AdminCategoryDetailView` (`apps/testimonies/api/views.py`) both annotate `.annotate(follower_count=Count("followed_by", distinct=True))` -- one query for the whole admin category list, not one `.count()` per row. No new migration (read-only aggregate over Slice 1's existing `UserFollowedCategory` table). 1 new test: two users follow a category, list and detail both report `follower_count: 2`; a category nobody follows reports `0`, not an omitted field or an error. Full backend suite: 320 tests, same pre-existing 21 `apps.authn` failures/errors (live Brevo calls, unrelated), `makemigrations --check` clean.

Test:
- backend: follow/unfollow is idempotent; a user's `for-you` feed includes testimonies from followed categories and from categories they've favorited/reacted to, and excludes everything else; a signal-less user gets an explicit empty result; a guest cannot follow (401); the admin category list/detail report an accurate follower count, including zero for an unfollowed category — all implemented and passing as of Slices 1-2 and 4, see their notes above
- mobile: "For You" rail is absent for a signal-less account and appears once a follow/favorite/reaction exists; follow chip state matches the backend; existing Trending sections are unaffected — all implemented and passing as of Slice 3, see its note above

Post-completion observation (2026-07-30, live-tested with the admin): on the current backend (only 4 categories: Deliverance, Faith, Healing, Salvation), "For You" felt too broad rather than personalized — the matching is category-level, not per-testimony, so favoriting/reacting in even 2-3 categories pulls in most of the entire catalog. Diagnosed together: this isn't a bug in the matching logic, it's that 4 categories can't discriminate much no matter how the query is written. Options discussed (cap categories contributing to a user's signal, drop implicit favorite/reaction signal, cap items per category, time-decay old signal, or simply add more categories) — the admin's call: **add more categories first** (a content decision, not code) once the current UI/UX refinement pass is done, and revisit whether the matching logic itself needs tightening only after there's a large-enough category list to actually judge it against. No code change made here; this is intentionally deferred, not forgotten.

Status: Phase 16 complete 2026-07-30 — all 4 slices implemented, see each slice's notes above. Category matching left intentionally coarse for now (see post-completion observation above) -- next real step is admin adding more categories, not further code changes.

### Phase 17: Scripture Streak (Habit Loop)

Background: third of the three engagement concepts from the 2026-07-29 review. Today's Scripture of the Day card is purely decorative from a retention standpoint — it displays the verse and does nothing else. Already decided earlier in this review: **default-on for every user**, not opt-in.

Infrastructure note: this phase does **not** need new scheduling infrastructure. `render.yaml` already runs a daily cron (`publish_scheduled_testimonies && publish_due_scriptures && publish_due_inspirational_pictures`, built in Phase 12); the reminder job below is one more management command appended to that same line. This makes the "biggest lift of the three" framing in Phase 15's background a bit less true than it first looked — the lift is a new model and command, not new infrastructure.

Proposed decisions (same caveat as Phase 16 — these are calls made to give you something concrete to react to, not agreed-upon like Phase 15's were):
- **What counts as "reading" today's scripture**: an explicit `POST` fired when the user actually opens the scripture detail (mirrors the existing `PublicTestimonyViewIncrementView` pattern — an explicit action, not inferred from a `GET` that could fire just from Home rendering the card). Uses the **user's local calendar date**, sent by the mobile client, not server UTC — the classic streak-app bug is a user reading right after local midnight getting robbed of credit because the server's day rolled over first.
- **Streak model**: denormalized fields on `Profile` (`scripture_streak_count`, `scripture_last_read_date`, plus two fields tracking freeze usage — see below), same "cheap to read on every Home load, kept in sync by the command that changes it" pattern as the reaction counters in Phase 15. A separate `ScriptureReadReceipt` (user, read_date, unique together) log backs it, mainly so a streak can be reconstructed/audited later if the denormalized fields ever drift.
- **Streak freeze**: 2 per calendar month, consumed automatically (no user action) the first time a single day is missed — covers exactly one missed day, doesn't stack to cover a longer gap. A gap of 2+ consecutive missed days, or a missed day with no freezes left that month, resets the streak to 1 on the next read rather than to 0 (today's read still counts). Matches Duolingo's Streak Freeze framing from the original review's research (their own published data: −21% churn from this exact mechanic).
- **Reminder push**: once daily, only to users who haven't read today's scripture yet, sent at a single fixed time rather than per-user local evening — there's no per-user timezone field on `Profile` today, so true "7pm your time" isn't achievable without adding one first. Flagging this as a real gap: if per-user local-time reminders matter, that's a small prerequisite slice (add a timezone field, capture it from the device) before this one, not part of this phase as scoped.
- **No in-app notification row for the daily reminder** — deliberately push-only, skipping the `UserNotification` row every existing `notify_*` helper creates. A once-a-day nudge for every user would flood the notifications table and the user's own notification list; this is the one place in the notification system that intentionally doesn't follow that pattern.

Build:
- backend: `ScriptureReadReceipt` model, `Profile` streak fields, `mark_scripture_read` service command computing streak continuation/freeze-consumption/reset, `POST /content/scripture/today/read/`
- backend: new management command (e.g. `send_scripture_streak_reminders`) appended to the existing daily cron line in `render.yaml`, querying users with `scripture_last_read_date != today` and push-opted-in, calling `send_push_to_users` directly (no new `NotificationType`, no `UserNotification` rows, per the decision above)
- mobile: Home's Scripture of the Day card becomes the streak card from the concept mockup (streak count, day-dot row, freeze status), calling the new mark-as-read endpoint when the user actually opens it

Sub-slices:

- **Slice 1 — User's streak updates when they read today's scripture (backend)** — Goal: Reading today's scripture is tracked as part of a daily habit, fairly and without double-counting. opening today's scripture for the first time that calendar day extends the streak (or starts a new one, or consumes a freeze and continues, per the rules above); opening it again the same day is a no-op
  - Implemented 2026-07-30. New `ScriptureReadReceipt` model (`apps/content/models.py`, `UniqueConstraint(user, read_date)`) and four new `Profile` fields (`apps/users/models.py`): `scripture_streak_count`, `scripture_last_read_date`, `scripture_streak_freezes_used`, `scripture_streak_freeze_month` (the last two track the monthly freeze allowance, resetting when the stored freeze month no longer matches the current one -- no scheduled reset job needed). `mark_scripture_read(*, user, read_date)` (`apps/content/services/commands.py`, new `apps/content/exceptions.py` for `ContentError`) implements the streak rules from the background above: gap of 1 day extends, gap of exactly 2 days consumes a freeze if one's available that month (else resets to 1), gap of 3+ days always resets to 1 regardless of freezes (freezes only ever cover one missed day), reading the same date twice is an idempotent no-op. Rejects a `read_date` more than 1 day from the server's own `timezone.localdate()` -- not an anti-cheating measure (this is a private, non-competitive stat, gaming your own number has no stakes) but a sanity bound against a genuinely broken device clock silently corrupting streak state. `POST /api/v1/content/scripture/today/read/` (`ScriptureReadView`, `TokenAuthentication` + `IsAuthenticated`, matching the mobile-authenticated convention rather than the admin `SessionAuthentication` one) takes `{"read_date": "YYYY-MM-DD"}` -- required, not defaulted server-side, since trusting the client's own local date over the server's is the entire point of this design -- and returns `{"streak_count", "last_read_date", "freezes_remaining"}`. Found and fixed a real bug while testing: the no-op idempotent path originally returned `user.profile` (Django's cached reverse-relation object), which could be stale if an earlier call in the same request already updated a separately-queried `Profile` instance with the same PK -- fixed to fetch fresh via `Profile.objects.get(user=user)`. New migrations `content.0008_scripture_read_receipt` and `users.0004_profile_scripture_streak_fields`. 13 new tests: 9 in new `apps/content/tests/test_services.py` covering the streak arithmetic directly (first read, consecutive extends, same-day no-op, single-day-gap freeze consumption, a second freeze in the same month, no freezes left resets to 1, freeze allowance resets across a calendar-month boundary, a 3+ day gap always resets even with freezes available, the date-sanity rejection) and 4 in `apps/content/tests/test_api.py` covering the endpoint contract (401 for guests, real response shape, 400 for a missing `read_date`, 400 for a date far from today). Full backend suite: 333 tests, same pre-existing 21 `apps.authn` failures/errors (live Brevo calls, unrelated), `makemigrations --check` clean.
- **Slice 2 — User sees their live streak on Home (mobile)** — Goal: A user sees their real reading streak front and center on Home. the streak card replaces the static Scripture of the Day card, shows the real current streak and remaining freezes, and calls Slice 1's endpoint when opened
  - Backend piece implemented 2026-07-30. `GET /api/v1/content/home-feed/` (`mobile_home_feed_view`, `apps/content/api/views.py`) gained a new `scripture_streak` key, via a new `_scripture_streak_payload(request)` helper returning `{"streak_count", "last_read_date", "freezes_remaining", "read_today", "recent_read_dates"}` (the last one the trailing 7 days of `ScriptureReadReceipt` dates, for the mobile day-dot row) for an authenticated request with a `Profile`, or `null` for a guest. Found and fixed the same authentication bug already seen once this session: the view's `@authentication_classes([])` override meant a sent token could never be recognized at all, even though `@permission_classes([])` still let guests through -- removed the override so the global default (`SessionAuthentication` + `TokenAuthentication`) applies, and guests still pass because permissions (not authentication) are what's actually empty. Also hit a Python 3.9 compatibility bug: the helper's first draft used `-> dict | None` (PEP 604), which raises `TypeError` at import time on this codebase's Python 3.9 -- fixed with `from typing import Optional` and `-> Optional[dict]`. 2 new tests in `apps/content/tests/test_api.py` (`scripture_streak` is `null` for a guest home-feed request; reflects real state -- `streak_count`, `read_today`, `freezes_remaining`, `recent_read_dates` -- for an authenticated user who just read). Full `apps.content` suite: 33/33 passing. No migration needed (read-only over Slice 1's existing fields).
  - Mobile piece implemented 2026-07-30. `Endpoints.contentScriptureTodayRead` added (`lib/core/network/endpoints.dart`). `HomeScriptureContent` (`lib/features/home/presentation/state/home_content_controller.dart`) gained `hasStreakData`, `streakCount`, `freezesRemaining`, `readToday`, `lastReadDate`, `recentReadDates` fields plus a `copyWith`, parsed from the new `scripture_streak` key on the home-feed response -- `hasStreakData` is a separate explicit flag rather than inferred from `streakCount == 0`, since a real, freshly-registered user's streak genuinely is 0 and that's not the same as a guest getting no streak data at all. New `ScriptureStreakController` (`lib/features/home/presentation/state/scripture_streak_controller.dart`, `NotifierProvider<ScriptureStreakController, HomeScriptureContent?>`) holds a local overlay of the streak state seeded from `homeContentProvider` via `ref.listen`, with `markTodayRead()` mirroring `TestimonyFeedController.setReaction`'s optimistic-update-then-reconcile shape: applies the increment locally, POSTs `{"read_date": <device's local date>}` to Slice 1's endpoint, reconciles with the authoritative `streak_count`/`freezes_remaining`/`last_read_date` from the response on success, rolls back and rethrows on failure. New `ScriptureStreakCard` widget (`lib/features/home/presentation/widgets/scripture_streak_card.dart`) replaces the plain `ScriptureCard` for a registered user with real streak data: 🔥 streak count header, verse text, a 7-day dot row built from `recentReadDates` (filled/checked for a read day, outlined for today if unread), a "Read today's verse →" CTA (swapped for a "Today's verse read" confirmation once `readToday` is true) wired to `markTodayRead()`, and a "Streak freeze available · N left this month" footer when freezes remain. Wired into `discover_screen.dart`'s `scriptureSection()`: shown only when `widget.access == UserAccess.registered && scripture.hasStreakData`; guests and users without streak data yet keep seeing the original plain `ScriptureCard`, unchanged. 11 new tests (2 in `home_content_controller_test.dart` for the `scripture_streak` mapping -- present vs. absent; 9 in new `scripture_streak_controller_test.dart` for `markTodayRead`'s optimistic update, reconciliation, rollback-on-failure, and already-read-today no-op). `flutter analyze` clean on all changed files. Full mobile suite: 4 pre-existing failures, all confirmed unrelated by reproducing them identically against a stashed clean tree (`git stash` + rerun) -- live network calls to the real production backend in tests that don't mock `ApiClient` intermittently return HTTP 400, a known environmental flakiness class, not a regression from this change.
  - Visual polish pass, 2026-07-30 (user: "The scripture streak card on the artifact is more polished than our own"). Pulled the original engagement mockup's exact CSS via its published Artifact and rebuilt `ScriptureStreakCard` to match: a diagonal deep-jewel-tone gradient with a soft radial glow tucked in the bottom-right corner (was a flat `AppColors.deepPurple` fill), an italic serif verse as the visual anchor with the reference/version as a small caption below it (was reversed -- reference bolded above an unstyled quote), and weekday letters inside every day-dot rather than only a checkmark on read days. User clarified "slightly different polished colors per day" meant the whole card's own accent should rotate with the actual day of the week (not a gradient across the 7 dots within one card, which was my first, rejected read). First implementation of that rotated through unrelated hues per weekday (orange, teal, gold, sky blue, etc.) -- user corrected this too ("The color should be gradient and shades of the primary color"): replaced with a single 7-step `Color.lerp` between `AppColors.purple50` and the app's actual primary (`AppColors.brandMainColor1`), so the base card gradient stays one constant deep purple every day and only the accent (glow blob, filled day-dots, CTA check) shifts shade/tint within that same purple family across the week -- distinct per day, but always unmistakably on-brand.
  - Same pass: user flagged that some scriptures run longer than the card has room for, and that the admin-authored prayer meant to close out each daily read (`ScriptureOfTheDay.prayer`, already a model field and already included in `ScriptureSerializer` and therefore already in the home-feed's `scripture` payload -- a real backend field with nowhere to render on mobile, not a new backend requirement) had never been surfaced. Added `prayer` to mobile's `HomeScriptureContent` and its home-feed parsing; the inline verse on the card is now clipped to 3 lines (`maxLines`/`overflow: ellipsis`); new `showScriptureDetailSheet` (`scripture_detail_sheet.dart`) is a bottom sheet showing the full verse and prayer, opened by tapping anywhere on the card (the whole card is now one `InkWell`, replacing the old CTA-only tap target). Opening the sheet also calls `onReadToday()` unconditionally -- matches Slice 1's own documented definition of "reading" as an explicit open action, and is safe since both the controller and the backend already no-op once today's read is recorded.
