  - Live-verified on the Android emulator against the real production backend after a full `flutter clean` rebuild (an incremental `flutter build apk --debug` was silently serving a stale build once during this pass -- caught by the running app still showing the pre-redesign card after two reinstalls, resolved by `flutter clean` before rebuilding): today (a live Thursday) renders the teal/mint weekday palette exactly as designed, the verse preview truncates correctly, and tapping the card opens the sheet showing the full "Isaiah 41:10" text plus its real admin-authored prayer text pulled live from the backend.
- **Slice 3 — User who hasn't read today gets one reminder before the day ends (backend)** — Goal: A user at risk of breaking their streak gets exactly one gentle nudge. the daily cron addition pushes once to eligible users only, respects existing push opt-out, never fires twice in the same day for the same user
  - Implemented 2026-07-30. New `send_scripture_streak_reminders()` (`apps/content/services/commands.py`) queries `Profile.objects.filter(user__account_status=ACTIVE).exclude(scripture_last_read_date=today)` -- correctly catches both never-read users (`NULL`) and stale-streak users (an earlier date), since Django's `exclude(field=value)` never matches `NULL` rows -- and delegates entirely to the existing `send_push_to_users` for opt-out and device-token filtering, matching the decision to keep this push-only with no `UserNotification` row. New `send_scripture_streak_reminders` management command (`apps/content/management/commands/`) wraps it, mirroring `publish_due_scriptures.py`'s existing thin-command style.
  - Found a real bug in the phase's own background while wiring this up: the plan's Build note said to append this to "the existing daily cron line in render.yaml," but that line (`itestified-scheduled-publisher`) actually runs on `*/5 * * * *` -- every 5 minutes, not daily. Appending there would have pushed this reminder up to 288 times a day per user, directly violating this slice's own "never fires twice in the same day" requirement. Fixed by giving it a genuinely separate cron job (`itestified-scripture-streak-reminder` in `render.yaml`) on its own `schedule: "0 19 * * *"` (19:00 UTC, once daily) -- the correctness of "once daily" now comes from the cron schedule itself, not from any idempotency guard in the code. Confirmed `FIREBASE_CREDENTIALS_JSON` is referenced by `apps/common/services/push.py` but was never actually declared in `render.yaml` for the existing cron service either (a pre-existing gap, left alone) -- added it as a `sync: false` placeholder on the new job so this reminder's push isn't silently starved of credentials that the old job also lacks on paper.
  - 5 new tests in `apps/content/tests/test_services.py` (`SendScriptureStreakRemindersTests`, mirroring `apps/notifications/tests/test_push_notifications.py`'s `patch("apps.notifications.services.send_push_to_tokens")` + `captureOnCommitCallbacks(execute=True)` pattern): targets both never-read and stale-streak users while excluding a user who already read today; excludes deactivated accounts; respects `allow_push_notifications=False`; creates zero `UserNotification` rows; returns 0 and skips the push call entirely when nobody is owed a reminder. Full `apps.content` suite passing. Live sanity-ran the management command against the local dev DB (after catching it up on a pending migration) -- correctly targeted every unread active user, logged `skipped_no_device_tokens` for the expected reason in that environment.
  - Still open, unchanged from the original background: no per-user timezone field exists on `Profile`, so this fires at one fixed UTC time (19:00) for every user rather than each person's own evening -- flagged again here as the item most likely to need a decision (add a timezone field, capture it from the device) if per-user local-time reminders matter later.
- **Slice 4 — Admin sees aggregate streak engagement (admin)** — Goal: Admin can tell whether the streak feature is actually driving engagement. a small addition (e.g. to an existing admin dashboard/stats view) showing how many users have an active streak and the distribution of streak lengths, so there's a way to tell whether the feature is actually working
  - Implemented 2026-07-30. Chose the existing Scripture of the Day admin page (`dashboard/frontend/src/app/(admin)/scripture-of-the-day/`) as the "existing admin dashboard/stats view," not the Phase 8 Analytics page (`analytics-page.tsx`) -- checked that page first and found its entire data layer (`get-analytics-view-model.ts`) is still 100% hardcoded fixture data, matching Phase 8's own "Not started" status; adding one real, backend-wired stat into an otherwise fully-mock page would have been misleading, and Scripture of the Day is both already real-data-wired and the more contextually relevant home for this stat anyway.
  - Backend: new `scripture_streak_engagement_stats()` in new `apps/content/services/queries.py` (this app's first selector/query module, per `AGENTS.md`'s services/queries.py convention -- everything in `commands.py` until now was a state-changing action). "Active" is defined as `scripture_last_read_date` within the last day (today or yesterday) -- documented explicitly in the docstring why a 2+ day-stale streak isn't counted as active even though its `scripture_streak_count` hasn't reset to 0 yet (that only happens lazily on the user's *next* read, whether via freeze or reset). Buckets active users' streak lengths into `1_to_3_days` / `4_to_7_days` / `8_to_30_days` / `31_plus_days`. New `AdminScriptureStreakStatsView` (`GET /api/v1/content/admin/scriptures/streak-stats/`, `SessionAuthentication` + `IsActiveAdmin`, matching every other admin content view's convention) just returns it.
  - Dashboard: `ScriptureOfTheDayViewModel` gained a `streakStats` field, fetched via a new `fetchStreakStats()` call inside `getScriptureOfTheDayViewModelFromApi` -- deliberately its own try/catch, independent of the main scripture-list fetch, so a stats-endpoint failure degrades to hiding the stats strip rather than putting the whole page into its existing full-page error state over a secondary addition. New `ScriptureStreakStatsCard` (`components/scripture-of-the-day/scripture-streak-stats-card.tsx`) renders above the existing table, reusing the Analytics page's own `MetricCard` visual language (`bg-[var(--color-surface-elevated)]`, same type scale) for consistency even though that page's data isn't reused.
  - 2 new backend tests (`ContentAdminApiTests` in `apps/content/tests/test_api.py`): buckets a mix of active/stale/never-read profiles correctly (stale-despite-nonzero-count case included explicitly); requires admin auth. 4 new frontend tests (2 in `get-scripture-of-the-day-view-model.test.ts` for the mapping and the independent-failure behavior; existing `scripture-of-the-day-page.test.tsx` suite re-run clean, no changes needed there since the card is conditionally rendered only when stats exist). Full backend suite and full frontend suite (`npx vitest run`, 186 tests) both passing; `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` clean on all changed frontend files; `makemigrations --check` clean (no new backend model, aggregate-only).

Test:
- backend: consecutive-day reads extend the streak; a single missed day with a freeze available continues it instead of resetting; a missed day with no freezes left (or a 2+ day gap) resets to 1 on the next read; reading twice in one calendar day doesn't double-count; the reminder only reaches users who haven't read today and respects push opt-out; a guest cannot mark a read (401) — all implemented and passing as of Slice 1, see its note above
- mobile: streak card reflects real state after opening the scripture; freeze-remaining count is accurate; card correctly shows a fresh streak after a reset rather than a stale number

Status: Complete 2026-07-30 — all 4 slices implemented, see their notes above. The timezone gap for the reminder push (Slice 3) shipped as a known, documented limitation (one fixed UTC send time) rather than a blocker; a per-user timezone field would be the prerequisite if that's ever revisited.

### Phase 18: Profile Identity & Reflection ("Your Journey")

Background: the last of the four original engagement concepts (Angle 03 — Identity & Reflection) from the 2026-07-29 review, and the one that didn't get turned into a phase when 15-17 were drafted (caught 2026-07-30, "We missed on the profile, check the artifact"). Today Profile is a pure settings/navigation menu — nothing on it reflects what someone has actually done in the app. A private "Your Journey" card fixes that, deliberately with no ranking or leaderboard (design decision already stated in the original mockup: "a testimony isn't a score to compete on").

Real gap found while scoping this (2026-07-30): of the mockup's three stats (Watched / Favorited / Shared), only **Favorited** is free today (`TestimonyFavorite` already exists). **Watched** has a small gap — `PublicTestimonyViewIncrementView` increments a global per-testimony counter but never records *which* user watched, so there's no per-user distinct-testimonies-watched count yet. **Shared** has a much bigger gap: the existing share sheet (`showTestimonyShareMenu`) is entirely decorative — the WhatsApp/Instagram/Facebook buttons have no `onTap` at all, no share package is even a dependency, nothing is ever actually shared (same finding already documented in Phase 11's own background). Real sharing is Phase 11's job, not this phase's.

Decision (2026-07-30, admin's call): **Shared stays mock data on the mobile side until Phase 11 ships.** Don't build backend tracking for a stat with nothing real behind it yet. Once Phase 11 Slice 2 (real share sheet) lands, come back and wire this stat to real data as a follow-up slice here.

Other decisions (proposed, following the artifact's own stated design — flag anything you'd call differently, same caveat as Phase 16/17 initially):
- **Watched tracking**: new `TestimonyWatch` model (user, testimony, unique together — same shape as `TestimonyFavorite`/`TestimonyReaction`), recorded once per distinct testimony the first time an authenticated user's view-increment call lands, not once per replay. "48 Watched" should read as 48 different testimonies, not 48 playback events.
- **Most-visited theme**: computed live at request time from category counts across favorites + reactions + watches (the same three signals Phase 16's For You feed already blends, just aggregated by category-count here instead of unioned into a feed) — no new aggregation table, this is a single authenticated user's own request, not a list of many users, so a live group-by is cheap.
- **Recap ("Your 2026 recap")**: teaser-only for this phase, per the artifact's own framing and its open "RECAP SCOPE" question — the card shows the CTA line, tapping it is a non-functional placeholder (or a "Coming soon" message, matching the pattern already used elsewhere this session for real-but-not-yet-wired affordances). Building an actual recap screen is separate future work once there's a full year of real activity data to make one worth building.
- **Privacy**: exposed only on the authenticated user's own profile request, never on the public author byline or any other user-facing serializer, never compared against other users.
- **Header redesign confirmed** (2026-07-30): the current `ProfileAvatarHeader` (`lib/features/profile/presentation/widgets/profile_avatar_header.dart`) is a big centered avatar (84px) with name/email stacked below it and a full-width "Edit Profile" pill underneath -- a vertical, centered layout. The artifact's header is a compact horizontal row instead: a smaller avatar on the left, name+email stacked next to it, a small "Edit" pill on the right. Admin's call: redesign to match the artifact. This touches the shared `ProfileAvatarHeader` widget, which is also used by the guest state (`'Guest Mode'` / `'Create Account'`) -- the horizontal layout needs to read correctly for both states, not just the registered one shown in the mockup.

Build:
- backend: `TestimonyWatch` model; `PublicTestimonyViewIncrementView` additionally records a watch for authenticated requests (existing endpoint, additive change, no new route); new `GET /api/v1/profile/me/journey/` returning `{"watched_count", "favorited_count", "most_visited_theme"}` (no `shared_count` from the backend at all yet -- mobile hardcodes that stat locally until Phase 11 exists to make it real, so there's nothing to later delete from the API)
- mobile: `ProfileAvatarHeader` redesigned to the compact horizontal layout (small avatar left, name/email + Edit pill on the right), covering both guest and registered states; Profile screen gains a "Your Journey" card directly below that redesigned header, above the existing menu list -- stat tiles, most-visited-theme bar chart, "Only you" private label, recap teaser

Sub-slices:

- **Slice 1 — User's watched testimonies are tracked (backend)** — Goal: The platform knows what a user has actually watched or read, once per testimony. opening a testimony's detail screen and starting playback (video) or viewing it (written) records that this user has watched this specific testimony, once per testimony no matter how many times they revisit it
  - Implemented 2026-07-30. New `TestimonyWatch` model (`apps/testimonies/models.py`, same shape as `TestimonyFavorite`: `user`+`testimony` `UniqueConstraint`). `PublicTestimonyViewIncrementView.post()` (`apps/testimonies/api/views.py`) additionally calls `TestimonyWatch.objects.get_or_create(user=request.user, testimony_id=testimony_id)` when `request.user.is_authenticated` -- `get_or_create`, not `create`, is what makes replays a no-op. No auth-recognition bug to fix here (unlike the two earlier `@authentication_classes([])` bugs this session) -- this view only overrides `permission_classes = [AllowAny]`, never `authentication_classes`, so it already inherits the global default (`SessionAuthentication` + `TokenAuthentication`) and correctly resolves `request.user` for a sent token. Registered in Django admin (`TestimonyWatchAdmin`, mirrors `TestimonyFavoriteAdmin`). New migration `testimonies.0011_testimonywatch_and_more`. 3 new tests in `apps/testimonies/tests/test_api.py`: records a watch for an authenticated viewer; three replays still leave exactly one row; a guest view-increment creates zero watch rows. Full `apps.testimonies` suite: 80/80 passing, `makemigrations --check` clean.
- **Slice 2 — Profile header matches the artifact's compact layout (mobile)** — Goal: The profile header looks more compact and modern, with no change in behavior. `ProfileAvatarHeader` becomes a horizontal row (avatar left, name/email + Edit pill right) for both guest and registered states, with no functional change to what any of those elements do
  - Implemented 2026-07-30. Found a real gap in the plan's own background while scoping this: `ProfileAvatarHeader` has a second, unrelated caller (`account_update_edit_profile_screen.dart`'s avatar picker -- big centered avatar with a plain "Edit" text link underneath, `title: ''`, `showTitle: false`) that the "touches the shared widget" note hadn't accounted for. Rather than replacing the widget's default layout (which would have broken that screen), added a `compact` bool parameter defaulting to `false`; the vertical/centered layout stays the untouched default, and the new horizontal row only renders when `compact: true`, wired only from `profile_screen.dart`. Compact mode: 24px avatar, name/email in an `Expanded` `Column` (both `maxLines: 1` + ellipsis so a long name/email can't push the pill off-screen), and an `OutlinedButton` pill (`purple30` border, `purple60` text, `borderRadius: 999`) for the primary action -- "Edit" for registered (shortened from "Edit Profile" to fit the pill), "Create Account" for guest. Updated `profile_flow_test.dart`'s `find.text('Edit Profile')` to `find.text('Edit')` to match.
  - Live-verified on the Android emulator: registered state (small avatar, "Igiebor Elvis" / email, "Edit" pill) and guest state ("Guest Mode" / subtitle, wider "Create Account" pill) both render correctly with no overflow; confirmed the "Edit" pill still navigates to the (visually unchanged, non-compact) avatar-picker screen correctly.
  - `flutter analyze` clean. Two pre-existing failures in `profile_flow_test.dart` (`email update flow validates and accepts OTP`, `logout keeps selected display mode`) reproduced identically against a stashed clean tree (`git stash` + rerun) -- confirming both predate this slice. Live log output shows real `HTTP 400` responses from the production backend during the run despite the file's own `HttpOverrides.global` mock, so the mock isn't actually intercepting traffic for some reason -- worth a closer look as a separate test-infra fix, but not something this slice touched or caused.
- **Slice 3 — User sees their journey on Profile (backend + mobile)** — Goal: A user can see a real reflection of their engagement with the platform on their own profile. the new journey endpoint returns real watched/favorited counts and a computed most-visited theme; Profile renders the "Your Journey" card with those real numbers, a mocked "Shared" tile, and a non-functional recap teaser, directly below the redesigned header
  - Implemented 2026-07-30. Backend: `GET /api/v1/profile/me/journey/` (`ProfileJourneyView`, `apps/users/api/views.py`, `IsAuthenticated`, no `authentication_classes` override so it inherits the global token-auth default like `CurrentProfileView`), backed by new `user_engagement_journey()` in new `apps/testimonies/services/queries.py` -- watched/favorited counts, plus category names blended from favorites+reactions+watches (same three signals Phase 16's For You feed already unions) via `collections.Counter`.
  - Mobile: `profileJourneyProvider` (`FutureProvider<ProfileJourney?>`, resolves to `null` for guests/failures rather than erroring) and `YourJourneyCard` wired into `profile_screen.dart` directly below the redesigned header, only when `access == UserAccess.registered`. "Shared" tile hardcoded to 0 per the phase's own decision (nothing real to count until Phase 11). Recap CTA is a tap-for-"coming soon"-snackbar placeholder.
  - Follow-up round after user review ("its not like the artifacts" -> "missing the bar chart... compare the artifacts with our ui"): the mockup's bar chart compares the user's top few categories, not just names a single winner -- extended `user_engagement_journey()` to also return `theme_distribution` (top 4 categories ranked by combined signal count, real counts only, capped via new `THEME_DISTRIBUTION_LIMIT`) instead of discarding everything but the top entry. Mobile's `_ThemeDistributionBars` renders proportional bars from these real counts (tallest = top-ranked, in the brand accent; the rest in a lighter tint) -- deliberately hidden entirely when a user has signal in 0 or 1 categories, since a one-bar "chart" isn't a comparison and padding it out with fabricated bars was ruled out. Also added the serif display font to the stat-tile numbers (`_StatTile`), matching the same font treatment already established by the Scripture streak card and the Phase 19 type audit.
  - 7 new backend tests total (`ProfileJourneyApiTests` in `apps/users/tests/test_api.py`): auth required; zero/no-signal case now includes empty `theme_distribution`; watched/favorited counts match their tables; most-visited theme and the full ranked distribution both correctly blend all three signal sources (a deliberately constructed case where no single table alone would show the true winner); distribution caps at 4 even with more real categories in play. 7 new mobile tests: 4 in `profile_journey_controller_test.dart` (response mapping, empty-distribution default, guest/failure null, no-ApiClient null) and 4 widget-level in new `profile_screen_journey_test.dart` mounting the real `ProfileScreen` -- a fresh-registered mount, a guest-does-not-see-it case, and (the one that actually caught a real timing bug, see below) an explicit guest-to-registered *transition* mid-session, plus a dedicated multi-category test asserting two proportional, differently-sized bars render with the correct top-ranked ordering.
  - Two real bugs caught and fixed during this slice, both worth recording: (1) the theme-line text used bare `RichText`, which `find.textContaining` in tests doesn't reliably match the way it matches `Text`/`Text.rich` -- switched to `Text.rich` (also arguably more idiomatic Flutter regardless of the test angle). (2) The first attempt at a guest-to-registered transition widget test used a single fixed-duration `pump()` after flipping access and failed intermittently -- the state flip triggers a *new* async provider watch (the journey fetch itself), which needs an additional bare `pump()` first to let that request actually go out before the timed pump can wait for its response; fixed by adding that extra `pump()` call. Neither was a bug in the shipped app code, both were test-only, but the second one is exactly why a live-device check that appeared to show "no card at all" after a real login turned out to be a Render deploy-lag red herring (confirmed by curling the production endpoint directly -- 403, not 404 -- and by the transition test passing reliably once fixed) rather than a real defect.
  - Live-verified end-to-end on the Android emulator against the real production backend: registered state shows real numbers (a real account's actual `Favorited: 3`, most-visited theme `Salvation`) and correctly shows *no* bar chart section at all, since that account's real engagement happens to be concentrated in a single category -- confirms the "hide below 2 categories" rule is working as designed, not a gap. The proportional multi-bar rendering itself is confirmed via the dedicated widget test above rather than a second live account, since reproducing multi-category real engagement live would have meant fabricating test activity across categories on a real account.
  - Full `apps.users` suite and full mobile `test/features/profile` suite passing (excluding the two already-documented pre-existing `profile_flow_test.dart` failures, unrelated to this slice); `flutter analyze` clean; `makemigrations --check` clean (no new backend model, aggregate-only).

Test:
- backend: watching the same testimony twice only counts once; watched/favorited counts match the underlying tables exactly; most-visited theme (and the full ranked distribution) picks the actual top categories and returns nothing (not an error) for a user with zero signal across all three sources; distribution caps at the top 4; the journey endpoint requires authentication (403 for guests) — all implemented and passing, see the note above
- mobile: the redesigned header renders correctly for both guest and registered states with all existing taps (avatar, Edit/Create Account) still working; stat tiles show real numbers for Watched/Favorited and a static placeholder for Shared; the bar chart reflects the real ranked distribution and hides itself below 2 categories; nothing on this card is ever shown to another user or ranked against anyone — all implemented and passing, see the note above

Status: Complete 2026-07-30 — all 3 slices implemented, see their notes above. Two decisions locked in from live discussion: Shared stays mock data until Phase 11, and the header matches the artifact's compact layout. The rest (watch-tracking shape, theme computation, teaser-only recap, and the follow-up real bar chart) follows the artifact's stated design, refined once via direct user comparison against the mockup.

### Phase 19: Testimony Type &amp; Pull-Quote Polish

Background: prompted by the Phase 17 Slice 2 streak card redesign (2026-07-30) — its serif-italic verse, clear size hierarchy, and truncation gave it a warmth the rest of the app's text doesn't have yet, and text testimonies (the most text-dense screens, carrying the same kind of personal, emotional content as the verse) were flagged as the natural next target ("we may need to visit all our text later, mostly text testimonies"). Reviewed via a before/after type-audit mockup (published as an Artifact, not built) covering `TextStoryCard` (Home/feed list cards) and `WrittenTestimonyDetailContent` (the detail screen) — user approved the direction ("I like the proposed design").

That review surfaced a real question: the proposed detail-screen treatment pulls one sentence out of the testimony as an italic quote, same device as the Scripture verse -- but a testimony's body has no structured way to say which sentence deserves that treatment. Discussed two options: an automatic heuristic (e.g. longest sentence, no new field or workflow) vs. a moderator-picked quote during the existing review workflow (mirrors `ScriptureOfTheDay.prayer`'s existing admin-authored-field pattern, and `Testimony.rejection_reason`'s existing moderator-authored-field-on-the-same-model pattern). Admin's call: the extra moderation step is worth it -- "a human picking the one sentence that hits hardest will consistently beat a heuristic."

Proposed decisions (same caveat as Phase 16/17 initially -- these give you something concrete to react to, not agreed-upon like Phase 15's were):
- **Pull-quote is moderator-curated only, no automatic extraction** -- confirmed above. A new `Testimony.pull_quote` field (`CharField`, blank=True, capped short -- proposing `max_length=280`, long enough for one real sentence, short enough to stay a pull-quote and not a second body), settable independently of `approve_testimony`/`reject_testimony` (not bundled into either action's signature) via a small new admin endpoint, so a moderator can add or edit it any time, not only at the moment of approval.
- **Optional, not required to publish** -- a testimony with a blank `pull_quote` simply renders without a pulled-quote block on mobile; there's no blocking gate on the moderation queue and no backfill effort implied for testimonies already published before this phase ships.
- **Detail-only, same scoping precedent as `my_reaction` (Phase 15)** -- `pull_quote` is added to `TestimonyDetailSerializer` only, not the list serializer; the list-card type refresh (Slice 1) is a pure typography change to the existing excerpt text, not a second surface for the curated quote.
- **No new fonts or design tokens** -- the type scale reuses exactly the two font families the streak card already introduced (`var(--font-display)`-equivalent serif for titles/quotes, existing UI sans for body/meta), per the audit mockup's own proposed scale table.

Build:
- backend: `Testimony.pull_quote` field + migration; new admin endpoint (mirrors `AdminUpdateVideoTestimonyView`'s existing small-scoped-PATCH pattern) for a moderator to set/edit it; add `pull_quote` to `TestimonyDetailSerializer`
- mobile: `TextStoryCard` (`lib/features/home/presentation/widgets/home_discover_cards.dart`) restyled per the audit's list-card proposal -- serif display title, refined excerpt size/line-height, caption-style meta line -- applies everywhere the card is reused (Home, For You, Trending, Category, Favorites, search), since it's one shared widget; `WrittenTestimonyDetailContent` (`lib/features/testimony_detail/presentation/widgets/testimony_detail_written_content.dart`) restyled per the audit's detail-screen proposal -- serif title, refined body line-height -- plus a new italic pull-quote block rendered only when `pull_quote` is non-empty

Sub-slices:

- **Slice 1 — Reader sees the refreshed type scale on testimony list cards (mobile)** -- Goal: Testimony list cards are easier to scan, with no backend change needed. `TextStoryCard`'s title, excerpt, and meta line adopt the audit's proposed scale; no backend dependency, ships independently of the pull-quote work
  - Implemented 2026-07-31. Pulled the exact CSS from the published type-audit Artifact (not memory) to confirm every value before writing code: title `serif, weight 500, 17.5px, line-height 1.22` (was `sans, w700, 18px`); excerpt `13px, line-height 1.58` (was unset/~14px, 1.4); byline `11px` (was 13px); meta caption line switched from `"Category • timeAgo"` at plain 11px to `"CATEGORY · TIMEAGO".toUpperCase()` at `9.3px, weight 500, letterSpacing 0.4` -- matches the mockup's `.new-sub` (`text-transform: uppercase`, `letter-spacing: 0.04em`, `·` not `•`) exactly, not just within the scale table's stated range. Deliberately did *not* italicize the excerpt into a quote treatment (the mockup's own "Thank God" example card did, as an illustration) -- the plan's own scope note is explicit that the curated pull-quote is Slice 3's job on the detail screen only, and the list-card excerpt stays plain body copy.
  - `TextStoryCard` is one shared widget reused across Home, For You, Trending, Category, Favorites, and search -- confirmed via grep this is the only place these particular styles live, so the refresh applies everywhere automatically with a single-file change. Confirmed `VideoCard` in the same file (a separate, unrelated card type) and a distinct `search_results_sections.dart` widget that also happens to use a `•`-separated meta line are untouched.
  - Live-verified on the Android emulator: serif titles, wider excerpt leading, and the uppercase `SALVATION · 5D AGO`-style caption line all render correctly across multiple real feed cards.
  - `flutter analyze` clean. Full `test/features/home` (12/12) and `test/features/browse` (50/53) suites run; the 3 non-passing browse tests reproduced identically against a stashed clean tree (`git stash` + rerun) -- confirmed pre-existing live-network-dependent flakiness (the same production-backend `HTTP 400` pattern documented earlier this session), not caused by this change. One of the three (`opens the notifications destination from registered home`) hadn't been individually confirmed as pre-existing before this slice; it now has been.
- **Slice 2 — Moderator can set a testimony's pull-quote during review (backend + admin dashboard)** -- Goal: A moderator can highlight the most powerful line of a testimony while reviewing it. the new field, migration, and admin endpoint exist; a moderator can add/edit a short quote from the dashboard, separate from the approve/reject decision itself
  - Implemented 2026-07-31. Backend: `Testimony.pull_quote` (`CharField(max_length=280, blank=True)`) + migration `0012_testimony_pull_quote`; `AdminTestimonyPullQuoteSerializer`/`AdminTestimonyPullQuoteView` mirror `AdminUpdateVideoTestimonyView`'s existing small-scoped-PATCH pattern exactly, wired at `admin/testimonies/<id>/pull-quote/`, independent of `approve_testimony`/`reject_testimony`; `pull_quote` added to `TestimonyDetailSerializer` (mobile) and `AdminTestimonyDetailSerializer` (admin) but not either list serializer, same precedent as Phase 15's `my_reaction`. 7 new tests (set, clear, independent-of-status, over-length-rejected, requires-admin-auth, plus one confirming the field's absence from the mobile list response) -- `apps.testimonies` 86/86 passing; full backend suite 358 tests, same 21 pre-existing `apps.authn` baseline failures, `makemigrations --check` clean.
  - Admin dashboard: new proxy route `src/app/api/admin/testimonies/[testimonyId]/pull-quote/route.ts` (mirrors the existing `.../edit/route.ts` pattern). `ApprovedDetailModal` (`testimonies-overlays.tsx`) gained a `PullQuoteEditor` -- a textarea (280-char cap) + Save button following the same client fetch+state pattern as `RejectModal`/`EditVideoModal` in the same file, showing inline Saving/Saved/error states without navigating away from the detail modal (unlike the approve/reject/edit actions, editing a quote shouldn't close the view). `pullQuote` added to `TextTestimonyRow`/`BackendTestimony`, populated only from the detail-fetch merge (list endpoint doesn't expose it). New test covers typing a quote, the Save button's disabled/enabled state tracking unsaved changes correctly, the PATCH call shape, and the "Saved" confirmation. Full dashboard suite: 187/187 passing, typecheck and lint clean.
- **Slice 3 — Reader sees the refreshed detail screen with the moderator's pull-quote, when set (mobile)** -- Goal: A reader sees the moderator's chosen pull-quote highlighted on the testimony detail screen. `WrittenTestimonyDetailContent` adopts the audit's proposed scale and renders the italic pull-quote block whenever `pull_quote` is non-empty; testimonies without one render the same as Slice 1's list-card treatment implies, just without that block
  - Implemented 2026-07-31. Pulled the exact detail-screen CSS from the published type-audit Artifact again to confirm values before coding: title `serif, weight 500, 21px, line-height 1.2` (was `w800, 24px`, no family); body `12.5px, line-height 1.62` (was `14px, 1.55`); byline switched from unstyled `w700 13px` to `purple60, w700, 11px` (matching Slice 1's own byline choice, not the audit's literal `#6e46ff`, for consistency with the already-shipped list-card treatment); meta line switched from `"Category • timeAgo"` at 12px to `"CATEGORY · TIMEAGO".toUpperCase()` at `9.3px, w500, letterSpacing 0.4`, same as Slice 1.

New widget test file (`testimony_detail_written_content_test.dart`, 3 tests: no block when absent, renders the quote text when present, meta line uppercases with a middle-dot) plus 2 new mapper tests in `testimony_detail_remote_provider_test.dart` (maps `pull_quote`, blank treated as no quote) -- all pass. - backend: `pull_quote` defaults to blank on existing and new testimonies (no backfill/migration data change); the admin endpoint requires moderator/admin auth and rejects an over-length quote; `TestimonyListSerializer`'s response never includes `pull_quote` (detail-only, confirmed by an explicit test per the Phase 15 `my_reaction` precedent)

It doesn't render through `TextStoryCard`/`WrittenTestimonyDetailContent` at all -- it's a fully separate, previously-untouched widget pair (`ModerationItemCard` for the list, an inline card in `MyTestimonyDetailScreen`), so Slice 1/3's "applies everywhere the shared widget is reused" claim never covered it. Fixed both to match the shipped scale exactly: list card title now `serif, w500, 17.5px, 1.22` (was `w600, 13px`), excerpt `15px, 1.5` (was `11px, 1.3`), meta line switched from `"Category • timeAgo"` to `"CATEGORY · TIMEAGO".toUpperCase()` at `12.5px, w500, letterSpacing 0.4`; detail-screen title `serif, w500, 21px, 1.2` (was `w700, 13px`), same meta-line format switch, body `15px, 1.55` (was `11px, 1.34`). ### Phase 20: Immersive Home Feed

Background: prompted directly by the Phase 19 type-polish work -- once testimonies read well, the surrounding screen started looking dated by comparison. Home today is a sectioned, admin-curated layout: a static "Guest Mode" banner, a Scripture of the Day card, a "For You" rail (Phase 16) with its own "See All", and a Trending rail underneath, each one a dead end you tap out of and back into. Discussed and reviewed via a published concept mockup (Artifact, not built) covering an auto-sliding carousel, a collapsing streak pill, autoplaying video, and dashboard-authored promo cards woven into the feed -- user approved the direction and all open questions below were resolved in that same review.

Decisions (agreed 2026-07-31, working from the reviewed mockup):
- **No revisit marker on looped content.** Once the feed runs out of real unseen testimonies, it loops back through already-seen ones (video and text), reshuffled -- rendered identically to a first view, no "you've seen this" badge or treatment.
- **Carousel sourcing is fully automatic.** Top inspirational pictures (Phase 7) blended with top moderator-curated pull-quote testimonies (Phase 19), no admin hand-picking or ordering step for this specific rotation.
- **Autoplay is connection-aware, not battery-aware.** Muted autoplay by default on Wi-Fi; a tap-to-play thumbnail (not autoplay) on cellular; one Settings toggle ("Play videos on cellular") for a user who wants it always-on. Keyed off connection type via the OS's own connectivity API, not low-power/battery-saver state, which signals something different (battery concern, not data cost).
- **Streak pill collapse, not removal.** After today's verse is read, Phase 17's full streak card collapses to a slim, still-tappable pill (matches the mockup's `.streakpill`) rather than disappearing -- keeps the habit loop visible without permanently costing feed space. Tapping the collapsed pill jumps straight into the existing `showScriptureDetailSheet` (already shipped in Phase 17), not back into the full card.
- **"From iTestified" cards are native, labeled, and cadence-fixed.** Same card chrome as a real testimony (so the feed doesn't feel like it's interrupted by foreign UI), but with a small "From iTestified" label so nobody mistakes one for an actual testimony. Admin-authored from the dashboard: title, body, optional CTA (label + destination), start/end date, active toggle -- same shape as `InspirationalPicture`'s existing admin-authored-content pattern. Insertion cadence (e.g. one per ~8-10 real testimonies, never as the very first card) is fixed in code, not admin-configurable at launch, so it can't be leaned on too hard.
- **Not AdMob, never was.** No third-party ad network, no real advertising SDK, no ad revenue mechanic -- "From iTestified" cards are house promotion only (giving, events, invite-a-friend, app announcements), consistent with the app's own purpose.

Build:
- backend: extend Phase 16's `for-you` ranking logic to become the entire Home feed (not a rail alongside separately-curated Trending sections) with loop-back pagination -- once a user's cursor exhausts real unseen content, the same endpoint starts serving already-seen testimonies again, reshuffled, rather than returning an empty page; new carousel endpoint blending top `InspirationalPicture` entries and top `pull_quote`-bearing testimonies; new `HomePromoCard` model (title, body, image, cta_label, cta_destination, starts_at, ends_at, is_active) + admin CRUD endpoints, mirroring `InspirationalPicture`'s existing admin pattern; feed-composition logic inserting active, in-window promo cards at the fixed cadence
- mobile: floating header that hides on scroll-down and reappears on scroll-up; auto-sliding carousel widget (crossfading slides, synced dot indicators); `ScriptureStreakCard` gains a collapsed-pill state wired to `showScriptureDetailSheet`; Home rebuilt as a single infinite-scroll list against the new unified feed endpoint, removing the sectioned rails and every "See All" entry point; video autoplay controller (exactly one active video at a time, muted by default, pauses off-screen, connection-aware per the decision above) plus a new Settings toggle; promo card rendering using the shared testimony card chrome
- dashboard: new "Home Promos" admin screen (create/edit/schedule/activate-deactivate a `HomePromoCard`), following the existing `InspirationalPicture` admin screen as the template

Sub-slices:

- **Slice 1 — Reader sees an auto-sliding showcase at the top of Home (backend + mobile)** — Goal: Home opens with an eye-catching, auto-rotating highlight reel. a new carousel blending top inspirational pictures and featured pull-quote testimonies rotates automatically at the top of Home; ships independently of the rest of this phase, no dependency on the feed rework below
  - Implemented 2026-07-31. Backend: `home_carousel_slides()` (new `apps/content/services/queries.py` selector) blends the 4 most recent published, non-expired `InspirationalPicture` entries with the 4 most recent approved testimonies carrying a non-blank `pull_quote` (Phase 19), pictures first then quotes, most recent within each -- genuinely automatic, no `FeaturedHomePicture`/`FeaturedHomeTestimony`-style admin curation step for this endpoint, per the phase's own decision. New public `GET /content/home-feed/carousel/` (`mobile_home_carousel_view`), same `authentication_classes=[]`/`permission_classes=[]` shape as the other mobile content reads. 3 new tests (blends both kinds with correct fields; excludes unpublished/expired pictures; excludes non-approved and blank-`pull_quote` testimonies) -- `apps.content` 43/43 passing, full backend suite 361 tests, same 21 pre-existing `apps.authn` baseline, no migration (read-only).
  - Mobile: `homeCarouselProvider` (`FutureProvider<List<HomeCarouselSlide>>`, new `home_carousel_controller.dart`) parses `results` into a `picture`/`testimonyQuote` union, empty list on any failure or when no `ApiClient` is registered (same guest-safe shape as `homeContentProvider`). New `HomeCarousel` widget (`home_carousel.dart`): a `Timer.periodic` (3.5s hold + 500ms `AnimatedSwitcher` crossfade) cycling through slides, with synced pill-style dot indicators -- deliberately built with real timer/animation logic rather than a static card, to match the mockup's own auto-advancing behavior, not just its look. Colors and gradients pulled directly from the reviewed mockup file: the exact `#6E46FF → #4A2F9E → #2C1A63` purple gradient for quote slides, `#3FB0A0 → #227B74 → #123F3D` teal for the guest slide, `9px`/`14px` kicker/quote type sizes, italic `fontFamily: 'serif'` for the primary line on every slide kind (matching the mockup's own choice to unify all three slide types under the same treatment). A client-only "Create Account" slide is appended for guests only (not returned by the backend) -- tapping it calls the existing `onCreateAccountRequested` callback, mirroring how the guest nudge was folded into the carousel per the design discussion rather than staying a separate static banner. Wired into `discover_screen.dart` right below the header/update-banner, above the existing "For You" rail; hidden entirely for a registered user with zero real slides (no empty placeholder), always shown for a guest (carries at least the account-creation slide).
  - Live-verified against real production data (2 published pictures, 2 pull-quote testimonies already live): both quote slides render pixel-faithful to the mockup, dots stay correctly synced through the crossfade. Found and adjusted one real issue live: one of the two real pictures has promotional text baked into the photo itself ("MASS EVANGIELSM... JESUS SAVES..."), which visually collided with the carousel's own overlay caption at the same bottom position -- strengthened the bottom scrim (stronger gradient, starting higher) as a general robustness improvement for busy/bright photos, but documented honestly that this doesn't fully resolve two overlapping text layers occupying the identical spot on one specific image; that's a content characteristic of this one upload, the same class of problem Phase 7 already hit once with the picture rail (which is why that surface moved to "photo, title below" instead of overlay). Left as a known, narrow edge case rather than over-engineering a fix for one image.
  - `flutter analyze` clean. New `home_carousel_controller_test.dart` (4 tests: maps both slide kinds, skips an unrecognized kind, empty on request failure, empty with no `ApiClient`) and `home_carousel_test.dart` (5 widget tests: empty state, picture slide renders, quote slide renders, guest CTA slide renders and taps through, dot count matches entry count) -- all passing. Full `test/features/home` (21/21) and `test/features/browse` (52/55, the same 2 pre-existing `discovery_routes_test.dart` failures reconfirmed against this session's already-documented live-backend flakiness baseline, unrelated to this change) both clean.
- **Slice 2 — Streak card gets out of the way once today's verse is read (mobile)** — Goal: The streak card stops taking up space once its job for the day is done. the full streak card collapses to a compact, still-tappable pill the moment `readToday` is true; tapping the pill opens the existing verse detail sheet directly
  - Implemented 2026-07-31. `ScriptureStreakCard.build()` now returns a new `_CollapsedStreakPill` whenever `scripture.readToday` is true, instead of the full card -- matches the mockup's `.streakpill` exactly (slim rounded bar, fire emoji + "Day N streak · today's verse read", a small green check-circle), using the app's own theme-aware `getSurfaceColor`/`getDividerColor`/`getTextColor` tokens rather than new hardcoded colors. Tapping the pill calls `showScriptureDetailSheet` directly (Phase 17's existing bottom sheet) -- no `onReadToday()` call, since reaching the collapsed state already implies today is read. No backend change needed; this is a pure read of the existing `readToday` field.
  - 3 new widget tests (`scripture_streak_card_test.dart`): full card renders pre-read, pill renders and shows the correct streak count post-read, tapping the pill opens the sheet (asserted via the sheet's own "TODAY'S SCRIPTURE" heading). `flutter analyze` clean; full `test/features/home` (24/24) passing.
  - Live-verified end-to-end on the Android emulator, including catching and fixing a real false alarm along the way: a `-r` reinstall initially kept serving pre-change behavior (the exact stale-build gotcha already documented earlier this session) -- resolved with `flutter clean` + `adb uninstall` + a fresh install, which lost the test account's session as expected (guest mode) and required manual Google re-sign-in. After signing back in, the collapsed pill rendered correctly ("Day 1 streak · today's verse read" -- the streak had reset to 1 since the account's last read was several real days earlier in this session), and tapping it opened the full verse + prayer sheet exactly as designed.
- **Slice 3 — Home becomes one continuous feed that never dead-ends (backend + mobile)** — Goal: A user can keep scrolling Home indefinitely and never hit a dead end. the sectioned Home (Trending rail, "For You" rail, each with its own "See All") is replaced by a single ranked, infinitely-scrolling feed; once real unseen content runs out, it loops back through already-seen testimonies, reshuffled, rather than stopping
  - Implemented 2026-07-31. Backend: new `GET /testimonies/home/feed/` (`HomeFeedView`), extending Phase 16's for-you ranking into the entire Home feed -- `_home_feed_base_queryset()` (`apps/testimonies/services/queries.py`) orders signal-category testimonies first (most recent within) for a user with real signal (follows/favorites/reactions), plain recency for a guest or signal-less user; `home_feed_page()` adds loop-back pagination on top -- once `page` reads past the real content, it wraps around a rotated starting point (`HOME_FEED_ROTATION_STEP`) instead of returning empty. Deliberately left un-authenticated (default auth classes + `AllowAny`, not overridden) so `request.user` is naturally the real user or `AnonymousUser`, avoiding the authentication-override bug this codebase has hit twice before (Phase 17/18). **Optimized per explicit request**: a page that still fits inside the real, un-looped content is a single indexed `COUNT` plus a DB-level `OFFSET`/`LIMIT` slice -- the full ordered-ID list and the O(total) rotation math only get materialized once a request actually reads past the end, not on every page. 8 new tests (6 direct selector tests in new `apps/testimonies/tests/test_services.py` covering ordering, signal-first-even-when-older, a small catalog still filling a full page, empty-catalog, and the loop-back property; 2 HTTP-contract tests). Full backend suite: 369 tests, same 21 pre-existing `apps.authn` baseline, no migration (read-only).
  - Mobile: `HomeFeedController` (new `home_feed_controller.dart`, append-only `Notifier` -- `loadMore()` appends a page rather than replacing the list, so already-built cards never get needlessly rebuilt while scrolling) backed by `Endpoints.testimonyHomeFeedPage(page)`. `testimonyFromApiPayload` (renamed from `_fromApiPayload` in `testimony_feed_controller.dart`, made public) is reused directly for mapping rather than writing a third near-duplicate mapper. `discover_screen.dart` rebuilt around `CustomScrollView` + `SliverList.builder` (lazy -- only visible/near-visible cards are ever built, critical for a feed that's infinite by design) with a scroll-position listener triggering `loadMore()` ~600px before the bottom. The old sectioned Trending/For You rails, their `SectionHeader`/"See All" entry points, and `_TestimoniesLoadingShimmer` are gone; the Inspirational Pictures rail and admin-curated `featured_testimonies` curation stayed out of this slice's scope and were left alone. `AppRouter.trendingVideos`/`trendingTexts`/`forYouFeed` and the `TestimonyListScreen`/`HomeTestimonyFeedList` widgets they exclusively reached are now unreachable dead code -- left in place rather than deleted, to keep this already-large slice scoped; noted here as a known follow-up cleanup, not forgotten.
  - **Card polish** (explicitly requested alongside this slice): `VideoCard` gained an opt-in `immersive` flag (default `false`, every existing rail caller -- favorites, search, category rails, testimony detail -- untouched) rendering the mockup's full-width `.v-card` treatment: rounded-20 radial-gradient card, a warm glow blob, bottom scrim, serif title, uppercase byline/category caption; only the continuous feed passes `immersive: true`. `TextStoryCard` gained the mockup's accent-line border (`purple40`/`darkGrey80`) and soft shadow universally (pure visual polish, safe everywhere it's already used). Live-testing one real inspirational picture surfaced a real content/overlay collision (documented in Slice 1's note) that doesn't apply here since video cards use real Cloudinary thumbnails, not admin-uploaded promotional graphics.
  - **Floating header** (part of this phase's own Build note, folded into this slice since it's the same screen-structure rework): the header/greeting/search/bell row moved into a `SliverAppBar(floating: true, snap: true, pinned: false)` -- Flutter's native mechanism for "hides on scroll down, snaps back on scroll up," chosen over a hand-rolled `AnimatedSlide` + manual scroll-direction tracking for correctness (automatic height reservation, no manual safe-area/overlay math, well-tested platform-idiom behavior) after the user flagged the previous plain-`ListView` header as "scrollable, that is wrong."
  - `flutter analyze` clean across the whole project. `test/widget_test.dart`'s existing smoke test updated (the old "Trending Video testimonies" section-header assertion no longer exists; now scrolls the `CustomScrollView` before asserting seed-feed content renders, since a lazy `SliverList` doesn't build cards pushed below the fold by the carousel/streak/pictures block above it -- a real, expected consequence of laziness, not a bug). Full `test/features/home` (24/24) and `test/features/browse` (52/55, the same 2 pre-existing `discovery_routes_test.dart` live-backend-flakiness failures reconfirmed against this session's already-documented baseline) both clean.
  - Live-verified end-to-end on the Android emulator: continuous mixed video/text feed scrolls correctly with real pagination pulling in new pages: immersive video cards render pixel-faithful to the mockup (gradient, glow, scrim, serif title, uppercase caption) against real Cloudinary thumbnails; text cards show the new accent-line border; the collapsed streak pill, carousel, and Inspirational Pictures rail all continue working unchanged; the app bar hides on scroll-down and snaps back on scroll-up mid-feed (not just at the very top), confirmed visually across multiple scroll gestures in both directions.
- **Slice 4 — Video plays automatically as it scrolls into view (mobile)** — Goal: Videos play themselves as a user scrolls past, without wasting their data. exactly one video plays at a time, muted, as it enters the viewport, and pauses the moment it leaves; defaults to autoplay on Wi-Fi and a tap-to-play thumbnail on cellular, with a Settings toggle to always autoplay
  - Implemented 2026-07-31. New packages: `visibility_detector` (knows which card is actually on-screen) and `connectivity_plus` (Wi-Fi vs cellular vs offline). `_ImmersiveVideoCard` (`home_discover_cards.dart`) converted to a `ConsumerStatefulWidget`: wraps itself in a `VisibilityDetector` that claims/releases a single shared `activeAutoplayVideoIdProvider` slot (new `video_autoplay_controller.dart`) once its visible fraction crosses 65% -- "last claimer wins" is sufficient to guarantee exactly one active id, since only one card can realistically cross that threshold at a time in a single-column vertical feed. Only the currently-active card ever initializes a real `VideoPlayerController` (lazy -- never pre-loads video data for off-screen cards); it's disposed the moment the card stops being eligible (loses the active slot, autoplay becomes disallowed, or the widget itself unmounts).
  - Connection-aware eligibility (new `video_autoplay_preferences.dart`): `NetworkConnectivityController` wraps `connectivity_plus`, defaulting conservatively to "don't autoplay yet" until the first real reading resolves (usually within one frame) rather than optimistically assuming Wi-Fi. `shouldAutoplayVideo()` is `true` on Wi-Fi always, `true` on cellular only if `VideoAutoplayPreferenceController` (a local, device-only preference via the existing `CacheService`/Hive -- a data-cost choice belongs to the device/plan, not the account, so no backend round-trip) says so, `false` when offline. New "Play videos on cellular" toggle added to the existing Display settings screen (`Profile > Display`), the most contextually relevant existing screen rather than a new menu entry.
  - UI matches the mockup: a "Playing" badge (top-left) and a mute toggle sit alongside the existing favorite badge (top-right) only while actually playing; a thin progress bar tracks real playback position. **Performance, per explicit request**: the progress bar's ~60fps position ticks are isolated in their own `AnimatedBuilder` listening directly to the `VideoPlayerController` (itself a `ChangeNotifier`) -- only that thin bar repaints per tick, not the whole card (network image, text, badges). The play/stop transition itself is deferred via `addPostFrameCallback` rather than triggered synchronously inside `build()`, since Flutter disallows `setState` while build is still running.
  - A real bug surfaced by the new widget test (not by live testing): `_onVisibilityChanged` read `ref` without a `mounted` guard, which throws if a visibility callback fires after a lazy `SliverList` has already disposed an off-screen card -- fixed by returning early when unmounted.
  - 9 new unit/widget tests (`video_autoplay_test.dart`: claim/release semantics, the preference controller's persistence via a real Hive box following the codebase's established `CacheService` test pattern, and `shouldAutoplayVideo`'s four connection/preference combinations via provider overrides) plus 4 new widget tests (`home_discover_cards_test.dart`: static fallback state, tap-to-open, the mute/favorite badges' independent tap targets, favorited state) -- real playback itself isn't exercised in `flutter test` (no video platform binding in that environment, same reason `TestimonyVideoPlayer` itself has no dedicated playback test), so that part relies on live-device verification instead. `flutter analyze` clean project-wide. Full `test/features/home` + `test/features/profile`: 93/95, the same 2 pre-existing `profile_flow_test.dart` live-backend-flakiness failures already documented earlier this session.
  - Live-verified end-to-end on the Android emulator after a full `flutter clean` + fresh install (required for the new native `connectivity_plus` plugin to link correctly): a video card autoplays muted the moment it's scrolled into view, shows the "Playing" badge and progress bar advancing in real time; tapping the mute icon unmutes without navigating to the detail screen (confirming the nested-InkWell tap-isolation pattern); navigating away and back correctly stops the old video and a different card autoplays once it's the one in view; the new "Play videos on cellular" toggle renders under Profile > Display, defaults off, and persists across a toggle.
  - **Type-scale refinement (2026-08-01, Home only).** Slices 1-4's sizes were pixel-matched to the desktop-rendered concept mockup; on an actual phone, most captions and body text in the 9-13px range read too small, while the handful of elements already at 14.5px+ (scripture verse, prayer text, video title overlay) read fine as-is. Raised everything below that floor across the carousel (kicker/quote/sub/CTA), streak card (header, day-dots, CTA, footnote, collapsed pill), picture cards, guest header subtitle, and discover cards. A first pass on the text-testimony card's body/byline/caption (13->14, 11->12.5, 9.3->11) was still visibly too subtle on-device; a second pass took the body text to 15px (deliberately half a point past the scripture-modal prayer text's own 14.5px, same secondary-color/height treatment, so it reads at least as clearly) and the byline/caption to 13.5/12.5. `flutter analyze` clean, `test/features/home` 37/37 passing, live-verified on the emulator across both passes with no overflow.
  - **Type-scale sweep to the rest of the app (2026-08-01).** Extended the same floor to every other reader-facing screen: testimony detail (written/video content, comment thread, comment sheet, video player lock/error states), search (hint text, result cards), favorites (cards), categories (list descriptions), notifications (message/timestamp), and profile (avatar header subtitle, journey card, logout dialog) -- established three tiers used consistently across all of them: primary body/summary text to 14.5-15px, bylines/names to 13-13.5px, captions/timestamps to 12-12.5px. Category's description was initially given the byline-tier bump (12->13.5) by a positional judgment call (it sits under a title like a subtitle) rather than a functional one; caught on review and corrected to the 15px body tier since it plays the same role as a testimony summary. Hit and resolved two verification snags: (1) a single-file rebuild after the category correction only took ~10s and silently kept serving the old 13.5px glyphs despite a fresh `install -r` and force-stop/relaunch -- pixel-measuring the rendered text (identical 33px glyph height across "different" builds) confirmed the APK itself was stale; a full `flutter clean` + rebuild + uninstall/reinstall fixed it, and is now the standing recommendation whenever a single-file follow-up rebuild finishes suspiciously fast. (2) `flutter test` across the full set surfaced 2 additional profile_flow_test.dart failures beyond the already-documented pre-existing pair (one text-finder failure, one 10-minute timeout) -- confirmed unrelated to this change (only numeric `fontSize` values were touched, no strings/widgets/imports in that flow's files) and left for a separate investigation. `flutter analyze` clean project-wide, `test/features/categories` 7/7 passing, live-verified on the emulator across Home, testimony detail, search, favorites, categories, notifications, and profile with no overflow.
- **Slice 5 — Reader sees a genuinely fresh, unpredictable mix on every open (backend + mobile)** — Goal: Home doesn't feel stale or repetitive across visits. a fresh app open or pull-to-refresh shows a different starting mix of testimonies rather than the exact same first post every time, and Inspirational Pictures appear woven directly into that same continuous feed instead of their own rail
  - Implemented 2026-08-01. Raised directly by the product owner watching the feed in practice: `_home_feed_base_queryset`'s ranking (signal-first, then recency) is fully deterministic, so a fresh open or pull-to-refresh showed the exact same first post every time -- the loop-back reshuffle only ever kicks in once pagination reads *past* the end of real content, not on a normal reload. Discussed backend-vs-mobile and what scales: mobile only ever holds one page at a time so client-side shuffling can't change which items land on page 1; a naive `ORDER BY RANDOM()` doesn't use an index and gets slower as the catalog grows. Landed on a seeded, bounded-window shuffle: `home_feed_page()` takes an optional `seed` (mobile mints one per feed session -- fresh on first load and on every pull-to-refresh, reused across that session's `loadMore()` pagination) and Fisher-Yates-shuffles only the first `HOME_FEED_SHUFFLE_WINDOW` (200) ranked ids via `random.Random(seed)`; the untouched remainder still reads in rank order. Cheap at this catalog's scale (only ids are fetched, no extra queries) and backward compatible (no seed = prior deterministic behavior, unchanged). Explicitly scoped as the first step of a documented migration path, not a final architecture: if traffic ever outgrows the per-session shuffle cost, the agreed next step is a precomputed `random_rank` column (indexed, keyset-paginated) with a periodic reshuffle job and a per-user rotation on top -- same `seed` param, same response shape, no breaking change. 6 new selector tests (`HomeFeedSeededShuffleTests`: same seed reproduces the same order, different seeds differ, the seeded order is a genuine shuffle and not secretly still a rotation, seeded pagination has no dupes/gaps within one loop, only the shuffle window gets shuffled and the rest stays ranked, no seed behaves exactly as before) plus 2 new API contract tests. Full backend suite passing. Mobile: `HomeFeedState` gained a `seed` field, minted via `Random().nextInt(0x7FFFFFFF)` (not security-sensitive, only needs enough variety to avoid session collisions) in `build()` and again in `refresh()`; `loadMore()` reuses `state.seed` unchanged. Live-verified end-to-end against the local backend first (caught and fixed two unrelated snags along the way: a missing migration on the local dev DB, and a stale cached auth token from an earlier build causing 403s), then confirmed the same behavior on production itself once Render's auto-deploy (on push to `main`, no manual trigger needed) picked up the change -- two different seeds against the live endpoint returned genuinely different orders.
  - **Inspirational Pictures woven into the feed.** Direct follow-up ask after reviewing the seeded-shuffle work: pictures should be part of the shuffled discovery experience, not a separate rail the reader has to explicitly "See All" into (mocked first via Artifact "Home Feed — Pictures Woven In" for review before building). Removed the "Inspirational pictures" `SectionHeader` rail entirely -- same "no more See All" call already made for the Trending/For You rails in Slice 3. `PictureCard` rewritten from a small rail item to the same full-width immersive treatment as video/text cards (photo, "INSPIRATION" badge, bottom title/caption overlay), reusing the shared `FavoriteBadge` rather than a third favorite-button style. New `home_feed_slots.dart` interleaves pictures into the testimony list -- one picture every `pictureFeedCadence` (6) testimonies, never as the very first card, cycling through the (typically small, curated) picture set with wraparound once exhausted, the same way looped-back testimonies already do; kept as its own file specifically so the interleaving math is directly unit-testable rather than only reachable through a full widget test. 8 new tests (2 widget tests for the rewritten card's real-image and fallback-gradient branches; 6 unit tests for the slot-interleaving: no-pictures passthrough, never-first, correct cadence position, cycling through multiple pictures, under-cadence count inserts nothing, testimony order/identity preserved). Backend untouched -- this is a mobile-only interleave over the existing separate pictures list, a deliberate scope choice since pictures are a small curated set (unlike testimonies, which needed the backend-side seeded-shuffle design to handle real scale). `flutter analyze` clean, full regression suite at the same pre-existing 2-failure baseline (both unrelated content/seed-data gaps, not touched by this change). Live-verified on production: picture cards render full-width in the mixed video/text/picture stream with no overflow, no rail, no "See All" anywhere in Home.
  - **Reviewed 2026-08-02, against AGENTS.md's query-discipline rules specifically -- 2 real backend gaps and 1 mobile gap found and fixed.** (1) The seeded id-list query ran through a queryset that already had `select_related()` attached for the unrelated no-seed fast path -- Django still emitted those joins even though only `id` was ever read off the result. The seeded branch now builds its own select_related-free queryset. (2) The docstring's "shuffle cost stays O(window) regardless of catalog size" claim was only true for the in-memory shuffle -- the id-list query itself re-ran on every `loadMore()` page, which is effectively all real traffic now that mobile always sends a seed. Added `_cached_base_ids`: caches the ordered id list per `(user, seed)` for 15 minutes (LocMemCache, Django's default -- per-process, so a best-effort hit rate on Render's multi-worker setup, not a guarantee; a miss just falls back to the original query, never wrong), so only the first page of a session pays that cost. Both `TestCase.setUp()`s that exercise seeds now call `cache.clear()` -- LocMemCache isn't tied to the transaction rollback TestCase gives each test, so a stale cached id list could otherwise leak across tests reusing a seed. 4 new `assertNumQueries` tests pin the actual query cost of both paths -- the tests that would have caught both issues before they shipped. (3) Mobile: `DiscoverScreen.build()` watches several unrelated providers (profile, notification count, favorites) in the same method, any of which changing re-ran the picture-interleaving loop over the whole feed for no reason since it was computed inline. New `homeFeedSlotsProvider` (`select()`-narrowed to just the testimony list and picture list) lets Riverpod skip recomputing it on unrelated rebuilds; 2 new tests confirm the actual caching behavior (same list instance back when nothing relevant changed), not just that `select()` was typed correctly. Architecturally the original implementation was already aligned with AGENTS.md (thin views, logic in the service layer, no forbidden practices) -- these were performance gaps, not correctness or architecture violations. Live-verified against production after deploy: same seed still reproduces the same page 1, pagination still doesn't repeat.
  - **Fixed a real UX gap in the same review pass (2026-08-02).** Tapping a picture card woven into the feed opened the full Inspirational Pictures grid regardless of which picture was tapped -- a leftover from when pictures only lived in their own rail, where "tap anywhere" sensibly meant "browse more." Now it opens that specific picture's own detail popup (preview, Download Image, Share Image), the same sheet already used when tapping a picture in the grid. That sheet was previously private to the grid screen's own State class; extracted into a standalone `showPictureActionsSheet(context, data)` in `inspirational_picture_sections.dart` so the grid and the feed card share one implementation instead of drifting into two. 1 new widget test confirms tapping the feed card opens the sheet rather than navigating away; the grid's own existing flow test still passes unchanged.
- **Slice 6 — Admin writes a "From iTestified" card from the dashboard (backend + admin dashboard)** — Goal: Admin can speak directly to users through a native-feeling promo card in the feed. an admin can create, schedule, edit, and activate/deactivate a native promo card (title, body, optional image and CTA) from a new dashboard screen
  - ✅ **Completed 2026-08-16.** Concept reviewed first via a published Artifact ("From iTestified Promo Cards") covering both the mobile card (image and text-only variants, cadence strip) and the dashboard screen, following this phase's own established pattern of reviewing a mockup before building (Slices 1 and 5). Three open calls proposed in that review and confirmed by the admin: image is optional (falls back to the gradient-and-quote treatment, same as a captionless Inspirational Picture); CTA destination is a small fixed menu (Giving screen / Submit a testimony / External URL) rather than free-text, ruling out a broken deep link by construction; admin list status is derived (Active/Scheduled/Ended/Inactive) from the date window plus the toggle rather than a separate field to keep in sync.
  - Backend: new `HomePromoCard` model (`apps.content`, alongside `InspirationalPicture` -- both are admin-authored home-feed content, same app boundary), mirroring `InspirationalPicture`'s admin pattern exactly (`SessionAuthentication`-only `ListCreateAPIView`/`RetrieveUpdateAPIView`, plus a `POST`=activate/`DELETE`=deactivate `ActivationView` matching `InspirationalPictureCategoryActivationView`'s own toggle shape). `computed_status()` is a model method, not a stored column, keyed off `is_active` + `starts_at`/`ends_at`; the admin list's `?status=` filter evaluates it in Python over the already-searched queryset rather than duplicating the window logic as a DB filter (this table is small by design, unlike testimonies/donations). CTA is validated as all-or-nothing (label + destination together or neither) with `cta_url` required only when the destination is `external_url`, enforced at both the model (`clean()`) and serializer level. New Cloudinary `home_promo_card` upload-signature resource type, reusing the exact direct-upload pattern already established for testimony/avatar/inspirational-picture media. 21 new backend tests (7 model, 14 API) covering auth, CTA/date validation, activation toggle, computed-status correctness across all four states, and status-filter/search on the admin list.
  - Dashboard: new `/home-promos` page (list + inline create/edit form + live preview pane), added to the primary nav. Deliberately a single-screen layout rather than Inspirational Pictures' separate list/upload-screen navigation, since the reviewed mockup showed the form always visible below the list; image upload reuses the same signed-direct-to-Cloudinary flow as Inspirational Pictures' `UploadScreen`. New API proxy routes (`list`, create, `[promoId]` edit, `[promoId]/activation`, `upload-signature`) follow the exact same JSON-passthrough/session-header pattern as every other admin proxy route in this app. 7 new component tests; `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint`, `next build`, and the full frontend suite (217 tests across 31 files) all clean.
  - Live-verified end-to-end on a local Postgres + real dashboard dev server, not just automated tests: logged in as a real admin through the dashboard's own login route, created two real promo cards (one with no CTA, one with a Giving CTA) through the real create proxy route, confirmed both render on the real list page, deactivated and reactivated one through the real activation route and confirmed the status-filtered list correctly picked it up in each state, edited a title through the real edit route, and confirmed the external-URL-without-a-URL validation correctly 400s end-to-end. All test data (temporary admin user, temporary promo cards) cleaned up afterward.
  - Deliberately out of scope for this slice: the public/mobile-facing "list active promo cards" read endpoint and the mobile feed-interleaving itself both belong to Slice 7, which depends on this slice -- building them now would be backend work with no client to wire it into yet, contrary to this plan's own Working Mode rules.
- **Slice 7 — Reader sees "From iTestified" cards woven into the feed (mobile)** — Goal: Admin's promo cards actually reach users, clearly labeled, without disrupting the feed. depends on Slices 3 and 6; active, in-window promo cards appear in the continuous feed at a fixed cadence, styled like a testimony card but clearly labeled, never as the very first card
  - ✅ **Completed 2026-08-16.**
  - Backend: new public `GET /content/home-feed/promos/` (`mobile_home_promo_cards_view`), deliberately its own dedicated endpoint rather than routed through `AdminHomeCurationView`'s featured-content model -- eligibility here is mechanical (`is_active` + inside the `starts_at`/`ends_at` window), not an admin hand-picking/ordering step, so it mirrors `mobile_home_carousel_view`'s own "automatic selection, no curation" shape instead. New `HomePromoCardPublicSerializer` exposes only what mobile needs (`id`/`title`/`body`/`image_url`/`cta_label`/`cta_destination`/`cta_url`) -- no admin-only fields (`is_active`, `updated_by_email`, raw `starts_at`/`ends_at`) leak to the public response. 6 new tests confirm scheduled/ended/inactive cards are excluded and no admin field leaks.
  - Mobile: `homePromoCardsProvider` (new `home_promo_cards_controller.dart`) mirrors `homeCarouselProvider`'s exact shape -- empty list on any failure or unregistered `ApiClient`, since this is house content (not user data), same reasoning that made the earlier comment-fallback bug wrong doesn't apply here. New `PromoCard` widget (`home_promo_card.dart`) reuses the immersive card family's exact chrome (rounded-20, radial-gradient-or-photo background, bottom scrim, serif italic title) with two new elements only: the "FROM ITESTIFIED" badge and the CTA pill -- unlike `PictureCard` (which shows either a photo+overlay or a centered fallback quote, never both), the badge and bottom overlay always render here regardless of whether an image is set, since the CTA is the point of the card and can never be the thing that silently disappears.
  - `home_feed_slots.dart` extended with a `PromoSlot` and its own `promoFeedCadence` (8, distinct from pictures' 6, per the reviewed "roughly every 8-10 testimonies" range) -- occasionally coinciding with a picture slot on the same testimony is fine (two special cards back-to-back, not a promo next to another promo); "never as the very first card" is guaranteed by construction the same way it already was for pictures (position starts at 1). New `promo_card_actions.dart` resolves a tapped CTA to the fixed three-destination menu confirmed in the concept review: `giving`/`submit_testimony` navigate in-app (`AppRouter.giving`/`AppRouter.addTestimony`), `external_url` opens the OS browser via the exact `Uri.tryParse -> canLaunchUrl -> launchUrl -> snackbar-on-failure` shape already established for WhatsApp sharing (`testimony_share.dart`).
  - 20 new mobile tests (5 provider, 4 widget, 11 interleaving -- including one confirming pictures and promos interleave independently without either dropping items). Updated `home_feed_slots_provider_test.dart`'s two existing caching tests to explicitly override `homePromoCardsProvider` to empty, since their shared mock `http.Client` answers every URL identically and would otherwise have had the new promo fetch spuriously map testimony JSON into bogus promo cards. `flutter analyze` clean project-wide; full `test/features/home` (66 tests) and `test/features/browse` (56 tests) suites, plus `test/widget_test.dart`, all pass with no regressions.
  - Live-verified the backend half for real: seeded one card in each of the four eligibility states (active/scheduled/ended/inactive) into a local Postgres and confirmed the public endpoint returns exactly the one genuinely-active card, with no admin-only fields in the response.
  - **On-device verification completed 2026-08-16** (an emulator became available after the above was written): ran the real app on an Android emulator (`API_BASE_URL` pointed at the local backend via `10.0.2.2`), seeded 16 real approved testimonies plus one photo-backed and one text-only promo card, and confirmed visually end-to-end -- both card variants render pixel-faithful to the reviewed mockup (badge, serif italic title wrapping correctly, body, CTA pill) woven into the real continuous feed at the correct cadence, including one case where a promo card landed immediately after an actively-autoplaying video card with no layout collision; loop-back reshuffle (Slice 5) continued working correctly once the small seeded set was exhausted, still interleaving promos correctly on reshuffled content. Tapped both in-app CTAs for real: `giving` opened the actual Giving screen, `submit_testimony` opened the actual Add Testimony screen; `external_url` was not tapped live (would open a real external browser) but reuses the identical, already-proven `url_launcher` code path as WhatsApp sharing. All seeded verification data (testimonies, promo cards, the throwaway author account) cleaned up afterward. Phase 20 is now fully verified, not just automated-test-verified.

Test:
- backend: the unified feed endpoint loops back through seen content once real unseen content is exhausted, without ever returning an empty page to an engaged user; the carousel endpoint returns only published inspirational pictures and testimonies with a non-empty `pull_quote`; a `HomePromoCard` outside its `starts_at`/`ends_at` window or with `is_active=False` never appears in the feed composition
- mobile: exactly one video plays at a time across the whole feed, never two simultaneously and never one left playing off-screen; autoplay behavior differs correctly between a Wi-Fi and a cellular connection in a controller test; the streak pill's collapsed/expanded state matches `readToday` and opens the verse sheet on tap; promo cards never appear as the first item and never back-to-back with each other
- dashboard: a scheduled promo card outside its date window doesn't appear as active in the admin list; deactivating a card is immediately reflected

Status: Completed 2026-08-16 -- drafted 2026-07-31 following the immersive-home concept review (Artifact: "iTestified — Immersive Home Concept"), all open decisions resolved in that same discussion. Slices 1-4 shipped 2026-07-31 (see their notes above), including the floating-header piece of this phase's own Build note (folded into Slice 3) and the video/text card polish requested alongside it; a Home-only type-scale floor refinement shipped 2026-08-01 (see Slice 4's own entry) after real-device testing showed the mockup-matched sizes read too small outside a desktop render; Slice 5 (seeded feed shuffle + Inspirational Pictures woven into the feed) shipped 2026-08-01, was built outside the normal mockup-reviewed slice process so got a dedicated review pass on 2026-08-02 against AGENTS.md's query-discipline rules (2 backend performance gaps + 1 mobile gap found and fixed, see Slice 5's own entry), and is live in production (Render auto-deploys `main`). Slices 6-7 (the promo-card system) both shipped 2026-08-16, reviewed first via a published concept Artifact per this phase's own established pattern (see Slice 6's own entry); Slice 7 was additionally confirmed on a real Android emulator the same day once one became available (see Slice 7's own entry). Known follow-up noted in Slice 3's own entry: `AppRouter.trendingVideos`/`trendingTexts`/`forYouFeed` and the screens they exclusively reached are now dead code, left in place rather than deleted to keep that slice scoped.

**Follow-up fix 2026-08-17**: the video testimony detail screen's "Related Videos" rail (`RelatedVideoCard`, `testimony_detail_primitives.dart`) predates this phase's card language entirely and was never touched by it -- plain 16:9 thumbnail with a bare play icon, plain sans title below at 13px, no accent border or shadow. Reviewed via a published mockup ("Transcript & Related Videos," covering this alongside Phase 22's mobile UI review below) before building -- approved as shown.
