Redesigned to reuse the immersive `VideoCard`'s established chrome (Slice 1's card-polish note) at rail scale: 4:5 thumbnail with a bottom scrim, serif title overlaid on the image instead of plain text below it, uppercase `"CATEGORY · TIMEAGO"` caption (same format as every other card in the app since Phase 19), accent border + soft shadow around the card. Deliberately left out of the redesign: autoplay and the glow-blob background, since those were purpose-built for the full-width feed context and would be excessive at rail scale; a functional bookmark/favorite badge, since `RelatedVideoCard` has never received favorite-state data or a toggle callback and wiring that is new plumbing beyond a visual redesign, not part of this fix. `relatedRailHeight` in `VideoTestimonyDetailContent` resized (162/180/210 -> 190/200/220) to fit the new taller thumbnail without clipping. `flutter analyze` clean; full `test/features/testimony_detail` suite (15 tests) passes.

### Phase 21: Premium Subscriptions & Billing Foundation

Background: proposed 2026-08-04 from the Christian Testimony Platform Business Blueprint (monetization/creator-economy vision, reviewed with the admin the same day). This phase is the foundation every other paid feature below (Phases 22, 24, 25, 26) depends on and must ship first.

Build:
- subscription tier model (`free`/`premium`) backed by Flutterwave's recurring "Payment Plans" API, extending the existing Phase 5 payment integration rather than introducing a new provider
- subscription state machine (`pending`, `active`, `past_due`, `canceled`, `expired`) driven exclusively by Flutterwave webhook events, never by client-reported status
- a single, reusable entitlement check that every later premium-gated phase depends on, rather than each feature re-implementing its own gate
- dashboard: admin visibility into subscriptions, mirroring the existing donations admin views
- all state-changing billing logic follows `backend/AGENTS.md` without exception: wrapped in `transaction.atomic()`, webhook handlers idempotent, business logic in services (never in views/serializers), explicit success- and failure-path tests -- given real money is involved, deviation from these rules is not acceptable here

Sub-slices:

#### Mobile User Flows
- **Slice 1 — Subscribe to premium** — Goal: A user can actually become a paying Premium subscriber. ✅ **Completed 2026-08-06, confirmed live on a real device** — new "Plans" screen (mobile) listing the premium tier and price, leading into the same Flutterwave checkout-webview flow Giving (Phase 5) already established rather than a second payment UI pattern; the backend creates a subscription in `pending` status and moves it to `active` via a synchronous verify call right after the checkout redirect, mirroring Donations exactly (not webhook-only, see the Status notes below for why)
- **Slice 2 — Subscription renews or fails** — Goal: A subscription keeps renewing automatically, and a failed payment doesn't cut a user off instantly. ✅ **Completed** (webhook-driven, backend-only, no direct UI, implemented as part of the same `apply_charge_callback`/`apply_cancellation_callback` commands built for Slice 1) — a failed renewal moves to `past_due` with a grace period before `expired`, never an instant hard cutoff. Mobile does need to *reflect* the resulting status wherever entitlement matters (e.g. a premium feature quietly re-locking if a renewal fails) — covered by Phase 21's shared entitlement check, not a screen of its own
- **Slice 3 — Manage my subscription** — Goal: A subscriber can see their plan status and cancel without losing what they already paid for. ✅ **Completed 2026-08-06, confirmed live on a real device** — new "My Subscription" screen (mobile), reachable from Profile alongside Change Password/Delete Account (Phase 14's existing settings pattern), showing plan, status, renewal date, and a Cancel action; cancellation takes effect at the end of the current paid period, never claws back time already paid for

- **Slice 6 — See my own plan status at a glance** — Goal: A user can tell at a glance whether they're Free or Premium, wherever it matters. added 2026-08-18, found via a plan/code audit of Slice 1-5: `mySubscriptionProvider` was already loaded and functional, but only ever read inside the "My Subscription" settings screen (buried in Profile) and Phase 22's transcript paywall gate (which locks content but never labels the viewer's *own* status) -- nowhere ambient showed a user at a glance whether they were Free or Premium.
  - ✅ **Profile header, completed 2026-08-18.** `ProfileAvatarHeader` (`lib/features/profile/presentation/widgets/profile_avatar_header.dart`) gained an `isPremium` flag: a small pill badge (crown icon + "Premium" label, same purple60-at-15%-opacity treatment as My Subscription's own "Active" status pill) next to the name in the compact header row. Free users show no badge at all -- absence already means Free, same convention the rest of the app uses. `ProfileScreen` converted from `ConsumerWidget` to `ConsumerStatefulWidget` to add the guarded `initState` load (`if (access == UserAccess.guest) return; if (mySubscriptionProvider.hasLoaded) return;`), the same pattern `TestimonyDetailScreen` already established for loading this same provider.
  - ✅ **Home header corner-crown badge, completed 2026-08-18.** Three placements (avatar ring, corner crown, inline pill) were mocked and reviewed against the app's real dark/light tokens before building (published as an interactive comparison artifact); corner crown was chosen over the inline pill used on Profile because it survives a real avatar photo better and doesn't compete with name-truncation on a row that already carries two icon buttons. `RegisteredHeader` (`lib/features/home/presentation/widgets/home_discover_headers.dart`) gained the same `isPremium` flag: a small gold circle (new `AppColors.premiumGold`, deliberately not purple60, so it reads as "special" against the app's own primary brand color rather than blending into it) with a `workspace_premium` glyph, positioned bottom-right of the 32px avatar with a border matching the screen background so it visually cuts into the avatar edge. `DiscoverScreen` gained the same guarded `initState` load as `ProfileScreen`/`TestimonyDetailScreen` (checked via `widget.access` directly, since it's already a constructor field here rather than read through `appFlowProvider`).
  - `flutter analyze` clean on every touched file. Full mobile suite run (419 tests): the only 2 failures are pre-existing and unrelated -- confirmed via `git stash` that both fail identically on the clean baseline (`app_flow_controller_test.dart`'s "shows registered home..." test makes a real network call to the non-existent `https://test.local` host and is environment-flaky regardless of any code here; `part6_flow_test.dart`'s one `visibility_detector` timer flake is the same one already tracked in this doc's Phase 20 follow-up notes).
  - Along the way, fixed a real test-infrastructure bug this change exposed rather than caused: `profile_screen_journey_test.dart` had to add `SubscriptionRepository` to its DI setup (via `configureDependencies`) once `ProfileScreen` started reading `mySubscriptionProvider`. An initial attempt called `configureDependencies(cacheDir: '.')` inside every test body (mirroring what looked like the same one-off pattern elsewhere) -- this hung the 2nd/3rd test in the file indefinitely: `Hive.openBox('app_cache')` blocked trying to reopen a box name still held open by the previous test's Hive instance, since nothing had closed it between tests. Fixed by calling `configureDependencies` once in `setUpAll` and only swapping the mocked `ApiClient` per test, the same shared-instance pattern `part6_flow_test.dart` already uses for this exact reason.

#### Admin Flows
- **Slice 4 — View and manage subscriptions** — Goal: Admin can see and manage every subscriber's status for support cases. ✅ **Completed 2026-08-07** — new "Subscriptions" page (dashboard), mirroring the existing Donations list/detail pattern (Phase 5): list, filter by status, search by subscriber email/name/reference, per-subscription detail with status history, manual override action for support cases (admin cancels a subscription with a required reason -- schedules the cancellation for active/past-due, cancels immediately for a still-pending one, mirroring the same non-destructive policy as self-cancel). No Figma design exists for this screen (added after the original handoff), so Donations was the deliberate reference point per this slice's own description rather than an invented layout. Skips Donations' amount/currency/date filter modal and hero-card/month picker, since the backend doesn't compute per-currency totals for subscriptions and building filter UI with no real backend support would be misleading.
- **Slice 5 — Admin sets subscription price** — Goal: Admin can change the Premium price going forward without disrupting existing subscribers. ✅ **Completed 2026-08-15** — dashboard control letting the admin set the Premium price per currency, replacing the old hardcoded `PREMIUM_PLAN_PRICING_MINOR_UNITS` Django setting. Not a simple field edit: a Flutterwave Payment Plan's amount is fixed at creation and can't be changed afterward, so setting a new price means creating a *new* plan on Flutterwave (via the existing `create_payment_plan` gateway method) and switching which plan `subscribe()` uses for new subscriptions going forward — existing subscribers stay on their original plan at their original price until they'd naturally re-subscribe, never repriced mid-subscription (same "never claw back what someone already agreed to" principle as cancellation in Slice 3). DB-backed via `PremiumPricing` (replacing the settings dict), so the admin's change persists and is visible in the dashboard; the old `FLUTTERWAVE_PREMIUM_PLAN_IDS` settings mapping and the `create_premium_payment_plan` management command's one-off manual flow are both superseded.
  - The backend (models/services/views, `apps.subscriptions`) already existed uncommitted in the working tree from earlier work but had zero tests and no dashboard wiring, per the 2026-08-15 audit. Both gaps closed this session: 13 new tests in `AdminPremiumPricingApiTests` (`apps/subscriptions/tests/test_api.py`) covering admin-role/session-only auth on both endpoints, currency/amount validation, 503 when the gateway isn't configured, 502 when Flutterwave rejects the plan, correct major/minor-unit conversion, creating vs. replacing a pricing row (with `PremiumPricingHistory` correctly recording `from_amount`/`to_amount`), and the core guarantee that changing a price never touches an existing subscriber's own captured amount/plan.
  - Along the way, found and fixed a real regression unrelated to this slice's own scope: the existing `SubscribeApiTests` (Phase 21 Slice 1) still patched `settings.FLUTTERWAVE_PREMIUM_PLAN_IDS`, a setting `subscribe()` stopped reading once Slice 5's `PremiumPricing` DB table took over pricing -- the patches had silently become no-ops, and 4 of those 11 tests were failing against whatever real Flutterwave plan ids happened to be seeded into the test DB by migration `0004_seed_premium_pricing_from_settings` from `config/settings/test.py`, not against anything the tests thought they controlled. Rewrote the whole class to seed explicit `PremiumPricing` rows per test instead (deterministic, decoupled from real env config); this also surfaced that "plan configured but blank" and "no pricing row at all" are now both classified as a 400 `SubscriptionUnsupportedCurrencyError` rather than the old 503 -- a real, reasonable behavior change from the DB-backed redesign that two tests were still asserting the old status code for, now corrected. Full `apps.subscriptions` suite (73 tests) passes.
  - Dashboard: new `/subscriptions/pricing` page (`dashboard/frontend`, App Router `(admin)/subscriptions/pricing`), reachable via a "Manage Pricing" link added to the main Subscriptions page header. No Figma design exists for this screen (confirmed via `dashboard/figma files/FIGMA_DASHBOARD_INVENTORY.md`/`_MODULES.md`, same as Subscriptions itself per Slice 4's own note), so it mirrors the App Version settings page's established layout/pattern (a read-only list of current rows + a plain server-form-POST-redirect below to change one) rather than the heavier paginated/filterable Subscriptions-list pattern, since pricing is a small, currency-keyed settings panel, not a growable collection. The admin types a normal major-unit amount (e.g. "3000" or "4.99"); the new `/api/admin/subscriptions/pricing` proxy route converts to minor units before calling the backend, matching the minor/major convention already used for display on the Subscriptions list. 4 new component tests (`premium-pricing-page.test.tsx`); `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint`, `next build`, and the full frontend suite (210 tests across 30 files, including the pre-existing `subscriptions-page.test.tsx`) all clean.
  - Live-verified end-to-end on a local Postgres + real (test-mode) Flutterwave key, not just automated tests: ran the actual Django dev server and Next.js dev server together, logged in as a real admin via both the backend's own `auth/admin/login/` and the dashboard's `api/auth/login`, and confirmed the full chain for real -- `GET /subscriptions/admin/pricing/` returns the real migration-seeded NGN/USD rows; invalid-currency and zero-amount POSTs correctly 400 without touching the database; a real `POST .../pricing/set/` for a throwaway test currency actually called Flutterwave's real API and got back a real `provider_plan_id`; and submitting the dashboard's own HTML form end-to-end (`/subscriptions/pricing` → `/api/admin/subscriptions/pricing` → backend → Flutterwave) produced the same real result with the correct major-to-minor amount conversion (`12.34` → `1234`) and correct `updated_by_email` attribution, redirecting to `?state=success` exactly as designed. All test artifacts (temporary admin users, temporary pricing rows) were cleaned up afterward; the real NGN/USD pricing rows were untouched throughout.

Test:
- webhook idempotency: a replayed webhook delivery must never double-renew or double-charge
- amount/currency correctness at every step, with an explicit regression test — this exact class of bug already broke Phase 5's donation flow once, undetected until a later review
- the entitlement check behaves correctly through every state transition, including the `past_due` grace period

Status: In progress -- Slice 1 (subscribe) and Slice 3 (manage/cancel) fully implemented end-to-end 2026-08-05, backend and mobile both wired, tested, committed, and pushed to `origin/main` on both repos. New `apps.subscriptions` app (`Subscription`/`SubscriptionStatusHistory`/`SubscriptionEventLog` models, a DB-level partial `UniqueConstraint` enforcing one non-terminal subscription per user, `subscribe`/`cancel_subscription`/webhook-driven `apply_charge_callback`/`apply_cancellation_callback` service commands, `is_user_premium`/`get_current_subscription` selectors for later phases to reuse, admin list/detail views mirroring Donations). `FlutterwaveGateway` relocated from `apps.donations` to `apps.common.services` since it's now genuinely shared, and extended with Payment Plans support. Flutterwave supports only one webhook URL per account, so donation and subscription webhook events both route through the existing `/api/v1/donations/provider/callback/` endpoint, dispatched internally by event type. Mobile: a redesigned Plans screen (Free vs. Premium comparison, NGN/USD currency switch) and a single state-aware "My Subscription" entry point in Profile, both wired to the real endpoints via a new `features/subscriptions` data/domain layer (Riverpod + repository/usecase pattern mirroring Giving), plus a `SubscriptionCheckoutScreen` reusing Giving's Flutterwave WebView pattern (polling `GET /mine/` after return instead of a `/verify/` call, since activation is webhook-only).

Verified 2026-08-05/06: 43 backend tests + 20 mobile tests (widget + controller level), all new and all passing, cover webhook idempotency, amount/currency conversion, every state transition (including the `past_due` grace period and the `cancel_at_period_end` scheduling below), the DB uniqueness constraint, and permission/auth boundaries. Full `apps.donations` + `apps.subscriptions` backend suites pass (73/73); a full backend regression run turned up no new failures outside the pre-existing, already-tracked `apps.authn`/`apps.users` Brevo-dependent test failures noted above (confirmed via `git stash` that they fail identically without any Phase 21 changes present). The full mobile suite (~385 tests) was also run end-to-end: the only failures (6-8, count varies run to run) are pre-existing and unrelated -- `responsive_release_gate_test.dart` and a handful of others call the live `itestified-backend.onrender.com` backend directly instead of a mock, so they're flaky by network dependency, not by anything touched here. None of the new subscriptions/Plans/My Subscription tests failed in any of the three full-suite runs used to check this.

Two real correctness bugs were caught and fixed before commit, both from the tests themselves or from re-reading this doc's own acceptance criteria against the code: (1) `subscribe()` originally wrapped its entire body in one `transaction.atomic()`, so a failed Flutterwave call rolled back the very "canceled, with a reason" record it was trying to persist, discarding the attempt instead of leaving it auditable -- fixed by giving each DB-writing step its own short atomic block. (2) `cancel_subscription()` and the `subscription.cancelled` webhook path both moved status straight to `canceled`/`expired`, immediately revoking entitlement -- directly contradicting this Slice's own requirement that cancellation "take effect at the end of the current paid period, never claw back time already paid for." Fixed by adding `Subscription.cancel_at_period_end`: status now stays `active`/`past_due` (entitlement intact) with the cancellation scheduled instead, computed lazily wherever "is this subscription current" matters (no scheduled job needed, since Flutterwave sends no further webhook for a subscription it has already cancelled); a lapsed scheduled cancellation is closed out to `canceled` lazily inside `subscribe()` so the DB's UniqueConstraint doesn't block a genuine re-subscription.

NGN/USD currency selection was added to Slice 1 on 2026-08-05 (raised mid-build: the business blueprint targets a global user base, and the original single-currency NGN design would have shown every non-Nigerian user a Naira price). Mirrors Giving's existing NGN/USD choice (Phase 5) rather than a new pattern: one Flutterwave Payment Plan per currency (`FLUTTERWAVE_PREMIUM_PLAN_IDS`, `manage.py create_premium_payment_plan --currency=NGN|USD`), `subscribe()` takes a `currency` argument, mobile's Plans screen gained a currency switch. The USD price ($4.99/month) is an illustrative placeholder pending a real pricing decision, same caveat already noted for the NGN figure. Broader multi-region work (more currencies, localized pricing tiers, tax/regulatory handling) stays out of scope here, per Phase 26's own sequencing.

Live-verified on-device 2026-08-06 (not just automated tests): logged into the real production backend on an emulator, confirmed My Subscription correctly renders the Free state from a real `GET /subscriptions/mine/` 404, confirmed the Plans screen's NGN/USD switch updates the displayed price correctly, and confirmed tapping Subscribe (in USD) sends the real request end to end -- `POST /subscriptions/` with `{"currency": "USD"}` -- and correctly surfaces the backend's exact 503 "Premium subscriptions are not configured yet." message in a SnackBar. That response is expected, not a bug: production has no real Flutterwave Payment Plan ids configured yet. **Deployment TODO before Premium can actually be subscribed to in production**: run `manage.py create_premium_payment_plan --currency=NGN` and `--currency=USD` against the live Flutterwave account, then set `FLUTTERWAVE_PREMIUM_PLAN_ID_NGN`/`FLUTTERWAVE_PREMIUM_PLAN_ID_USD` in Render's environment.

`current_period_end` accuracy improved 2026-08-06 (raised by the admin after a real test payment): it was computed as a flat `timedelta(days=30)` from the last successful charge, which drifts against a real calendar month by 1-3 days depending on the month. Checked Flutterwave's own docs first rather than guessing further: they don't document whether their "monthly" interval bills exactly 30 days later or on the same calendar date next month, and neither the payment plan nor the `GET /v3/subscriptions` response exposes a next-billing-date field to read back and cross-check against. Switched to real calendar-month arithmetic (`_add_one_month`, clamping to the last day of the target month for e.g. Jan 31 -> Feb 28/29) instead, matching how every processor we could confirm behavior for (Stripe, PayPal) actually bills "monthly" plans -- the closest available match to Flutterwave's real behavior given they don't document it. This is still an estimate, not a guarantee, since Flutterwave's exact scheduling isn't confirmed; the field remains informational (drives the mobile "Renews on" display) and re-derives itself from the real charge event on every renewal webhook, so drift can't compound across cycles even if a single cycle is off by a day or two. 5 new tests cover the month-rollover edge cases (mid-month, month-end clamping in leap/non-leap years, year rollover, time-of-day preservation).

Real end-to-end test payments completed 2026-08-06 (test-mode Flutterwave cards, on the live production backend, two separate accounts) and surfaced three real findings:

1. **A subscription could sit stuck in `pending` after a genuinely successful payment, with no visible indication anything was in progress** -- `MySubscriptionScreen` doesn't distinguish "no subscription" from "subscription exists but still pending" (still true, not fixed; noted as a follow-up below). Diagnosed the underlying cause properly rather than guessing: pulled the real transaction from Flutterwave's own `/v3/transactions` API (confirmed `status: "successful"`, real `tx_ref`), then manually POSTed that exact event to our webhook endpoint -- it processed correctly, proving the webhook *handler* itself was correct. Initially suspected a Flutterwave dashboard config gap (test-mode webhook URL not registered); the admin corrected this same day -- Donations' webhook already works reliably in the same test-mode setup, so the account-level webhook delivery mechanism was never the problem. Re-diagnosed properly: Flutterwave's own docs describe webhooks as being for payment methods "not processed in real-time" (e.g. bank transfers) -- for a real-time card charge, the reliable confirmation path is a synchronous verify call right after the checkout redirect, which is exactly why Donations has never had this problem (it already calls `POST /donations/verify/`). Subscriptions never got that same fallback. **Fixed same day**: added `POST /subscriptions/verify/` (mirrors `verify_donation()` exactly, idempotent against a webhook winning the race) and rewired `SubscriptionCheckoutScreen` to call it synchronously instead of polling `GET /subscriptions/mine/`. Webhooks remain authoritative for renewals/cancellations, since there's no "return to app" moment for those to hook a verify call into.
2. **USD price displayed wrong on My Subscription**: `formattedAmount` rounded to a whole dollar (charged $4.99, displayed "$5"), inconsistent with the exact price shown on the Plans screen. Fixed same day -- USD now always shows cents, NGN stays whole.
3. **A second live test payment exposed a different gap**: the checkout WebView's Chromium renderer crashed mid-payment (confirmed in device logs: "Renderer process crash detected"). The payment had genuinely succeeded on Flutterwave's side (confirmed via their API, same diagnostic approach as #1), but the app had no way to find that out -- the crash happened before the redirect could be detected, so `_handlePaymentReturn` (and the new verify call) never ran, leaving the screen frozen with no recovery path. The `webview_flutter_android` version pinned in this project doesn't expose a render-process-crash callback to catch this specific failure. **Fixed same day** with a more general-purpose fix instead of chasing that one failure mode: an "Already paid?" action in the checkout screen's AppBar, available any time the WebView stage is showing, that checks the real subscription status directly rather than depending on the redirect ever firing -- covers this crash and any other reason the automatic detection might fail.
4. **The fixes above went un-tested for several attempts due to a stale build, not a code bug** -- multiple `flutter run` restarts on the test device kept silently serving an old build (still showing the pre-verify polling-based success copy, "may take a few moments to reflect everywhere in the app", and missing the new "Already paid?" action and debug logs entirely), despite the source files on disk being correct each time. Confirmed via a suspiciously-fast ~24s Gradle rebuild (this project's established signal that a change wasn't actually picked up, per earlier sessions) versus a genuine ~166s rebuild after `flutter clean` + full uninstall/reinstall. Once the app was verifiably running current code, a real end-to-end test on a fresh account (never previously subscribed, avoiding the DB uniqueness constraint) confirmed the entire chain works correctly: real checkout redirect -> `[SubscriptionCheckout]` debug log showing the exact parsed `transaction_id` from Flutterwave's own query param -> `POST /subscriptions/verify/` -> `SubscriptionStatus.active` -> My Subscription correctly showing Premium details. This is genuine, observed, non-mocked confirmation, not an inference from logs or a webhook replay.

Environment gotcha worth remembering for future device testing on this project: a suspiciously fast Flutter/Gradle debug rebuild (much faster than the usual ~150-250s) is a real signal the build didn't pick up recent changes, not just a good caching day -- confirm with a full `flutter clean` + reinstall before trusting on-device behavior contradicts the source code.

Known follow-up, not fixed yet: `GivingCheckoutScreen` (donations) has the exact same two gaps as subscriptions did (no visible "payment pending" state, no recovery path from a WebView crash) -- not touched here since it was out of scope for this pass, but worth applying the same fixes there.

All 6 slices complete as of 2026-08-18 (see Slice 5/6's own bullets above for that work); Slice 2 (renewal) was implemented as part of the same webhook-driven commands built for Slice 1, with no separate work needed. Phase 21 is done.

Corrected 2026-08-15 (found in a plan/code consistency audit): `AdminPremiumPricingListView`/`AdminSetPremiumPriceView` (`apps/subscriptions/api/views.py`) and `set_premium_price()` (`apps/subscriptions/services/commands.py`) already exist as full, non-stub implementations -- this line previously said Slice 5 was "not yet started," which was wrong about the code inventory. It remains functionally unusable, though: no test in `apps/subscriptions/tests/test_api.py` covers `PremiumPricing`/`set_premium_price`, and no reference to it exists anywhere in `dashboard/frontend/src` -- so the dashboard control it's meant to power was never actually built. Remaining work for this slice: add backend tests for the pricing endpoints/service, then build and wire the dashboard control itself.

### Phase 22: AI Transcription & Translation

Background: proposed 2026-08-04, same blueprint review as Phase 21.

**Groundwork review 2026-08-16** (before any implementation started): checked this phase against the actual codebase and found it wasn't build-ready as written — no async task queue exists anywhere (no Celery/Redis/RQ, only plain synchronous management commands on a Render cron schedule), no AI vendor is integrated or provisioned, and no "upload completed" hook exists to trigger a job from (testimony video upload is direct-to-Cloudinary from the client; the backend never observes it finishing). Three open decisions resolved the same day:
- **Gating: both transcription and translation are premium-gated**, matching `BUSINESS_BLUEPRINT.md`'s Premium Plan list literally (both bullets appear there, not just translation). Decided over the alternative of leaving transcription free for accessibility/SEO reasons because: (1) it's the explicit, already-reviewed-with-the-admin written product decision, not something to quietly deviate from without a strong reason; (2) transcription and translation share one technical pipeline, so gating them identically keeps the entitlement check to one place instead of per-sub-feature logic. Revisitable later as a deliberate exception if accessibility concerns become pressing, but not assumed now.
- **Async approach (revised 2026-08-17): Celery + Redis**, not DB-polling cron. Original 2026-08-16 call was DB-polling specifically to avoid provisioning new infrastructure; revisited on the admin's explicit ask for the best-practice answer at scale rather than the cheapest one to ship first. DB-polling's known ceilings that matter for this app's own stated ambition (global platform, "world's largest searchable archive," plus Phase 27 live broadcasts and Phase 24 referral payouts still to come): polling interval imposes a latency floor with no backpressure under a burst (e.g. a promo push), and safe concurrent workers require hand-rolled `select_for_update(skip_locked=True)` row locking that Celery's broker handles natively. Celery also gives per-task retry/backoff and OpenAI rate-limiting for free instead of reimplementing a subset of it in the job model. Infrastructure now stood up in code: `config/celery.py` (Django-integrated Celery app, autodiscovers `tasks.py` per app), `CELERY_*`/`REDIS_URL` settings in `config/settings/base.py`, `celery`/`redis` added to `requirements/base.txt`, a dedicated `itestified-celery-worker` service plus a managed `itestified-redis` instance added to `render.yaml` (worker kept separate from the web dynos so a slow Whisper/GPT call never blocks a request-handling gunicorn worker), and `config/settings/test.py` sets `CELERY_TASK_ALWAYS_EAGER` so the test suite needs no live broker. The `TranscriptionJob`/`TranslationJob` models themselves (status, retry count) are unchanged by this swap — they still exist and still back Slice 4's dashboard retry/failure view, they're just driven by Celery tasks instead of a polling command. The existing cron-based commands (`publish_scheduled_testimonies` etc.) are untouched — this migration is scoped to Phase 22 and any future async work, not a rewrite of already-working scheduled jobs.
- **Vendor: OpenAI** for both — Whisper API for transcription, a GPT-class model (prompted) for translation. One vendor, one API key, simplest billing/ops story over provisioning a second account with DeepL.
- **Upload trigger**: since testimony creation already happens *after* the client's direct Cloudinary upload completes (the client already has `video_url`/`audio_url` in hand when it calls the backend to create the testimony record), no Cloudinary webhook is needed — the job can simply be created synchronously (via `transaction.on_commit`, matching the existing pattern in `apps/donations/services/commands.py` and `apps/notifications/services.py`) inside the testimony-creation service command whenever a video or audio testimony is submitted.
- **Real blocking dependency found**: this phase's own Build note already assumed audio testimonies exist ("video/audio testimonies... triggered on upload") — they don't (see Phase 28). Transcription can ship for video testimonies alone first; the audio half of Slice 1 (backend) depends on Phase 28 shipping first.

Build:
- new `TranscriptionJob`/`TranslationJob` models + Celery tasks that process them (see groundwork review above for the Celery/Redis infrastructure, already stood up)
- async transcription pipeline for video testimonies now, audio testimonies once Phase 28 ships (Whisper API), triggered via `transaction.on_commit` at testimony-creation time enqueuing a Celery task, not a webhook and not a polling command
- on-demand translation of testimony text/transcripts into a configurable language set (GPT-class model, prompted), cached per (testimony, language) rather than regenerated per request
- both transcription and translation gated behind Phase 21's entitlement check (resolved above)
- dashboard: visibility into transcription/translation job status and failures, since this is an async pipeline where a silent failure would strand a testimony indefinitely

**Restructured 2026-08-17**: the original Slice 1/2 (labeled "Mobile User Flows") can't actually be built mobile-only -- there is zero transcript/translation data anywhere in the system today, so a mobile screen reading one has nothing real to wire into until the backend piece exists. Split following the same backend-then-client sequencing Phase 20 Slice 6-&gt;7 already used successfully (Slice 6 shipped backend+dashboard alone and tested; Slice 7 was mobile, explicitly depending on 6, built after) rather than trying to build both layers of one slice at once.

Sub-slices:

#### Backend Flows
- **Slice 1 — Transcription job runs on upload, result readable via API** — Goal: A newly uploaded video testimony automatically gets a readable transcript, gated to Premium. a new video testimony triggers an async transcription job automatically at creation time (`transaction.on_commit`, per the groundwork review above); once done, the transcript is readable through the testimony detail API, gated to premium viewers. Audio testimonies join once Phase 28 ships. Ships and is tested standalone, no mobile UI yet -- Slice 3 depends on this.
  - ✅ **Completed 2026-08-17.** `TranscriptionJob` (`apps/testimonies/models.py`, one row per testimony, `pending`/`processing`/`done`/`failed`) created via `enqueue_transcription_job` (`services/commands.py`), called from both `AdminVideoTestimonyUploadView` and `AdminVideoTestimonyCreateFromUrlView` right after `serializer.save()` -- a written testimony or a repeat call is a safe no-op. The Celery task (`apps/testimonies/tasks.py:run_transcription_job`) calls a new boundary function, `apps/common/services/ai_text.py:transcribe_video` (OpenAI Whisper, the only place this codebase talks to OpenAI for transcription) -- fails closed (job marked `failed` with a clear message, never an unhandled crash) exactly like `FIREBASE_CREDENTIALS_JSON`'s existing pattern when `OPENAI_API_KEY` isn't set. `TestimonyDetailSerializer` gained `transcript_status` (always visible, metadata not content) and `transcript` (`null` unless the job is `done` *and* the viewer is premium via `apps.subscriptions.selectors.is_user_premium` -- reused as-is, exactly the shared entitlement check it was built for). Known, documented gap: Whisper's 25MB file-size limit isn't worked around (no audio-extraction/compression step) -- a longer video fails closed with a clear error rather than silently truncating, but isn't solved here.
  - 22 new backend tests (`apps/testimonies/tests/test_transcription.py`): enqueue idempotency and written-testimony no-op, success/failure task paths, serializer field gating (written/pending/done-for-premium/done-hidden-from-free/hidden-from-guest), and the admin upload/create-from-url endpoints actually triggering a job. `captureOnCommitCallbacks(execute=True)` used throughout (matching `apps/notifications/tests/test_push_notifications.py`'s existing precedent) so tests exercise the real service -> Celery -> task -> AI-boundary -> DB pipeline end-to-end under `CELERY_TASK_ALWAYS_EAGER`, mocking only `ai_text`'s boundary functions -- never Celery itself. Full backend suite: 519 tests, same pre-existing 21-failure `apps.authn` baseline (confirmed identical against a clean stash), nothing new broken.
  - Live-verified for real, not just eager-mode tests: started an actual Celery worker (`celery -A config worker`) against local Postgres + Redis, confirmed it discovers both new tasks on boot, then created a real video testimony through the ORM and called `enqueue_transcription_job` directly -- the job was dispatched over the real Redis broker, picked up by the separate worker process moments later (genuine async decoupling, not synchronous), and correctly failed closed with `"Missing required environment variable: OPENAI_API_KEY"` (no local key configured, matching production's current state). Worker and throwaway data cleaned up afterward.
- **Slice 2 — Translation generated on request and cached** — Goal: A Premium user can request a testimony in another language without regenerating it every time. a premium user requesting a language creates (or reuses, if already generated) a `TranslationJob` for that `(testimony, language)` pair; async via the same Celery pipeline, result cached and never regenerated on repeat requests. Ships and is tested standalone -- Slice 4 depends on this.
  - ✅ **Completed 2026-08-17**, alongside Slice 1. `TranslationJob` (one row per `(testimony, language)`, unique constraint) created via `request_testimony_translation`, which raises `TestimonyTranslationNotReadyError` (-> 400) if the testimony has no `done` transcript yet -- translation's source text is always the transcript, never the raw video, so Slice 2 hard-depends on Slice 1 per-testimony, not just per-phase. A `done` job is returned as-is with no re-enqueue (the caching behavior); a `failed` job is retried on the next request. New endpoint `POST /testimonies/<id>/translations/` (`TestimonyTranslationView`, `TokenAuthentication`/`IsAuthenticated`, premium-gated via `is_user_premium` -> 403) is deliberately idempotent and reused for both the initial request *and* polling -- no separate GET endpoint, since a repeat POST on an already-`done` job just returns it. Translation itself goes through the same `ai_text.py` boundary (`translate_text`, GPT-class model), same fails-closed behavior.
  - Included in the same 22-test file and the same live worker verification above.

#### Mobile User Flows
- **Slice 3 — Reader reads a transcript** — Goal: A reader can read along with a video/audio testimony once its transcript is ready. depends on Slice 1. Transcript card appears on the video/audio detail screen once the async job completes.
  - **UI reviewed 2026-08-17** via a published mockup ("Transcript & Related Videos," alongside the Related Videos redesign in Phase 20's own follow-up note) before building. Placement: a new card on the video/audio detail screen, after the engagement/reactions row and before the comments teaser -- deliberately not directly under the player, so it reads as "go deeper" content rather than competing with the primary watch/react actions. Defaults collapsed (header + language row only, body hidden until tapped), matching the existing "Comments" section's own teaser-then-expand pattern on the same screen rather than introducing a second pattern. Three states designed: ready (collapsed by default, expands to header + language chips + transcript body + a "generated automatically" caption), still-processing (a pulsing indicator + "Generating transcript & translations..." -- the async job can take real time), and locked-for-free-plan (dimmed placeholder text behind a lock icon + upgrade prompt, matching the gating decision above).
  - ✅ **Completed 2026-08-17.** `TranscriptCard` (`lib/features/testimony_detail/presentation/widgets/transcript_card.dart`) renders all three reviewed states; renders nothing at all for a written testimony, a legacy pre-Phase-22 video (`transcript_status == not_available`), or a `failed` job -- none were part of the reviewed design, so left as a clean no-render rather than inventing an undesigned state. Premium gating is presentation-layer here (`mySubscriptionProvider`, loaded once per screen visit in `TestimonyDetailScreen.initState`, matching `MySubscriptionScreen`'s own pattern) *in addition to* Slice 1's entitlement-layer gate -- a non-premium viewer always sees the locked card regardless of `transcript_status`, never a flash of real content. `Testimony` entity gained `transcriptStatus`/`transcript` (detail-only, same `pullQuote` precedent). Real bug caught and fixed along the way: the header's `GestureDetector` used the default `deferToChild` hit-test behavior, so tapping the empty gap `spaceBetween` leaves between the label and the chevron silently did nothing for a real user, not just in tests -- fixed with `HitTestBehavior.opaque`.
- **Slice 4 — Reader translates a testimony** — Goal: A reader can read a testimony in a language of their choosing. depends on Slice 2. User selects a language and sees translated text.
  - **UI reviewed 2026-08-17**, same mockup as Slice 3. Language selection is a horizontal chip row inside the transcript card (Original + target languages), selected chip filled, tapping an uncached language shows a brief per-chip loading state while it generates rather than blocking the whole card. Initial language set proposed in the mockup: Yorùbá, Igbo, Hausa, Français, Kiswahili -- matches the blueprint's own Nigeria -> Africa -> UK/US rollout order; final list is a product call to confirm, not a technical constraint.
  - ✅ **Completed 2026-08-17.** `TranscriptController` (`lib/features/testimony_detail/presentation/state/transcript_controller.dart`, family-keyed per testimony id) selects a language, and for a non-original one calls the idempotent `POST /translations/` endpoint in a short in-memory poll loop (pending/processing -> re-call after a delay, capped at 8 attempts) until it settles `done`/`failed` -- no separate polling endpoint needed, matching Slice 2's own design. A `done` result is cached in the controller's own state; re-selecting an already-done language makes no further calls. `pollDelay` is an overridable instance field specifically so tests don't spend real wall-clock seconds waiting on it.
  - 14 new mobile tests total: 6 controller tests (original-language no-call, request-and-store, poll-until-done-then-cached, failure handling, per-testimony independence, expand toggle) and 8 widget tests (each hidden case, locked-regardless-of-status, both processing states, collapsed-by-default, expand-reveals-content). `flutter analyze` clean project-wide; `part6_flow_test.dart` (which actually mounts the real video detail screen) confirmed unaffected -- its one failure is the same pre-existing `visibility_detector` timer flake already documented earlier this session, reconfirmed identical against a clean stash.
#### Admin Flows
- **Slice 5 — Admin sees job failures** — Goal: Admin can catch and retry a stuck transcription/translation job instead of it failing silently. a failed transcription/translation job is visible in the dashboard with a retry action, never silently stuck in "processing"
  - ✅ **Completed 2026-08-17.** Backend: `retry_transcription_job`/`retry_translation_job` (`apps/testimonies/services/commands.py`) reset a `FAILED` job to `PENDING` and re-enqueue via the same `transaction.on_commit` pattern as the original enqueue -- refuses (raises `AIJobNotRetryableError` -> 400) for any job not currently `FAILED`, since pending/processing is already in flight and done is already correct. New `AdminTranscriptionJobListView`/`AdminTranslationJobListView` (`?status=` filter, paginated) and `...RetryView` endpoints (`SessionAuthentication`/`IsActiveAdmin`, same pattern as `AdminDonationListView`/`AdminDonationReverseView`) at `admin/testimonies/{transcription,translation}-jobs/` and `.../<id>/retry/`. 16 new backend tests (`apps/testimonies/tests/test_ai_job_admin.py`): retry command success/refusal/missing-job for both job types, list filtering, retry API auth/success/400/404. Full `apps.testimonies` suite (154 tests) and full backend suite (535 tests) pass, same pre-existing 21-failure `apps.authn` baseline as every prior phase.
  - Dashboard: new `/ai-jobs` page (`dashboard/frontend/src/app/(admin)/ai-jobs/`, added to the sidebar nav) lists both job types merged into one table sorted by most-recently-updated, with status-filter chips (All/Failed/Processing/Pending/Done) and a plain HTML `<form method="post">` Retry button per failed row -- deliberately no client JS state, matching the existing subscriptions-cancel/donations-reverse precedent of a server-rendered form posting to a Next.js route handler that proxies to the backend and 303-redirects back. Kept intentionally simpler than Donations/Subscriptions (no per-row detail modal, no pagination UI) since this is a low-volume internal ops queue, not a customer-facing list. New `get-ai-jobs-view-model.test.ts` (3 tests: merge+sort, error-if-either-backend-call-fails, empty state); full frontend suite (223 tests) and `eslint .`/`tsc --noEmit` pass with no regressions.
  - Live-verified against the real local stack (Postgres/Redis/Celery worker, not eager mode): logged into the dashboard as a real admin via a real session cookie (not `E2E_BYPASS_AUTH`), loaded `/ai-jobs`, confirmed it rendered the real failed translation jobs already sitting in the local DB (each failing closed on the same missing `OPENAI_API_KEY`, consistent with Slice 1/2's own live verification) with working status-filter links, then submitted a real Retry -- confirmed via the Celery worker's own log that a genuine new task was dispatched over Redis and picked up by the separate worker process (`run_translation_job[...] received` -> re-failed closed on the same missing key), proving the retry path is real async dispatch, not a UI-only state flip.

Test:
- a failed job produces a visible, retryable failed state, never an indefinite "processing" status -- implemented and passing at every layer (Slice 1/2 backend jobs, Slice 5 dashboard visibility + retry)
- a repeated translation request for the same (testimony, language) pair reuses the cached result rather than re-calling the paid API -- implemented and passing (Slice 2 backend and Slice 4 mobile both cache independently)
- a non-premium user's testimony still gets queued but never surfaces transcript/translation content in the API response regardless of job outcome, confirmed for both an authenticated free user and a guest -- implemented and passing at both the entitlement layer (Slice 1) and mobile's presentation layer (Slice 3), which never shows real content to a non-premium viewer regardless of job status

Status: Slice 1-5 ✅ completed 2026-08-17 -- see each slice's own note above for what shipped, tests, and verification. Audio's half of Slice 1 blocks on Phase 28. A real `OPENAI_API_KEY` still isn't configured anywhere (local or production) -- the full pipeline, including admin retry, is proven end-to-end against a real Celery worker/Redis/Postgres and fails closed correctly without one, but no actual transcript has ever been generated for real. On Render specifically, the `itestified-redis`/`itestified-celery-worker` services declared in `render.yaml` still need a manual Blueprint sync before this phase does anything at all in production -- pushing the YAML alone does not provision them.

### Phase 23: Creator & Ministry Profiles

Background: proposed 2026-08-04, same blueprint review as Phase 21. Re-scoped 2026-08-18 after a design discussion resolved three open questions the original proposal had left implicit:

- **A Ministry/Creator profile requires an active Premium subscription, full stop, regardless of content type.** Phases 28/32 already premium-gate self-service video/audio upload (the admin's own words in Phase 32's background: *"only premium users can upload"*); written-testimony submission (`submit/written/`) stays Free-tier for ordinary users, but running a Ministry page is a distinct, stricter capability from submitting one testimony, so it doesn't inherit that exception. Since Premium is a strict superset of Free, this needs no per-content-type special-casing. Losing Premium never deletes the profile, its testimonies, or its followers (same "never claw back what's already there" rule as Phase 21 cancellation and Phase 32's own gating) — it only blocks new Ministry-specific actions (profile edits, new testimonies) until resubscribed.
- **Admin verification layers on top of the Premium gate, it doesn't replace it.** Premium answers "is this account allowed to operate as a creator"; verification answers "we confirmed this identity is real" (preventing e.g. someone subscribing and impersonating a well-known ministry). A Ministry profile is fully functional — followable, has stats, receives prayer reactions — before an admin ever reviews it; the verified badge is additive, never a gate on the rest of this phase.
- **No moderation shortcut for anyone.** Testimonies from a verified Ministry go through the exact same Phase 4 moderation queue as every other testimony — verification is an identity signal, not a content-trust signal, and mixing the two would undermine the moderation integrity every other phase has been careful about.

Reviewed against a 4-screen mockup (setup → public profile → analytics → prayer inbox, published as an interactive artifact) before writing the slices below.

Build:
- **new `apps/creators` app** — a real, well-defined domain (identity + follower graph + analytics + prayer response), not bolted onto `apps/users` or `apps/testimonies`, matching how `apps/subscriptions` got its own app in Phase 21 rather than living inside `apps/donations`
- `CreatorProfile` — one row per Ministry/Creator account, `OneToOneField` to `User` (not `Profile`) to stay consistent with `Testimony.author` already being a `User` FK: `display_name` (required, defaults to the user's `Profile.full_name` at creation but independently editable — e.g. "Grace Restoration Ministries" vs. a personal name), `bio` (blank-ok text), `is_verified` + `verified_at` + `verified_by` (FK to the admin `User`, audit trail per `backend/AGENTS.md`'s admin-action rule), `created_at`/`updated_at`. Creation is entitlement-checked in the service layer via the existing `apps.subscriptions.selectors.is_user_premium` (same selector Phase 22 already reuses) — not a DB constraint, since premium status changes over time and a schema constraint can't reference another table's live state
- `CreatorFollow` — mirrors `UserFollowedCategory`'s existing shape exactly (Phase 16 precedent for a simple follow join table): `follower` FK `User`, `creator` FK `User`, `unique_together`, `created_at`; a `CheckConstraint` blocks `follower == creator`
- `PrayerResponse` — one row per response, FK to the specific `TestimonyReaction` being responded to (not to testimony+user loosely, so "already responded to this exact reaction" is a real, checkable state), `response_text`, `created_at`; triggers a synchronous notification via a new `apps/notifications/services.py::notify_prayer_response`, same pattern as every other notification in that file — no Celery needed here, this isn't AI-latency-bound like Phase 22
  - **Scoped to `praying_for_you` reactions specifically**, not `amen`/`gives_me_hope` — those two are passive acknowledgments with no implied expectation of a personal reply; only "praying for you" carries that expectation, so only it gets an inbox+response flow
- **Follower notifications are batched, not per-testimony** — genuinely new infrastructure (nothing in `apps/notifications` batches today; every existing `notify_*` function fires synchronously per-event). A new Celery beat task (`apps/creators/tasks.py`, reusing Phase 22's already-stood-up Celery/Redis) runs daily, and for each `CreatorProfile` with testimonies approved in the prior 24h, sends one digest push per follower listing what's new — no new tracking field needed since the task's own fixed schedule defines the window
- creator analytics is a pure read-side aggregation (`apps/creators/selectors.py::get_creator_analytics`) over `Testimony.view_count` and `TestimonyReaction` already stored per-testimony (Phase 3, Phase 15) — no new data collection, no new write path

Sub-slices, backend first (mobile has nothing to build against until these exist):

#### Backend Flows
- **Slice 1 — Premium subscriber creates/manages a Ministry profile** — Goal: A Premium subscriber can establish a public Ministry identity on the platform. ✅ **Completed 2026-08-18.** New `apps.creators` app (own domain, mirrors `apps.subscriptions` getting its own app in Phase 21 rather than living inside `apps.donations`). `CreatorProfile` (`OneToOneField` to `User`, `display_name`/`bio`/`is_verified`/`verified_at`/`verified_by`) with `create_creator_profile`/`update_creator_profile` in `services/commands.py`, both entitlement-checked via the existing `apps.subscriptions.selectors.is_user_premium` -- a lapsed-Premium user keeps their existing profile fully intact but can't edit it until resubscribed. `POST`/`PATCH`/`GET /api/v1/creators/me/`, `SessionAuthentication`-free (`TokenAuthentication`, mobile-only). 21 tests (`test_services.py`, `test_models.py`, `test_api.py` combined for Slices 1 and 3).
- **Slice 2 — Follow a creator/ministry** — Goal: A user can follow a Ministry and get notified in batches when it posts new testimonies. ✅ **Completed 2026-08-18.** `CreatorFollow` mirrors `UserFollowedCategory`'s shape exactly (Phase 16 precedent), with a `UniqueConstraint` and a `CheckConstraint` blocking self-follow at the DB level, not just in the service layer. `follow_creator`/`unfollow_creator` are both idempotent (repeat calls reuse/no-op rather than erroring). `POST`/`DELETE /api/v1/creators/<user_id>/follow/` mirrors `CategoryFollowToggleView`'s exact toggle shape. `GET /api/v1/creators/<user_id>/` (public profile) returns `follower_count`/`is_following`, works regardless of verified status. Digest batching: new `apps.creators.tasks.send_creator_follower_digests` (Celery task) + `apps.notifications.services.notify_creator_digest` (`NotificationType.CREATOR_DIGEST`) send exactly one notification per follower per creator per run, never one per testimony -- caught and fixed a real bug here via its own test: `Testimony`'s default `ordering = ["-created_at"]` was silently folding `created_at` into a `.values_list("author_id").distinct()` call, turning "distinct authors" into "distinct testimonies." **Not yet wired into an actual schedule** -- no Celery beat process/schedule exists anywhere in this codebase yet (Phase 22 only ever used on-demand `.delay()`, never a periodic task), and provisioning one is a deploy-configuration decision (a new Render worker service, same class of decision as Phase 22's Redis/worker Blueprint sync) kept deliberately separate from this task's own logic, which is written, tested, and live-verified. 13 new tests (7 task tests, 3 `notify_creator_digest` tests, plus follow/unfollow cases folded into Slice 1's 21).
- **Slice 3 — Creator sees engagement analytics** — Goal: A Ministry can see how its content is actually performing. ✅ **Completed 2026-08-18.** `get_creator_analytics` (`apps/creators/selectors.py`) is a pure read-side aggregation over `Testimony.view_count` and `TestimonyReaction`, scoped to `APPROVED` testimonies only (a pending/rejected one shouldn't inflate a creator's visible stats) -- no new data collection. `GET /api/v1/creators/me/analytics/` returns follower count, testimony count, total views, and a per-reaction-type breakdown.
- **Slice 4 — Creator responds to a prayer reaction** — Goal: A Ministry can personally respond when someone says they're praying for them. ✅ **Completed 2026-08-18.** `PrayerResponse` is `OneToOneField` to the specific `TestimonyReaction` (not a loose testimony+user pair), so "already responded to this exact reaction" is a real DB-enforced state, not something re-derived in the service layer. `respond_to_prayer_reaction` (`services/commands.py`) rejects, with a distinct exception each: a missing reaction, a reaction on someone else's testimony, a non-`praying_for_you` reaction (`amen`/`gives_me_hope` are passive acknowledgments with no implied expectation of a reply, per this phase's own Background note), an already-responded reaction, and an ordinary (non-Ministry) testimony author trying to respond at all. `GET /api/v1/creators/me/prayer-inbox/` (paginated) + `POST /api/v1/creators/prayer-reactions/<reaction_id>/respond/`; response reaches the original reactor as a real `notify_prayer_response` notification (new `NotificationType.PRAYER_RESPONSE`), not just a dashboard-visible log row.
- **Slice 5 — Verify a creator/ministry** — Goal: Admin can mark a Ministry as verified without touching its content moderation status. ✅ **Completed 2026-08-18.** `verify_creator_profile` sets/clears `is_verified`+`verified_at`+`verified_by` together; confirmed by its own test that it never touches the creator's testimonies' moderation status, keeping identity trust and content trust fully independent as this phase's Background note requires. `GET /api/v1/creators/admin/` (paginated, `?is_verified=` filter) + `POST /api/v1/creators/admin/<user_id>/verify/`, same `SessionAuthentication`/`IsActiveAdmin` pattern as every other admin endpoint in this codebase.

#### Mobile User Flows
- **Slice 6 — Set up a Ministry profile** — Goal: A Premium user can actually create and manage their Ministry profile from the app, not just via the API. ✅ **Completed 2026-08-18** (mobile UI for Slice 1). New `features/creators` Clean Architecture layer (entities/repository/datasource/usecases/controller, mirroring `features/subscriptions`), registered in both Riverpod (`creator_providers.dart`) and GetIt (`service_locator.dart`), matching this codebase's existing double-registration convention. `MyMinistryScreen` (`features/profile`, same placement precedent as `MySubscriptionScreen`) handles all four states: not-Premium upsell (never a dead end, matching Phase 32's own pattern), no-profile-yet setup prompt, the create/edit form, and the saved-profile view. Entry point: a new "My Ministry" tile in Profile's registered-user menu, always visible so a Free user can discover the upsell.
  - **Ministry photo upload, found missing during live testing and built as a same-day follow-up** (not in the original slice scope): `CreatorProfile.avatar_url`, deliberately separate from the personal account photo (`Profile.avatar`) since a Ministry can look different from whoever runs it. New `creator_avatar` `resource_type` in `apps.common.services.media_uploads.create_direct_upload_signature` + `CreatorAvatarUploadSignatureView` (`POST /api/v1/creators/me/avatar-upload-signature/`, Premium-gated), mirroring `ProfileAvatarUploadSignatureView`'s existing signed-direct-to-Cloudinary pattern exactly. Mobile stages the picked/uploaded photo locally (`MyCreatorProfileController.pickAndUploadAvatar`) rather than saving immediately like the personal-photo flow does, since this form has one atomic Save covering name/bio/photo together and the profile row may not exist yet during initial setup; `discardPendingAvatar()` clears a picked-but-unsaved photo on Cancel. 10 new backend tests + 5 new mobile controller tests. Live-verified end to end on a real emulator against the real local backend: picked a real photo from the device gallery, watched it upload and preview correctly, saved the profile, and confirmed the real Cloudinary URL persisted on `CreatorProfile.avatar_url`.
- **Slice 7 — Follow a creator/ministry** — Goal: A user can discover and follow a Ministry right from a testimony they're reading. ✅ **Completed 2026-08-18** (mobile UI for Slice 2). `CreatorPublicProfileScreen` (`features/creators`, family-keyed controller per creator user id, mirrors `TranscriptController`'s own per-testimony family pattern) shows the public profile with a Follow/Following toggle (optimistic, reverts on failure) and a graceful "not a Ministry account" state for the common case of a testimony author who isn't one. Entry point required one small backend addition: `TestimonyDetailSerializer` gained `author_id` (detail-only, list payloads unchanged) so mobile has a real id to link to -- the testimony detail screen's existing author row (written-testimony content only; video testimonies don't render an author row anywhere yet, a pre-existing gap not introduced here) is now tappable, opening the Ministry profile if one exists.
- **Slice 8 — View creator engagement stats** — Goal: A Ministry can check its own engagement analytics from the app itself. ✅ **Completed 2026-08-20** (mobile UI for Slice 3). New `CreatorAnalyticsScreen` (`features/creators`) fetches `GET /creators/me/analytics/` and renders follower/view/testimony-count tiles plus a reaction-type breakdown; reached from the Slice 13 hub's stat row.
- **Slice 9 — Respond to a prayer reaction** — Goal: A Ministry can respond to prayer requests from the app itself. ✅ **Completed 2026-08-20** (mobile UI for Slice 4). New `PrayerInboxScreen` (`features/creators`) lists `praying_for_you` reactions on the creator's own testimonies (paginated, infinite-scroll), with a "Respond" bottom sheet that calls `POST /creators/prayer-reactions/<id>/respond/`; a responded row shows the sent text and a checkmark in place of the button. Live-verified end to end: a real response reached the reactor's notification with the exact text sent.

#### Follow-up Flows (found 2026-08-20, via a product discussion about Ministry identity)
- **Slice 10 — Author display reflects the Ministry name, not the personal name** — Goal: Once someone sets up a Ministry profile, every testimony they've written (past and future) shows the Ministry's name and photo instead of their personal name -- and they're told this will happen before it takes effect, not surprised by it. ✅ **Completed 2026-08-20.** `TestimonyListSerializer.get_author_name`/`get_author_avatar` now prefer `author.creator_profile.display_name`/`avatar_url` when a `CreatorProfile` exists, falling back to the personal `Profile` otherwise; every public-facing queryset that feeds this serializer (browse, favorites, my-testimonies, for-you, home feed, detail) gained `"author__creator_profile"` alongside its existing `select_related`, so this costs zero extra queries. Deliberately left the admin dashboard's own separate `AdminTestimonyListSerializer.get_author_name` untouched -- admins already see `author_email` for identity, and the real personal name is more useful there for moderation accountability than a self-chosen display name. Confirmed retroactive: a testimony created before the Ministry profile existed picks up the Ministry name too, since the lookup is by `author_id` at read time, not a value copied at submission time. The "Set up a Ministry profile" screen (`MyMinistryScreen`, Slice 6) gained a one-time notice shown before the first Create, reflecting the typed name live: "Your name will show as '\<display name\>' on all your testimonies, past and future." 6 new backend tests.
- **Slice 11 — Browse Ministry testimonies** — Goal: A user can discover Ministries through their content directly from the browse feed, instead of only stumbling into one by opening a random testimony first. ✅ **Completed 2026-08-20.** `?ministry_only=true` on `PublicTestimonyListView`, scoped to testimonies whose author has a `CreatorProfile`, reusing `TestimonyListSerializer` unchanged; unverified Ministries are included too, not gated on `is_verified`, per this phase's own Background note. Mobile: the general Search/Browse screen (`SearchScreen`, `showAllTab: true`) gained a fourth "Ministries" tab alongside All/Videos/Text, backed by a new dedicated `ministryTestimoniesProvider` (server-side fetch, since list payloads don't carry a per-row "is this a Ministry" flag to filter on client-side) -- the per-category browse screen (`showAllTab: false`) doesn't get a redundant Ministries tab. Tapping a result behaves exactly like today's testimony detail flow (Slice 7's tappable author row still applies). 3 new backend tests.
- **Slice 12 — Ministry identity is removed when the account is deleted** — Goal: Deleting an account fully removes the Ministry identity attached to it too, so a deleted account's Ministry can't still be found, followed, or shown as verified. ✅ **Completed 2026-08-20.** `delete_own_account` (Phase 14 Slice 4) now hard-deletes the `CreatorProfile` (not scrubbed in place, unlike the personal `Profile` -- a Ministry identity isn't shared content other users depend on the way testimonies/donations are) inside the same `transaction.atomic()` block, plus explicitly deletes `CreatorFollow` rows for that creator (they FK to `User`, not `CreatorProfile`, so `on_delete=CASCADE` wouldn't have caught them). An old testimony's author-row tap then falls back to the same "not a Ministry account" state Slice 7 already handles gracefully. `PrayerResponse` rows already sent are left untouched, same as comments. 1 new test, live-verified against the real local DB: created a Ministry with a follower and a testimony, deleted the account, confirmed the `CreatorProfile`/`CreatorFollow` rows were gone, the testimony's author name fell back to "Deleted User" (never a lingering Ministry name), and the public profile endpoint 404s for the former follower.
- **Slice 13 — My Ministry becomes a hub** — Goal: A Ministry owner opens "My Ministry" and gets a real command centre for their page, not just a status card. ✅ **Completed 2026-08-20** -- found via a live-testing review of Slice 6 against the original mockup, which turned out to never have designed this screen at all (the mockup jumps from the creation form straight to what a *follower* sees; the owner's own return-visit view was built later from prose alone). Redesigned Slice 6's saved-profile view (`_buildProfileState` in `my_ministry_screen.dart`) to mirror the public profile's layout instead of diverging from it: a large centered avatar/name/verified-badge (or verification action, see Slice 14) header and bio, matching `CreatorPublicProfileScreen`'s treatment, rather than the original small left-aligned card. Added a quick-stats row (followers · total views · pending prayer requests, from `get_creator_analytics`) that opens the Analytics screen when tapped -- Slice 8's real entry point. Added a "Prayer Requests" row, highlighted when the pending count is nonzero, opening the prayer inbox -- Slice 9's real entry point. Added a "View public profile" action opening `CreatorPublicProfileScreen` in the owner's own preview (self-follow correctly fails gracefully with a clear error rather than crashing, a known rough edge left as-is). Added a "Recent testimonies" section reusing the existing `moderationProvider`/`ModerationTestimony` data (Phase 3's "My Testimonies"), filtered to approved. The existing "Edit" action is preserved, not replaced.
- **Slice 14 — Ministry requests verification** — Goal: A Ministry owner can explicitly ask to be verified and see where that request stands, instead of verification being an invisible thing only admins can see or start. ✅ **Completed 2026-08-20** -- found via the same review as Slice 13: `is_verified` used to just sit at `false` until an admin happened to notice, with no request signal, no ordered queue, and nothing shown to the owner either way. `CreatorProfile` gained `verification_requested_at` (nullable, set once by the owner via a new action, never auto-set at profile creation -- verification stays opt-in, since many Ministries may never care about the badge and auto-queuing every new profile the way testimony submission does would flood the admin queue with accounts nobody asked to have reviewed). New owner-only `POST /api/v1/creators/me/request-verification/`, idempotent (a repeat call no-ops once already requested or already verified). `GET /api/v1/creators/admin/` (Slice 5's existing admin list) now orders by `verification_requested_at` ascending -- oldest request first, mirroring Phase 4 Slice 1's moderation queue -- so admins see a real queue instead of guessing which unverified profiles actually want review; confirmed by its own test that a requested profile surfaces ahead of a never-requested one. `verify_creator_profile` (Slice 5) is unchanged; no decline/rejection concept was added, since not verifying isn't a moderation decision the way rejecting a testimony is. Mobile: the hub (Slice 13) shows one of three states -- a "Request Verification" button (not yet requested), a "Verification pending" label (requested, awaiting admin), or the existing verified badge. 9 new backend tests. Live-verified end to end: requested verification on a real test account, confirmed `verification_requested_at` persisted via the API, and confirmed the hub correctly showed "Verification pending" on next load.

Test:
- a non-premium user's attempt to create a `CreatorProfile` is rejected with a clear premium-required message, never a generic error
- a lapsed-Premium creator keeps their existing profile, testimonies, and followers fully intact but can't edit the profile or publish new ones until resubscribed
- follower digest notifications batch correctly (one push per follower per day, not one per testimony) and never fire for a creator with zero testimonies approved that day
- a creator's response to a `praying_for_you` reaction reaches the original reactor as a real notification; `amen`/`gives_me_hope` reactions never appear in the response inbox
- verifying or unverifying a creator never changes their testimonies' moderation status or visibility — the two systems stay fully independent
- a user cannot follow themselves; following/unfollowing is idempotent (repeat calls don't error or duplicate rows)

Status: All 14 slices ✅ completed 2026-08-20 -- see each slice's own bullet above. Backend: `apps.creators` (88 tests) + `apps.testimonies` (164 tests, including the Slice 10/11 author-display and ministry-only-filter additions) + `apps.authn` (Slice 12's delete-account addition) all pass; full backend suite is 634 tests, with the same pre-existing 21-failure `apps.authn` registration/OTP baseline (live Brevo API calls) documented since Phase 6, confirmed unrelated by name -- none touch creators, testimonies, or account deletion. Live-verified against the real local Postgres DB end to end for every slice, not just the test suite: profile create/follow/analytics/digest/prayer-response/admin-verify/verification-request, the avatar upload's real Cloudinary round trip, a Ministry's in-app prayer response producing the correct reactor notification, a pre-existing testimony retroactively picking up a Ministry's name and becoming `?ministry_only=true`-discoverable the moment its profile was created, and account deletion actually removing the `CreatorProfile`/`CreatorFollow` rows, falling the testimony's author name back to "Deleted User," and 404ing the public profile for a former follower. Mobile: all of Slices 6-14 live-verified end to end on a real Android emulator against the real local backend. Mobile test suite: 29 new/updated tests across the creators feature pass, `flutter analyze` is clean project-wide; the 2 failures in the full 456-test mobile suite are pre-existing and unrelated (`app_flow_controller_test.dart` session-state test, and a stale `Timer.periodic` teardown flake in `NotificationsController` surfacing through `part6_flow_test.dart`).

Known unrelated gap found during Slice 12's live verification: the local dev DB is missing the `media_exports` app's `0001_initial` migration (a different, parallel-session feature), which surfaced as a `relation "media_exports_brandedvideoexport" does not exist` error when cascade-deleting a `Testimony` with no branded exports -- not run here since it's an unprompted state change to a possibly-shared local DB; worth a `python manage.py migrate media_exports` next time that repo owner is around.

### Phase 24: Referral Program (Attribution + Manual Month-End Payout)

Background: proposed 2026-08-04, same blueprint review as Phase 21 (`BUSINESS_BLUEPRINT.md`'s "Recurring Referral Model" section — 15% recurring commission on a subscriber's payment is the blueprint's own illustrative example, not necessarily the locked rate; see Slice 1 below). Payout approach confirmed with the admin 2026-08-04: **manual, month-end payout for now** — no automated disbursement. Recurring revenue-share payouts to individuals, especially cross-border (e.g. a Nigeria-based referrer earning commission on a US-based subscriber), can implicate tax reporting and money-transmission rules depending on jurisdiction and payout method; automating disbursement is deliberately deferred until that question has a real legal answer. Building the attribution/ledger tracking now is safe regardless of how that question resolves.

Re-scoped 2026-08-21 after a design discussion resolved five open questions the original proposal had left implicit:

- **Commission rate is admin-configurable, not hardcoded**, mirroring Phase 21 Slice 5's pricing pattern — one global percentage (not per-currency, since a percentage already applies uniformly regardless of what currency a subscription is priced in). Each commission ledger row locks in the rate that was actually in effect when it was computed; changing the rate later only affects future cycles, never recalculates past ones — same "never claw back what was already earned" rule this codebase applies everywhere else (Phase 21 cancellation, Phase 21 Slice 5's own pricing changes, Phase 23's verification).
- **Attribution is captured once, at account registration, and never touched again** — deliberately the simplest version, chosen over letting an existing user apply a referral code later at subscribe-time. `ReferralAttribution` is a permanent, immutable record created the moment a new `User` row is created via a referral code; commission is a fully separate, later concern computed per billing cycle purely from "does this subscriber have an attribution, and are both sides currently active." The real tradeoff, accepted deliberately: a user who already had an account before this feature (or who signed up organically) can never be attributed to anyone after the fact — this program only ever touches net-new signups, not existing users converting to Premium.
- **The referral link reuses Phase 11's existing infrastructure rather than building new deep-linking from scratch** — same dashboard domain, same already-verified `assetlinks.json`, same `AppLinkSource` parsing layer, just a second path pattern (`/r/<code>`) alongside Phase 11's `/share/<id>`. The public landing page is deliberately generic (no referrer name, avatar, or any other PII) — Phase 11 Slice 1 already hit exactly this trap once (a public, crawler-indexed page leaking `author.email`) and fixed it by making page content a fixed, non-personalized string; this reuses that lesson instead of re-learning it.
- **A referrer must be an active Premium subscriber for a given cycle's commission to be earned** — not just at the moment they generated their link. No retroactive back-pay if the referrer's own subscription lapses and later resumes; that cycle's commission is simply never generated, same no-clawback principle as above. Referral Program Terms are admin-editable content (reusing Phase 13 Slice 4/7's existing mechanism, not a new content system) that a subscriber must view before "Get my referral link" is reachable, with an explicit, timestamped acceptance recorded — mirroring Phase 14 Slice 2's "explicit acknowledgment before a consequential action" precedent, since this involves real money.
- **Cancellation stops commission immediately but never destroys the underlying attribution.** `ReferralAttribution` rows are permanent once created; commission for any cycle is a pure live check ("active subscription this cycle?"), so a referred subscriber who lapses and resubscribes months later automatically resumes generating commission with zero extra logic needed. Chosen deliberately for the business, not just because it's simpler: the blueprint's own stated philosophy is rewarding long-term customer relationships, and permanently severing a referrer's earning the instant someone misses one payment would punish them for something entirely outside their control, for no real fraud-prevention benefit.

Build:
- new `apps.referrals` app (mirrors `apps.creators`/`apps.subscriptions` each getting their own app rather than living inside an unrelated one)
- `ReferralAttribution` — `OneToOneField` to the referred `User`, `ForeignKey` to the referrer `User`, `created_at`; created exactly once, at registration, never updated or deleted afterward
- `ReferralCode` — `OneToOneField` to `User`, a random 8-character code (uppercase alphanumeric, excluding visually ambiguous characters like `0`/`O`/`1`/`I`/`L`, generated via the same `secrets`-based approach `apps.authn` already uses for admin invite codes), unique, generated lazily the first time an active subscriber opens "Get my referral link"; permanent afterward and keeps working for new signups even through a later lapse (only commission eligibility depends on current subscriber status, not the code's continued existence)
- `ReferralCommissionRate` — versioned like Phase 21 Slice 5's `PremiumPricing`: a new row per admin change, `percent`, `is_active`, `created_by`, `created_at`; commission computation always reads the currently-active row and copies its value onto the ledger row it creates, never a live reference
- `ReferralCommission` — the ledger: referrer, referred user, the Phase 21 renewal charge it was computed from, `amount`, `currency`, `rate_percent` (copied, not referenced), `billing_period`, `is_paid`, `paid_at`, `paid_by`
- commission computation hooks into Phase 21's renewal webhook (`apply_charge_callback`): on each successful renewal charge, if the paying subscriber has a `ReferralAttribution` and their referrer is *currently* an active subscriber, create one `ReferralCommission` row for that cycle at the currently-active rate; no attribution, no active referrer, or a failed/lapsed charge all mean no row is created for that cycle, not a row created and later reversed
- referral link mechanism reuses Phase 11 exactly: new dashboard route `/r/[code]` (same Next.js app, same domain Phase 11's `/share/[testimonyId]` already lives on) rendering a generic branded landing page (store badges, the code shown/copyable, "enter this code when you create your account") with zero referrer-identifying content; Android App Links gain a second `pathPrefix="/r/"` intent-filter entry on the already-verified `assetlinks.json` host (no new domain-verification step needed); mobile's existing deep-link parsing layer (`lib/core/deep_link/`) gains a second link-shape it recognizes, routing to Register with the code pre-filled instead of to a testimony; iOS again has no Universal Links yet, same open blocker as Phase 11 Slice 4 — falls back to the web landing page for now, not started as a gap
- Register (Phase 2 Slice 1) gains one optional "Referral Code" field; on `complete_registration`, a valid, unused-by-this-user code creates the `ReferralAttribution` inside the same transaction as user creation — an invalid/unrecognized code is silently ignored (never blocks registration), and a user can't be attributed to themselves if the code happens to resolve to their own not-yet-created account
- Referral Program Terms: new admin-editable content entry using Phase 13 Slice 4/7's existing content mechanism; mobile renders it with an explicit accept action before the referral-link screen is reachable, and records `referral_terms_accepted_at` on the user
