- **no automated payout in this phase.** The ledger is admin-visible with a mark-as-paid workflow; the actual transfer happens outside the system (bank transfer) and is only tracked here as paid/unpaid

Sub-slices:

#### Backend/Admin Flows
- **Slice 1 — Admin sets the referral commission rate** — Goal: Admin can set and change the referral commission percentage without a code deploy, the same way Premium's own price is admin-configurable. ✅ **Completed 2026-08-21.** New `apps.referrals` app; `ReferralCommissionRate`/`ReferralCommissionRateHistory`, mirroring Phase 21 Slice 5's `PremiumPricing` exactly — one row mutated in place plus a separate history table, not a new row per admin change — a rate change takes effect for commission computed from that point forward only, never recalculating already-generated ledger rows. `GET`/`POST /api/v1/referrals/admin/commission-rate/`, `SessionAuthentication` + `IsActiveAdmin`. No seed migration (unlike `PremiumPricing`, there's no pre-existing settings value to bridge from) — reads default to 0% until an admin sets a real value. 16 tests (`test_services.py`, `test_api.py`). Live-verified against the real local backend: logged in as an admin, confirmed CSRF enforcement, set the rate to 15%, and confirmed it persisted on re-GET and in the DB directly.
- **Slice 2 — Commission computed per billing cycle** — Goal: A referrer earns commission automatically as their referred subscribers keep paying, and it stops the moment either side isn't eligible. ✅ **Completed 2026-08-21.** New `ReferralAttribution` (`OneToOneField` referred user, FK referrer, DB `CheckConstraint` blocking self-referral — Slice 6 is what actually creates these via signup, not built yet, but the model was needed now) and `ReferralCommission` (the ledger: referrer, referred user, FK to the `Subscription`, `provider_transaction_id` unique so a retried webhook delivery can never double-create a row, `amount`/`currency`/`rate_percent` snapshotted, `billing_period_end`, `is_paid`/`paid_at`/`paid_by`). `record_commission_for_successful_charge` hooks into the shared Flutterwave webhook (`apps.donations.api.views.DonationProviderCallbackView`, right after `apply_charge_callback` matches a successful charge) rather than into `apply_charge_callback` itself, reusing the exact same "otherwise-unusual cross-app import" pattern that view already established for `apps.subscriptions` — treats the first charge and every renewal identically (a referred subscriber's first payment is exactly when the referral converts). No commission row when: no attribution, the referrer isn't currently `is_user_premium` (Phase 21's existing entitlement selector — reused, not duplicated), no rate has ever been set (0% is treated as "ledger stays empty," not a 0-amount row), or the charge itself failed. 20 new tests (`test_commission.py`: service-level + a self-referral DB-constraint test + real webhook-integration tests through `donation-provider-callback`); full `apps.donations`/`apps.subscriptions` suites (108 tests) still pass unchanged. Live-verified against the real local backend: seeded a real referrer (active subscription) + referred user + attribution, fired a real signed `charge.completed` webhook request, and confirmed a real `ReferralCommission` row (₦300,000 charge × 15% = 45,000) landed in the DB with the correct referrer/referred user and rate snapshot.
- **Slice 3 — Admin reviews and marks month-end payouts** — Goal: Admin can review and confirm which referral commissions have actually been paid out. ✅ **Completed 2026-08-21.** Backend: `AdminReferralCommissionListView` (`GET /api/v1/referrals/admin/commissions/`, paginated, `?is_paid=`/`?q=` referrer-or-referred-email search/`?from=`/`?to=` billing-period filters, plus a `totals` breakdown by currency mirroring `AdminDonationListView`'s own totals shape) and `mark_referral_commission_paid`/`AdminMarkReferralCommissionPaidView` (`POST .../<id>/mark-paid/`) -- explicit `ReferralCommissionAlreadyPaidError` on a repeat attempt rather than a silent no-op, so a confused double-click surfaces as a real error instead of a second misleading `paid_by` overwrite. 17 new backend tests (`test_admin_commissions.py`); full referrals suite (37 tests) and `apps.donations`/`apps.subscriptions` still pass. Dashboard: new `/referral-payouts` admin page (`ReferralCommissionsPage`), built by directly mirroring this session's own `creators-ministries` feature end to end -- Unpaid/Paid/All tabs (defaulting to Unpaid, the actionable set, same reasoning as Creators & Ministries defaulting to its verification queue), search, per-currency total pills, and a single "Mark as paid" row action (only shown on unpaid rows -- no dropdown of dead options on a paid row) opening a real confirm modal that POSTs through a Next.js route handler with a 303 redirect, same convention as every other admin mutation in this dashboard. Added to the sidebar nav between Subscriptions and Creators & Ministries. 9 new dashboard tests; full dashboard suite (239 tests) and `tsc --noEmit` both clean. Live-verified end to end against the real local backend and a real logged-in dashboard session, including through the actual Next.js form-action route (not just the raw backend API): the real `ReferralCommission` row from Slice 2's live verification appeared correctly on the ledger and its repeat mark-paid attempt correctly failed; a second, freshly seeded live commission was marked paid by POSTing the real `/api/admin/referral-commissions/<id>/mark-paid/` route exactly as a browser form submission would, landed on the real success screen, and was confirmed persisted (`is_paid`/`paid_by`/`paid_at`) directly in the DB. Unpaid/Paid tab filtering and email search both confirmed against the real backend state too.

#### Mobile User Flows
- **Slice 4 — Get my referral link** — Goal: An active subscriber can get their own permanent, shareable referral link/code. ✅ **Completed 2026-08-21.** Backend: `ReferralCode` (`apps.referrals`, `OneToOneField` to `User`, 8-char code from an ambiguity-free alphabet excluding `0/O/1/I/L`, mirroring `apps.authn`'s own `secrets`-based temp-password generator), lazily created only once Slice 5's terms are accepted -- never at registration or subscription time. `GET /api/v1/referrals/me/link/` (`TokenAuthentication`, mobile-only) returns `{terms_accepted, code}`: 403 if not Premium, `code: null` if Premium but terms not yet accepted (not an error -- mobile routes to the terms screen instead), the real code once both gates pass. Mobile: new `features/referrals` Clean Architecture layer (mirrors `features/creators`), registered in both Riverpod and GetIt. `MyReferralLinkScreen` (`features/profile`, same cross-feature-import precedent as `MySubscriptionScreen`/`MyMinistryScreen`) mirrors My Ministry's not-Premium upsell card exactly, plus a terms-not-accepted prompt state and the final code/share state. Share via `showReferralShareMenu` (`features/referrals/presentation/widgets`), reusing Phase 11 Slice 2's `ShareItem` primitive and its WhatsApp-deep-link-plus-native-OS-sheet pattern verbatim. **Note:** the dashboard's own `/r/[code]` public landing page (Phase 24's Build note) was deliberately deferred out of this slice pair's own scope and built as part of Slice 6 instead, once signup attribution actually needed somewhere for a tapped link to land -- see Slice 6's own bullet. 13 new backend tests (`test_referral_link.py`) + 1 new content-endpoint assertion; 5 new mobile controller tests. Full backend referrals+profile_content suites (88 tests) and the full mobile suite (461 tests, same 2 pre-existing unrelated failures as documented in Phase 23's own status note) both pass; `flutter analyze` clean. Live-verified end to end against the real local backend: made a real user Premium, confirmed 403 pre-Premium, confirmed `terms_accepted: false, code: null` pre-acceptance, accepted terms, confirmed a real code was lazily generated and stays stable across repeat calls.
- **Slice 5 — Referral Program Terms** — Goal: A subscriber sees and explicitly accepts what the referral program actually means before they can start using it, since real money is involved. ✅ **Completed 2026-08-21.** Backend: reuses Phase 13's `apps.profile_content` mechanism exactly as planned -- new `referral_program_terms` key on `ProfileContentKey`, seeded via a data migration with real (not placeholder) terms copy that deliberately never hardcodes a specific commission percentage (Slice 1 made that admin-configurable and it can change), served automatically through the same existing `GET /api/v1/profile-content/blocks/` every other content screen already reads -- no new endpoint needed. New `ReferralTermsAcceptance` (`OneToOneField` to `User`, `accepted_at`) rather than a field on the core `User` model -- self-corrected from the original Build note's "records `referral_terms_accepted_at` on the user" wording, the same class of correction as Slice 1's `ReferralCommissionRate` design, to keep this entirely inside `apps.referrals`' own boundary. `POST /api/v1/referrals/me/accept-terms/`, idempotent (a repeat accept returns the original `accepted_at`, never errors or bumps it). Mobile: `ReferralTermsScreen` mirrors `delete_account_screen.dart`'s exact checkbox-gates-a-disabled-button pattern (a full-row `InkWell`, not just the checkbox hit target) rather than a separate confirmation dialog, reading the terms body straight from the existing `profileContentBlocksProvider` cache-then-network controller. Live-verified end to end: fetched the real seeded terms text via the real backend, accepted via the real API, confirmed idempotency, and confirmed `MyReferralLinkScreen`'s state correctly flips from the terms prompt to the real code once accepted.
- **Slice 6 — Signup attribution** — Goal: A new subscriber who registers via a referral link or code is correctly and permanently credited to their referrer, with zero ambiguity about when that link is decided. ✅ **Completed 2026-08-22.** Backend: `attribute_referral_signup(referred_user, code)` (`apps.referrals`) looks up the code, blanks/unknown codes are silently ignored (never blocks registration), self-referral is defensively rejected (can't actually occur in this flow since a brand-new user can't already own a code, but guarded anyway); called from `apps.authn.complete_registration` (a new cross-app import, same already-established pattern as that function's existing `apps.creators` import) inside its own `@transaction.atomic` block, at the exact moment the `User` row is created. `CompleteRegistrationSerializer` gained an optional `referral_code` field. No double-attribution risk by construction -- only one code value can ever be submitted in a single `complete_registration` call, so "whichever link was tapped last" naturally wins. 13 new backend tests (5 direct `attribute_referral_signup` unit tests, 3 `complete_registration` integration tests, 1 API-level test with real Brevo-email-dependent registration tests skipped by this environment's own pre-existing OTP-email limitation -- verified for real instead via Django shell + a real HTTP call against the live local server, bypassing only the email-send step, confirming the full real flow end to end). Mobile: Register's Step 1 gained an optional "Referral Code" field, threaded through `AuthFlowController` → `CompleteRegistration` usecase → `AuthRepository` → `HttpAuthRemoteDataSource` (adds `referral_code` to the POST body only when non-empty) all the way to the real backend call. New `referralCodeFromReferralLink` deep-link parser (mirrors `testimonyIdFromShareLink`) wired into `app_link_handler.dart`; a new named route `AppRouter.authRegister` pushes `AuthFlowScreen(initialReferralCode: ...)`, which force-switches to Create Account mode and pre-fills the code. Android App Links gained a second `pathPrefix="/r/"` intent-filter on the already-verified host (no new domain verification needed). Dashboard: new `/r/[code]` route (`ReferralLandingPage`, mirrors `/share/[testimonyId]`'s structure exactly) is the actual link target Slice 4's shared links point at -- deliberately fully static with no backend lookup at all (not even to check the code is real), so there is nothing for the page to leak and no way to probe valid-vs-invalid codes; it just echoes the code from the URL back inside fixed copy and the same Google Play/App Store badges the testimony share page already uses, reusing the exact `androidStoreUrl`/`iosStoreUrl` constants. Live-verified two different real codes render byte-identical pages apart from the code itself. Found and fixed a real (if minor) regression along the way: the new field made Register's Step 1 taller, pushing the "Log In" bottom link below the fold on short viewports -- not a production bug (the ListView still scrolls), but it broke two widget tests that tapped "Log In" without scrolling first; fixed by having those tests scroll into view first, matching normal Flutter testing practice. 21 new mobile tests (5 deep-link parser, 2 deep-link-handler integration, 1 end-to-end referral-code-threading assertion via a static tracking field on `MockAuthRemoteDataSource`, plus the two existing-test fixes); full mobile suite (466 tests) and `flutter analyze` both clean, same 2 pre-existing unrelated failures as every other slice this phase.

Test:
- commission requires both the referred subscriber's charge to succeed and the referrer to be currently active; either one failing means no row is created for that cycle, and no row is ever created then reversed
- a rate change only affects commission computed after the change; existing ledger rows keep the rate they were actually computed at
- no double-attribution if a user follows two different referral codes before completing registration — whichever one is present at `complete_registration` wins, and the attribution row, once created, is never reassigned
- a user cannot be attributed to themselves
- an existing user (one who registered before this feature, or without a code) can never gain an attribution retroactively
- the month-end ledger total reconciles exactly against the underlying renewal events for that period
- the public `/r/<code>` landing page never renders the referrer's name, email, or any other identifying content
- a referred subscriber who cancels and later resubscribes resumes generating commission automatically, with the same original attribution and no admin action needed

Status: All 6 slices ✅ completed 2026-08-21/2026-08-22 — see each slice's own bullet above. Phase 24 (Referral Program) is fully built end to end: admin sets/reviews commission, an active subscriber gets a real referral link/code after accepting terms, sharing it leads to a real public landing page, and signing up through it creates a permanent attribution that starts earning commission the moment both sides are active paying subscribers.

**Follow-up (2026-08-22), found via a product discussion about discoverability:** Slice 4's "Referral Link" Profile menu tile is real but passive -- a user only finds it by already browsing Profile. Added two low-effort surfaces instead of a bespoke banner: (1) `HomePromoCtaDestination` (Phase 20's "From iTestified" house-promo mechanism, `apps.content`) gained a `REFERRAL_LINK` destination -- the enum's own docstring had already named "invite-a-friend" as an intended use case -- so an admin can create a real "Refer & Earn" promo through the existing dashboard UI (`home-promos-page.tsx` dropdown updated to match) that deep-links straight to `MyReferralLinkScreen` on tap (`promo_card_actions.dart` gained a matching case), reaching every user (Free and Premium alike) who scrolls the Home feed, with zero new banner UI to build. (2) A small "Refer & Earn" teaser card on the mobile Plans/Upgrade screen, between the intro blurb and the pricing cards, tapping through to the same Referral Link screen -- reaches Free users at the exact moment they're already evaluating Premium. 3 new backend tests (`apps.content`), 3 new mobile tests (promo mapping/routing + a Plans-screen tap test); found and fixed the same "pushed content below the fold" widget-test issue as Slice 6 (the new Plans teaser pushed the Free/Premium cards further down a short test viewport) using this file's own pre-existing `skipOffstage:false` convention. Live-verified end to end: created a real `HomePromoCard` with `cta_destination=referral_link` via the real local backend and confirmed it's served correctly through the same public feed endpoint mobile consumes. Full backend (`apps.content`+`apps.referrals`, 133 tests), mobile (468 tests, same 2 pre-existing unrelated failures), and dashboard (239 tests) suites all pass; `flutter analyze` and `tsc --noEmit` both clean.

### Phase 25: Offline Download (Premium)

Background: proposed 2026-08-04, same blueprint review as Phase 21.

Build:
- local caching of video/audio testimonies for offline playback, gated behind Phase 21's entitlement check
- scoped explicitly as convenience caching, not DRM-protected download — redistribution protection is a separate, much larger problem intentionally kept out of scope here
- server-generated branded export copies for videos shared to social media or saved to the device gallery
- every branded export carries iTestified attribution and a call to action to install/open iTestified for more inspiring testimonies
- branded exports retain the existing testimony deep link in the share caption/metadata contract

Sub-slices:

#### Mobile User Flows
- **Slice 1 — Download a testimony for offline playback** — Goal: A Premium user can watch a testimony without an internet connection. ✅ **Completed 2026-08-18** — Premium-gated local video caching, persisted metadata, progress state, local-file playback, and a 500 MB per-device limit.
- **Slice 2 — Manage downloaded content** — Goal: A user can see and control what's taking up space on their device. ✅ **Completed 2026-08-18** — Profile-accessible downloads list, individual removal, remove-all flow, refresh, storage usage indicator, and reopening saved testimonies.
- **Slice 5 — Share or save a branded video export** — Goal: A user can share a testimony as a polished, branded video, not just a link. ✅ **Completed 2026-08-18** — request and poll the branded derivative, share it through the native share sheet, or save it to the device gallery; the existing link-sharing flow remains unchanged and is included in the caption

#### Backend Flows
- **Slice 3 — Generate a branded export derivative** — Goal: The platform can produce a watermarked, on-brand version of a testimony video once, and reuse it. ✅ **Completed 2026-08-18** — for approved public video testimonies, create or reuse a server-side Cloudinary-branded asset with iTestified watermark/logo/end-card overlays; expose an authenticated, idempotent request/status contract for mobile; never replace the original playback asset
- **Slice 4 — Export policy and branding configuration** — Goal: Admin controls how every exported video is branded, and can update it without breaking already-shared exports. ✅ **Completed 2026-08-18** — persist the active logo/watermark/end-card configuration, provide authenticated admin read/update endpoints and export status visibility, version branding changes so new derivatives are regenerated without mutating old ones, and keep the public deep-link URL stable

#### Dashboard/Admin User Flows
- **Slice 6 — Manage export branding** — Goal: Admin can manage export branding visually from the dashboard, not just via the API. ✅ **Completed 2026-08-18** — dashboard surface for branding assets, CTA copy, enable/disable state, export-generation status/error visibility, branding preview, and confirmation before changes affect future externally shared media

Test:
- an explicit product decision, then a matching test, for whether downloaded content stays playable after a subscription lapses
- branded exports are generated from the approved testimony asset, never from an unmoderated/pending asset
- repeated export requests reuse an existing matching derivative rather than creating duplicate media jobs
- an export includes the iTestified attribution/end card and the stable testimony deep link is available to the mobile share contract
- admin branding changes affect newly generated exports without mutating existing offline files or the original testimony video

Product decision (2026-08-18): downloads are convenience caching, not DRM. Premium is required when creating a download, but an already-downloaded file remains playable after the subscription lapses because offline playback cannot reliably re-check entitlement and the product explicitly excludes redistribution protection. Users can remove individual downloads or clear the full cache. The mobile implementation uses a 500 MB per-device cache limit and persists only local metadata plus files; no media bytes pass through the backend. Social/gallery sharing is a separate branded-export path: the original offline file stays private, while any exported file receives server-side iTestified attribution and a CTA.

Status: In progress — Mobile Slices 1-2 and 5, Backend Slices 3-4, and Dashboard Slice 6 implemented 2026-08-18. Video is wired first; the export contract remains media-type agnostic so Phase 28 audio can reuse it.

**Follow-up (2026-08-22), found via a UI review of Slice 1/5's action row:** "Download for offline playback" (Premium) and "Share or save branded video" (free for everyone) rendered as two identical outlined buttons stacked back to back — nothing distinguished the gated one until a free user tapped it and was silently redirected to the Plans screen mid-viewing. Reviewed against two mocked designs (a button-signal redesign, then a separate backend signed-URL hardening design that was discussed but deliberately deferred — see the design's own note that the current client-only gate is an acceptable, intentional tradeoff given the existing "convenience caching, not DRM" decision, not a bug) before building. Shipped: Share promoted to a filled primary button (`AppColors.purple60`) since it's the one action every viewer can complete and the one that brings new people into the app; Download demoted to a muted outlined button with a new reusable `PremiumBadge` widget (`testimony_detail_primitives.dart`, reusing the exact gold/dark-brown pairing already established for the Home header's Premium corner badge) so the gate is visible before the tap. Tapping Download as a non-Premium viewer now opens `showPremiumDownloadUpsellSheet` (`testimony_detail_meta_sheets.dart`) — an in-place bottom sheet explaining the perk with "Upgrade to Premium"/"Not now" actions — instead of silently pushing `PlansScreen`; "Not now" leaves the user exactly where they were. No gating logic changed: `is_user_premium`-equivalent client check timing and behavior are identical, only the presentation changed. 5 new tests in `testimony_detail_verification_test.dart` (badge shown/hidden by entitlement, upsell shown on tap, Upgrade opens Plans, Not now dismisses in place) — found and fixed two real test-environment gaps while writing them: (1) the default test session in this file is a guest, and Download's guest-check runs before its entitlement check, so exercising the entitlement path needed a `_RegisteredAppFlowController` override (mirroring `part6_flow_test.dart`'s own established pattern) rather than the file's existing default; (2) switching to a registered session then triggered `TestimonyDetailScreen.initState`'s subscription-refresh call, which needs an `ApiClient` this file deliberately never registers — fixed by overriding `mySubscriptionProvider` with a `hasLoaded: true` fake so the screen's own "already know their status" guard skips the real fetch. Full mobile suite (473 tests, same 2 pre-existing unrelated failures) and `flutter analyze` both clean.

**Follow-up (2026-08-22), same review, a second product question:** confirmed guests can reach neither action — both `_handleDownload` and `_handleBrandedExport` in `testimony_detail_screen.dart` run their pre-existing `UserAccess.guest` check first and short-circuit to `GuestRestrictions.showFavoriteRestriction` before either the entitlement check or the export dialog can run; unchanged by this follow-up, confirmed by inspection. Separately, the branded-export feature's copy ("Share or save branded video") implied a local-save capability it never should have had for a social-distribution feature — product decision: branded export is share-only, permanently, not a second local-save path (that's Download's own job, and it's the Premium-gated one). Removed `saveToGallery()` from both `BrandedVideoExportController` and `BrandedVideoFileService` (the `gal` package import dropped from the latter but kept in `pubspec.yaml`, since `inspirational_pictures` still uses it elsewhere), relabeled the button to "Share branded video", and dropped the ready-state dialog's second action so it now offers only "Share". Caught a real build-cache bug while live-verifying on-device: `flutter build apk --debug` silently kept a stale compiled `kernel_blob.bin` across two rebuilds despite the edited source being on disk (confirmed by unzipping the APK and grepping the kernel blob directly) — `flutter clean` before rebuilding was the fix; worth remembering if a future on-device check ever shows stale UI text again despite a correct source diff. Live-verified against a real local backend (Django + Celery + Redis, not Render) for the first time this phase — the mobile app's `API_BASE_URL` default was still pointed at the deployed Render backend, rebuilt with `--dart-define=API_BASE_URL=http://10.0.2.2:8000/api/v1` for the emulator to reach the host's local server, with `DJANGO_ALLOWED_HOSTS` widened to accept that address. `flutter analyze` and the full mobile suite stayed clean through this change (no test count shift, since it only touched already-covered branded-export code).

### Phase 26: Multi-Currency & Multi-Region Expansion

Background: proposed 2026-08-04, same blueprint review as Phase 21.

Build:
- extends Flutterwave's existing NGN/USD support (Phase 5 precedent) to additional currencies
- localized pricing display at checkout, not just currency conversion
- evaluate whether UK/US coverage needs a second payment processor alongside Flutterwave, whose strongest coverage is Africa

Status: not started — sequenced after Phase 21 since it expands an already-working subscription system rather than preceding it.

### Phase 27: Live Testimony Broadcasts

Background: proposed 2026-08-04, same blueprint review as Phase 21.

Build:
- real-time video infrastructure (Mux Live / Agora / LiveKit-class vendor) — a genuinely separate technical domain from the existing Cloudinary pre-recorded upload pipeline, not an extension of it
- scheduling, live viewer count, automatic archival into the normal testimony catalog after the broadcast ends

Sub-slices:

#### Mobile User Flows
- **Slice 1 — Creator schedules and starts a live broadcast** -- Goal: A Ministry can go live to its audience.
- **Slice 2 — Viewer watches a live broadcast** -- Goal: A user can watch a Ministry's broadcast as it happens.
- **Slice 3 — Broadcast is archived as a normal video testimony afterward** -- Goal: A finished live broadcast doesn't disappear -- it becomes a normal testimony people can still watch.

Test:
- a dropped stream mid-broadcast is handled gracefully (real-time infra has different failure modes than anything else in this app) and viewers see a clear "broadcast ended" state rather than a stuck player

Status: not started — recommend building this last of all seven new phases. Different technical domain, materially higher infra/operational cost (live streams need active health monitoring), and the blueprint itself treats it as one bullet among many rather than a headline feature.

### Phase 28: Audio Testimonies

Background: found during the 2026-08-16 `BUSINESS_BLUEPRINT.md` alignment review — the blueprint lists "Listen to public audio testimonies" under **Free Plan** (Core Product, not a perk) and "Upload unlimited audio testimonies" under **Premium Plan**, but `TestimonyType` has only ever supported `written`/`video` (`apps/testimonies/models.py:70-72`, Phase 3). Audio was never built, despite the blueprint treating it as a first-class content type alongside video from the start. This phase closes that gap; it's foundational (content model, moderation, browse/search all need to know about it) rather than a small add-on.

Correction (2026-08-17): an earlier draft of this note incorrectly assumed video testimony submission was already open to free users and therefore out of scope to gate. That was wrong — confirmed by direct inspection of `apps/testimonies/api/urls.py` and `apps/testimonies/api/views.py` that there is currently no user-facing video upload path at all. Every video-creation endpoint (`admin/testimonies/upload-video/`, `upload-signature/`, `create-video-from-url/`, `<id>/upload-now/`) uses `IsActiveAdmin`; the only non-admin submission endpoint is `submit/written/` (`AuthenticatedWrittenTestimonyCreateView`, `IsAuthenticated`, written-only). All video testimonies today are admin-curated, not self-submitted. See Phase 32, which adds the missing self-service video upload path, premium-gated from day one — exactly like audio, with no existing free-tier behavior to take away either.

Build:
- extend `TestimonyType` with `AUDIO`; `Testimony` gains `audio_url` (mirrors the existing `video_url`/`thumbnail_url` pattern) and a duration field
- upload flow reuses the existing Cloudinary direct-upload-signature pattern (`create_direct_upload_signature`), a new `resource_type="audio"` branch alongside the existing `video`/`image`/`avatar`/`inspirational_picture`/`home_promo_card` ones
- submitting/uploading an audio testimony is premium-gated (Phase 21 entitlement check); listening to a published audio testimony is free for everyone, including guests — matches how video watching is already free
- moderation: audio testimonies go through the exact same Phase 4 moderation workflow as video and written — no new moderation states or logic
- mobile: new audio player UI (distinct from the video player — waveform/progress bar, play/pause), matching the existing testimony detail screen's card patterns
- upload policy is admin-configurable through the dashboard: accepted formats, maximum file size, and maximum duration
- default upload policy is AAC/M4A and MP3, 50 MB maximum file size, and 15 minutes maximum duration
- audio playback supports background listening after the user locks the phone or leaves the app

Sub-slices:

#### Backend Flows
- **Slice 1 — Audio as a real testimony type** — Goal: Audio testimonies work everywhere a video testimony already does. `TestimonyType.AUDIO`, `audio_url`/`duration` fields, migration; audio testimonies flow through existing moderation/browse/search/favorites/reactions unchanged
- **Slice 2 — Uploading audio requires Premium** — Goal: Only Premium users can submit audio testimonies. the audio upload/submission endpoint checks Phase 21's entitlement and rejects a non-premium attempt with a clear, upgrade-prompting message (never a generic 403)

#### Mobile User Flows
- **Slice 3 — Non-premium user is prompted to upgrade** — Goal: A free user trying to submit audio sees a clear upgrade prompt, not a dead end. attempting to submit an audio testimony as a free user shows an upsell, not a dead end
- **Slice 4 — Premium user records/uploads an audio testimony** — Goal: A Premium user can actually submit an audio testimony. same submission flow shape as video (Phase 3), audio-specific fields only
- **Slice 5 — Anyone plays a published audio testimony** — Goal: Anyone, including guests, can listen to a published audio testimony. guest and free users included, matching free-plan listening

#### Dashboard/Admin User Flows
- **Slice 6 — Configure audio upload policy** — Goal: Admin controls the limits applied to future audio uploads without a mobile release. Admin can view and update accepted formats, maximum file size, and maximum duration; the backend owns and enforces the active values. Changes require confirmation and do not alter existing testimonies.

Test:
- a non-premium upload attempt is rejected with a clear premium-required message, not a generic error
- audio testimonies appear correctly in browse/search/moderation alongside written/video, with no special-casing needed in those existing queries beyond the new type value
- a non-premium (or guest) user can still fully listen to a published audio testimony submitted by someone else
- an admin can change the audio upload policy, and the new limits are enforced by the backend and reflected in the mobile submission UI
- an approved audio testimony continues playing when the phone is locked or the app is backgrounded

Product decisions (2026-08-23): audio is a third first-class testimony type; `audio_url` and `duration_ms` are stored on `Testimony`; Premium is required for both the upload-signature request and final submission; guests and free users can play approved audio; Premium users can record or import audio; default limits are 15 minutes and 50 MB with AAC/M4A and MP3 accepted; admins can change those limits; audio playback supports background listening. Backend, mobile, and dashboard/admin work remain separate slices.

Status: not started

### Phase 29: Playlists & Testimony Collections

Background: blueprint Premium Plan bullet ("Create playlists and testimony collections") with no corresponding phase anywhere in this plan before the 2026-08-16 alignment review.

Build:
- new `Playlist` model (owner, title, visibility `public`/`private`) + an ordered through-model associating testimonies with a playlist and a position
- creating/editing/reordering a playlist is premium-gated; viewing a *public* playlist someone else made, and playing through it, is free for everyone — same creation-gated/consumption-free split as Phase 28's audio upload vs. listen

Sub-slices:

#### Mobile User Flows
- **Slice 1 — Premium user creates a playlist** — Goal: A Premium user can organize testimonies into their own collection. title, add/remove/reorder testimonies
- **Slice 2 — Premium user sets a playlist public or private** -- Goal: A user controls who else can see a given playlist.
- **Slice 3 — Anyone views a public playlist and plays through it** — Goal: Anyone can watch through a public playlist, even without an account. guest and free users included
- **Slice 4 — Premium user deletes a playlist** — Goal: A user can remove a playlist they made without affecting the testimonies in it. deleting a playlist never deletes the underlying testimonies, only the association

Test:
- reordering persists correctly and survives a reload
- a private playlist is invisible to other users, including via a direct link/id guess, not just hidden from a listing
- deleting a playlist leaves every testimony in it fully intact and unaffected

Status: not started — no dependency beyond Phase 21's entitlement check on creation; can run in parallel with Phase 22/28.

### Phase 30: Exclusive Testimonies & Interviews (Premium Content Visibility)

Background: blueprint Premium Plan bullet ("Access to exclusive testimonies and interviews") with no corresponding phase before the 2026-08-16 alignment review. Today every published testimony is visible to everyone, guests included — there is no content-visibility gating concept anywhere in the app yet, which makes this a genuinely new axis, not an extension of an existing one.

Open question to resolve before Slice 1 starts, not assumed here: **who can mark a testimony exclusive** — admin-only (curated "interviews," closer to the blueprint's own wording), or any verified creator/ministry (Phase 23)? This changes the permission model of Slice 1 and should be confirmed with the admin at build time.

Build:
- `Testimony` gains an `is_premium_exclusive` flag
- an exclusive testimony is excluded from guest/free-user browse, search, and full detail view — shown instead as a locked preview with an upgrade prompt; a premium subscriber sees it exactly like any other testimony (an optional badge is the only visible difference)
- entitlement is re-checked live, not cached at first view, matching Phase 21's existing "never trust client-reported status" principle — a lapsed subscriber loses access to previously-viewable exclusive content immediately, not at next login

Sub-slices:

#### Admin/Creator Flows
- **Slice 1 — Mark a testimony exclusive** — Goal: Certain testimonies can be reserved as Premium-only content. scope (admin-only vs. verified creators too) confirmed with the admin before this starts, per the open question above

#### Mobile User Flows
- **Slice 2 — Free/guest user sees a locked preview** — Goal: A non-Premium user can see that exclusive content exists, and is invited to unlock it. title/thumbnail visible, full content and playback gated behind an upgrade prompt
- **Slice 3 — Premium user sees exclusive testimonies normally** — Goal: A Premium user watches exclusive content with zero extra friction. no friction, optional badge only

Test:
- an exclusive testimony never appears in playable/readable form in a guest's or free user's browse, search, or detail response — this must be enforced server-side, not just hidden client-side
- a subscription lapsing mid-session immediately revokes access to previously-visible exclusive content on the next request, matching Phase 21's entitlement re-check pattern

Status: not started — depends on Phase 21 (entitlement check) and touches Phase 23 (creator profiles) for the who-can-mark-exclusive question; that ownership decision blocks Slice 1.

### Phase 31: Priority Moderation & Publishing (Premium)

Background: blueprint Premium Plan bullet ("Priority moderation and publishing") with no corresponding phase before the 2026-08-16 alignment review. Phase 4's moderation queue (completed) is a plain FIFO admin queue today with no ordering or priority concept.

Build:
- moderation queue ordering considers the submitter's premium status at the time of listing — premium submissions surface first, non-premium submissions keep their existing recency ordering within their own tier
- fairness-safe by construction: this re-orders the queue, it never hides, skips, or silently drops a non-premium submission — every pending testimony is still reachable and reviewed, just not first
- dashboard: a visible "Priority" indicator on premium-submitted testimonies in the moderation queue, so an admin understands why ordering isn't pure chronological

Sub-slices:

#### Admin Flows
- **Slice 1 — Premium submissions surface first in the moderation queue** — Goal: Premium submitters get their testimonies reviewed sooner. secondary sort remains existing `created_at` order within each tier
- **Slice 2 — Dashboard shows a priority badge on premium submissions** -- Goal: Admin can tell at a glance which queue items are Premium-priority.

Test:
- a premium submission surfaces ahead of an older non-premium submission in the queue
- re-ordering never causes a non-premium submission to be skipped, hidden, or lost — only deprioritized; a full queue scroll/page-through still reaches every pending item

Status: not started — small, low-risk addition to the already-completed Phase 4; no dependency on Phase 22/28-30, can be built independently whenever convenient.

### Phase 32: Self-Service Video Testimony Upload (Premium)

Background: raised by the admin 2026-08-17: "currently, no video upload for users at all, its only by admins, please confirm. We need a phase for it. Everyone can watch videos but only premium users can upload and download for offline access." Confirmed by inspection: every video-creation endpoint in `apps/testimonies/api/urls.py` lives under `admin/testimonies/` with `Admin*`-prefixed views, all gated `IsActiveAdmin` (`apps/testimonies/api/views.py`). The only non-admin testimony-submission endpoint is `submit/written/`. There is no self-service video upload path today — every video testimony currently in the app was uploaded by an admin, not submitted by a user. This corrects an earlier, wrong assumption recorded in Phase 28's Background (see the 2026-08-17 correction there).

Lighter-weight than Phase 28's audio work: the `video` content type, `Testimony.video_url`/`thumbnail_url` fields, and the Cloudinary signed-upload flow (`create_direct_upload_signature(resource_type="video")`) all already exist and are proven in daily admin use. This phase is purely a new premium-gated *access path* onto existing infrastructure — no new `TestimonyType`, no new model fields, no new moderation states.

Watching a published video testimony is already free for everyone, guests included (Phase 3's existing browse/detail views) — untouched by this phase. Downloading a video for offline playback is separately premium-gated by the already-planned Phase 25 (Offline Download); this phase and Phase 25 are companion features for video specifically (upload it premium, take it offline premium) and need no changes to Phase 25 itself.

Build:
- new user-facing (non-admin) video submission endpoint, parallel to `submit/written/` but for video: signed direct-to-Cloudinary upload via the existing `create_direct_upload_signature(resource_type="video")` service, then a create-from-URL call to record the testimony — same two-step shape as the admin flow, just re-gated
- entitlement check reuses Phase 21's premium check instead of `IsActiveAdmin`; a non-premium attempt gets a clear upgrade-prompting rejection, never a generic 403
- submitted video testimonies enter the exact same Phase 4 moderation queue as any other testimony — no new moderation logic
- mobile: a user-facing video recording/upload screen (the free-user-visible counterpart to the existing admin-only upload tooling), plus an upsell prompt when a non-premium user attempts it
- **security hardening, required before this signature endpoint is opened beyond admins**: today `create_direct_upload_signature` only signs `folder`+`timestamp` (`apps/common/services/media_uploads.py:97-100`) — no file-size/duration cap or format allowlist is enforced, and `resource_type` itself isn't cryptographically bound by the signature (it's just whichever Cloudinary endpoint the client happens to call). That's tolerable while only admins can obtain a video signature, but Phase 32 hands the same capability to every premium subscriber — a much larger, less-trusted population — so this phase must add, as part of Slice 1, not after: an explicit max file size/duration signed into the upload params (Cloudinary supports signing `max_file_size`/eager transformation constraints), and a validated `allowed_formats` restriction so the signature can't be replayed against an unintended resource type

Sub-slices:

#### Backend Flows
- **Slice 1 — User-facing video upload requires Premium** — Goal: A Premium user can submit their own video testimony, safely bounded in size/format. new non-admin submission endpoint reusing the existing signed-upload infrastructure, entitlement-checked via Phase 21; signature is issued with an enforced max file size/duration and an `allowed_formats` allowlist (see security hardening note above) — this is not deferred to a follow-up

#### Mobile User Flows
- **Slice 2 — Non-premium user is prompted to upgrade** — Goal: A free user trying to submit video sees a clear upgrade prompt, not a dead end. attempting to submit a video testimony as a free user shows an upsell, not a dead end
- **Slice 3 — Premium user records/uploads a video testimony themselves** — Goal: A Premium user can self-publish a video testimony the same way an admin already can. same submission shape as the existing admin upload screen, user-facing and entitlement-gated
- **Slice 4 — Anyone watches a published video testimony** — Goal: Anyone, including guests, can watch a published video testimony. guest and free users included; this already works today and this phase must not regress it

Test:
- a non-premium user's video-submission attempt is rejected with a clear premium-required message, never a generic error
- a premium user's submitted video testimony enters moderation exactly like any other testimony and, once approved, is fully watchable by every user including guests — upload is gated, watching never is
- offline download of that same video remains governed solely by Phase 25's own entitlement check, unaffected by this phase
- an upload attempt exceeding the signed max file size/duration, or using a format outside the allowlist, is rejected by Cloudinary rather than silently accepted

Status: not started — parallel to Phase 28 (same pattern, different media type, no code dependency beyond both reusing Phase 21's entitlement check); companion to Phase 25 for the "download offline" half of the video premium story.

## Risks To Watch Early

- building schema directly from UI mocks instead of real domain needs
- mixing mobile-user auth and dashboard-admin auth carelessly
- creating separate models for every screen instead of stable domain entities
- unclear moderation state definitions leading to migration churn
- under-specifying audit requirements for admin actions
- postponing permissions design until too late
- storing derived analytics as source-of-truth data without clear rules

## Default Delivery Pattern

For each feature area:

1. define the domain language
2. model the data and constraints
3. add service commands and query selectors
4. expose API endpoints
5. add backend tests
6. wire the relevant UI client
7. verify the integrated flow end-to-end
8. review migration and operational impact

Do not start from serializers or views.
